# Decode Scaling Mission

Feature IDs: `INFRA-DECODE-PARALLEL-STAGES`, `INFRA-DECODE-FRAME-PIPELINING`

The live implementation checklist is [`DECODE-SCALING-OPEN-TASKS.md`](DECODE-SCALING-OPEN-TASKS.md). Add discoveries there immediately; keep measurements and completed or rejected evidence here.

## Goal

On `/Users/bartosztomczyk/Documents/SplotLabs/test.ivf`, decode 30 frames at 1, 2, 4, 8, and 10 threads faster than dav2d, with the 10-thread splot wall time at most 50% of dav2d's and no 1-thread regression.

Retention policy (maintainer, 2026-07-31): individual candidates are no longer gated at 10%. A candidate is retained when its direction is consistently positive across alternating pairs, 1 thread does not regress, 2/4 threads do not materially regress, output is byte-exact, and complexity is proportionate. Retained wins stack; each survivor is re-measured independently and in combination after rebases and dropped only on neutral or negative confirmation. Architectural barrier removals and scalar kernel wins are both in scope, including safe `std::simd` vectorization; `unsafe_code = forbid` stands, and third-party decoders remain engineering inspiration only.

## Benchmark contract

Splot decoder-only timing:

```sh
./target/release/splot decode \
  --quiet --output-format null --limit=30 \
  /Users/bartosztomczyk/Documents/SplotLabs/test.ivf \
  --threads=10 --frame-delay=auto
```

Dav2d timing:

```sh
/Users/bartosztomczyk/Devel/dav2d/build/tools/dav2d \
  --demuxer ivf \
  -i /Users/bartosztomczyk/Documents/SplotLabs/test.ivf \
  -o /dev/null --threads 10 -l 30 --framedelay 10
```

The splot arm runs `--frame-delay=auto`, which is `max(threads, 3)` at two or more workers and 1 at one worker (SCALE-039), so a matrix run at `T` threads uses depth `max(T, 3)`.
Dav2d keeps `--framedelay T`: SCALE-038 measured it flat to `--framedelay` above its thread
count (2T wall 0.5371/0.5384/0.5383 s at framedelay 2/3/4, occupancy 1.978 throughout), so
matching the deeper splot depth would not move it and the comparison stays fair either way.

Use rebuilt release binaries, warmups, alternating paired samples, and an idle machine. Prove decoded output
separately from timing. A deliverable candidate must pass exact output comparison, the required corpus/oracle
checks, `cargo xtask check-duplication`, `cargo xtask ci`, and the 1/2/4/8/10 paired timing matrix.

## Confirmed matrix 2026-08-01 (post-#1177)

Official ground truth for exact `origin/main` `22e21b007`, the first matrix on SCALE-066's retained fat-LTO profile, superseding the post-#1175 baseline (its ratios are the "post-#1175" column) and every earlier one. The
timed binary hashes `73d436e05b179f64...`, the artifact SCALE-066 measured; a whole-workspace `cargo build --release` of this tree behind a clean `cargo fmt --all -- --check` found nothing to rebuild and reproduced that
hash. Host Apple M2 Max (8 performance plus 4 efficiency cores), up 3 days 13 hours, one-minute load 2.64 before the first timed sweep and 2.65 after the last. Method per width: 3 warmups per arm, then 11 alternating
splot/dav2d pairs with the slot order rotated each pair, `SPLOT_DECODE_DISCARD_HASH=1`, `--limit=30`, wall from `perf_counter` and CPU from `RUSAGE_CHILDREN`, splot on `--frame-delay=auto` and dav2d on `--framedelay
max(T, 3)`. A throwaway warm sweep ran first. **Two sweeps at 1/2/8/10 workers and five at 3 and 4**; every column pools all sweeps at that width and each interval bootstraps the paired median 20,000 times.

| T | splot wall | dav2d wall | ratio | post-#1175 | paired median | 95% CI | splot faster | splot CPU | dav2d CPU | splot cores | dav2d cores | verdict |

| 2 | 0.5206 s | 0.5387 s | **0.9665x** | 0.9784x | -3.38% | [-3.52, -3.00] | **22/22** | 1.017 s | 1.065 s | 1.95 | 1.98 | **WIN** |
| 3 | 0.3705 s | 0.3753 s | **0.9871x** | 1.0017x | -1.32% | [-1.41, -1.10] | **52/55** | 1.042 s | 1.102 s | 2.81 | 2.94 | **WIN** |
| 4 | 0.2855 s | 0.2876 s | **0.9927x** | 1.0035x | -0.74% | [-0.89, -0.57] | **45/55** | 1.063 s | 1.114 s | 3.72 | 3.87 | **WIN** |
| 8 | 0.1757 s | 0.1604 s | 1.0953x | 1.1126x | +9.54% | [+8.98, +9.83] | 0/22 | 1.153 s | 1.157 s | 6.56 | 7.21 | LOSS |
| 10 | 0.1705 s | 0.1558 s | 1.0939x | 1.0955x | +9.02% | [+8.69, +10.43] | 0/22 | 1.288 s | 1.331 s | 7.55 | 8.54 | LOSS |

**The goal's width set now stands at 1, 2, 3 and 4 won.** SCALE-066's 22-pair
preview reproduced at 55: three and four workers flip the post-#1175 losses by
1.5 and 1.1 points and all five sweeps at each width read below parity (3T
0.9881 / 0.9863 / 0.9873 / 0.9888 / 0.9890, 4T 0.9950 / 0.9912 / 0.9890 /
0.9945 / 0.9904). Neither interval comes within a factor of four of SCALE-065's
+-0.15% tie band, so its 220-pair rule is not in play and both calls are decided
on sign too. Eight and ten workers stay lost at 1.0953x and 1.0939x.

**Every remaining loss is occupancy, and every cause is closed.** Splitting each
benchmark into `T x wall - CPU`, **splot's total CPU is now under dav2d's at all
six widths** — by 59.7 core-ms at three workers, 51.2 at four, 43.5 at ten and
4.2 at eight, where the post-#1175 matrix had it 6.0 above — while its excess
idle is 45.2 / 42.8 / 126.5 / 189.9 core-ms, 15.8 and 19.0 per worker at eight
and ten, 103% and 130% of the whole 15.28 and 14.64 ms wall gaps there.
**SCALE-068 re-measured the whole loss decomposition on this profile and every
closure held**, so the named terms are unchanged and each keeps its row: the
first-pyramid fill, whose AV2 § 8.2 CDF chain SCALE-054 closed and whose one
successor SCALE-055 priced at -2.04% against a 4%-at-both-widths go line, now
re-priced at -2.19% on a ceiling that grew from 5.05% to 5.41% of the ten-worker
benchmark; the fixed `--limit=1` term, which is dav2d's `--framedelay max(T, 3)`
cost and not splot's startup (SCALE-056) and is now a credit at both lost
widths; and the steady CPU residual, down from +5.467 to +4.611 core-ms per
frame and now **100%** parallel inflation with every sub-term under the
1%-of-wall bar. **The one thing that moved is the mix: the fill is now 79% of
the ten-worker gap and 62% of the eight-worker one**, because LTO compressed
every region except the latency-bound serial chain.

Marginal steady frame (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60` over 60 frames), which
the 30-frame benchmark understates because its prologue is where splot is
relatively cheapest (SCALE-032). SCALE-068 re-measured 1T and 10T on the
shipped artifact, 7 alternating reps at ten workers and 5 at one; the 8T row is
SCALE-053's, pre-LTO, and is the only stale one:

| T | splot CPU/frame | splot wall/frame | splot cores | dav2d CPU/frame | dav2d wall/frame | dav2d cores | CPU ratio | wall ratio |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 21.097 ms | 21.097 ms | 1.000 | 21.351 ms | 21.352 ms | 1.000 | **0.988x** | 0.988x |
| 8 (pre-LTO) | 27.642 ms | 3.625 ms | 7.625 | 23.052 ms | 2.990 ms | 7.710 | 1.199x | 1.212x |
| 10 | 30.157 ms | 3.405 ms | **8.857** | 25.546 ms | 2.851 ms | **8.959** | 1.181x | 1.194x |

Steady occupancy is at parity, so the whole steady gap is work: +4.611 core-ms
per frame at ten workers, down from +5.467, against **-0.254 at one**, where
pre-LTO one worker still cost +0.422. **The steady gap is now 100% parallel
inflation** — from 1 to 10 workers splot pays +9.060 core-ms per frame against
dav2d's +4.195, and splot's one-worker steady frame is 1.2% *cheaper* than
dav2d's.

### Steady lifecycle and the fill chain (SCALE-068, post-LTO)

Re-traced with an env-gated task timeline — every pool task's worker interval
plus each admission job's `order_key`, which names its frame and its phase — on
exact `origin/main` `96e1dbe50`, ten threads. The instrumented binary is
byte-identical to the shipped artifact at 1 and 10 workers and wall-neutral
(-0.182% at ten and -0.128% at eight over nine alternating pairs each). Steady
window is coded frames 30-60, three reps at `--limit=90`. The dav2d column is
carried from SCALE-043, because that decoder and its build are untouched.

| Term | pre-045 | post-045 (SCALE-047) | **post-LTO (SCALE-068)** | dav2d |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Frame period `P` | 5.18 ms | 4.14 / 3.94 / 4.07 ms | **3.94 / 4.02 / 4.02** | 3.38 ms |
| Work per frame `W` | 37.10 core-ms | 36.52 / 36.74 / 35.74 | 37.33 / 37.60 / 37.29 | 29.87 |
| Busy cores | 7.16-7.40 | 9.21 / 9.42 / 9.21 | **9.47 / 9.34 / 9.27** | 8.83-9.02 |
| Idle cores | 2.51-2.64 | 0.75 / 0.58 / 0.79 | 0.53 / 0.66 / 0.73 | 0.93-1.01 |
| Above the ten-worker floor | 28-30% | 8.1 / 2.9 / 8.5% | **5.6 / 7.0 / 7.8%** | 12-15% |
| Ordered chain work `C` | 5.85 core-ms | 6.67 / 6.46 / 6.82 | **6.02 / 6.14 / 5.52** | 7.13 |
| Chains at once `k = C / P` | 1.13 | 1.61 / 1.64 / 1.68 | **1.53 / 1.53 / 1.37** | 2.11 |

**`C` fell about 10% under LTO and `P` did not follow, so `k` fell back below
SCALE-043's 1.58 go line** — the arithmetic still closes, `P_s / P_d = (C_s /
C_d) x (k_d / k_s) = 0.828 x 1.426 = 1.180` against a traced period ratio of
1.183. Splot's ordered chain is now **17% cheaper than dav2d's** and its period
is still 18% longer, purely because it runs 1.48 chains at once against 2.11.
That is not a lever: steady runs **5.6-7.8% above its own ten-worker work
floor** against dav2d's 12-15%, so there is no scheduling headroom left to
convert and the steady period is set by `W`. Removing steady wall now means
removing steady work, and every named sub-term is under the 1%-of-wall bar.

**The fill's § 8.2 CDF chain is unchanged and LTO barely touched it.** Over
eleven alternating pairs at ten workers the six parses of coded frames 2-7 span
**16.690 ms and sum to 16.688** — 100.0% back-to-back, zero slack, tighter than
the 0.01-0.05 ms starts SCALE-054 measured — and each successor starts within
0.001 ms of its predecessor's end. Against a same-source build with the profile
reverted to plain release, the chain is 16.690 against 17.438 ms (**-4.3%**) and
frame 2's parse alone 6.829 against 6.989 (**-2.3%**), where the same profile
change moves the whole ten-worker benchmark -1.8% and the marginal steady frame
-3.6% of wall. The trough is the same shape: 1.6-3.0 of ten busy cores through
the milliseconds where frame 2's parse is the only unfinished work. **This is
why the fill's share of the gap rose: it is latency-bound on a spec-fixed
serial chain, so a whole-program code-generation win cannot reach it.**
**SCALE-073 then priced the length**: a byte-exact prep job cut the span to
12.743 ms (-20.9%) and moved neither width. **Back-to-back is not critical.**

**SCALE-046 is rejected on measurement**: its 2 frames of 92 are coded
sequence 0 and 5, both inside the fill and neither in the steady window, and
the timeline prices them under 0.1 ms. Its ledger row carries the census.

### Benchmark region decomposition (SCALE-068, post-LTO)

The 30-frame benchmark split by `--limit` rather than by trace, so one
instrument covers both decoders: `wall(L) - wall(L-1)` is the marginal cost of
admitting output frame `L`, and `T x dwall - dcpu` is the idle core-time that
region carries. Output frame 1 is coded frame 5 (order hint 1, SCALE-046's
census), so `--limit=2` adds coded frames 1-5, the first pyramid. Medians of 9
alternating reps on the shipped `73d436e05b...` artifact; the regions sum to
the gap by construction. `pre` is SCALE-053's same-instrument reading on
`2f6a5d1fc`, before the fat-LTO profile.

| Region | 10T gap | pre | 8T gap | pre | 4T gap | pre | 3T gap | pre |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| fixed, `--limit=1` | **-6.60 ms** | -3.98 | **-2.07** | -0.14 | +4.22 | +4.94 | +4.69 | +5.07 |
| coded frames 1-5, the fill | **+10.82** | +10.01 | **+9.42** | +8.42 | +0.90 | +1.12 | +1.22 | +2.74 |
| output frames 2-7 | +2.47 | +3.94 | +4.04 | +5.35 | +1.78 | +1.50 | +3.90 | +4.22 |
| output frames 8-29 | +7.00 | +7.21 | +3.81 | +6.57 | **-8.97** | -4.61 | **-15.92** | -7.27 |
| **benchmark gap** | **+13.69** | +17.17 | **+15.20** | +20.20 | **-2.07** | +2.94 | **-6.11** | +4.76 |

**Fat LTO compressed every region except the fill, so the fill is now the whole
shape of both remaining losses**: its share of the gap rose from 58% to **79%**
at ten workers and from 42% to **62%** at eight, while steady fell 0.21 and
2.76 ms and the fixed term deepened its credit by 2.62 and 1.93. Splot spends
26.26 ms of wall on 170.6 core-ms there at ten workers, **6.50 of 10 busy
cores** against dav2d's 15.44 ms on 153.7 at **9.96**; at eight it is 5.76
against 8.17, at four 3.80 against 3.98 and at three 2.73 against 2.98. Running
the fill at dav2d's own occupancy is worth **-9.13 ms at 10T, 5.41% of the
benchmark against 5.05% pre-LTO, and -8.03 at 8T, 4.56% against 3.83%** — but
that unreachable ceiling still only reaches 1.029x at ten workers and 1.045x at
eight, so **the fill family cannot win either lost width even in the limit**.

### Endgame ranking (SCALE-068)

Ceilings are priced at the current 1:1 conversion: steady occupancy is at
parity, so one core-ms per frame is 0.113 ms of steady wall, 3.3% of splot's
3.405, and the 1%-of-wall stacking bar is 0.30 core-ms. **The order is
unchanged from SCALE-053 and the fill's lead over the rest widened.**

1. **The first-pyramid fill, now 79% of the ten-worker gap and 62% of the
   eight-worker one. Ceilings above; SCALE-054's mechanism and SCALE-055's
   price both reconfirm at the new numbers.** The chain is AV2 § 8.2: the six
   parses of coded frames 2-7 are 100.0% back-to-back with zero slack, and this
   stream's single tile with `context_update_tile_id = 0` makes the end of a
   frame's only tile the earliest legal publication point, so it cannot be
   shortened. Its one successor with headroom, a within-frame row-granular
   parse-to-reconstruct handoff, was priced by SCALE-055 and stays **NO-GO**.
   Re-pricing that replay by the ceiling's own move (x1.072 at ten workers,
   x1.192 at eight) gives full handoff **-2.19% / -3.54%**, the bundled parse
   re-key **-2.91% / -5.23%**, and the unreachable no-parse-gate bound
   **-3.52% / -5.94%**, against a 4%-at-both-widths go line. **Eight workers
   now crosses on the bundle and ten never crosses under any assumption**, so
   the binding side is unchanged and so is the verdict. Decisive either way:
   at the bundled price both widths still lose, 1.057x and 1.037x. **The other
   way into this chain — making the parse itself faster, since it runs
   back-to-back — is closed by SCALE-069**, which decomposed a fill frame's
   parse and found the coefficient symbol walk is 9% of it, every itemised
   mechanism at or under 0.1 ms of a link, and the 2.2x against dav2d in the
   per-block object model rather than in per-symbol work. **SCALE-070 then
   priced that object model** — zero-fill, the pooled quant round trip, the
   984-byte plane move, the 88-byte block `Result` — at **0.171 ms of a
   6.78 ms link**, 5.8x under its 1 ms line, while the mutex-free half of the
   arena, built byte-exact, *regresses* the link by 0.066 ms. **SCALE-071
   priced the one slice it could not**, against that row's own 0.83 ms line:
   a real out-pointer decode measures **0.000 ms**, because AAPCS64 already
   returns the 104-byte `Result` indirectly through `x8`, and the fill,
   re-measured on distinct buffers at the pool's real temperature, is
   **0.059 ms** — 14x under. **SCALE-073 closed it one level up**: hoisting the
   chain's own 0.876 ms-per-link CDF-independent prologue off it byte-exactly
   bought -20.9% of span and **0.0 ms of wall**.
2. **Steady CPU residual**, +4.611 core-ms per frame at ten workers, down from
   +5.467. It is worth 0.521 ms of steady wall — steady parity is now within
   reach of this term alone (2.884 against dav2d's 2.851) — but only about
   **+2.7 ms of the 30-frame benchmark at 10T and +0.6 at 8T**, because the
   benchmark's late frames run at 1.030 and 1.037x against steady state's
   1.194x. Stage attribution in the refreshed ledger below. **Every named
   sub-term is still under the bar**, and the term is now **100% parallel
   inflation**: splot's one-worker steady frame went below dav2d's, so there is
   no scalar residue left to charge it to. That is SCALE-034's territory, half
   of it host and memory-system pressure both SCALE-034 and SCALE-050 measured
   as N one-worker decodes against one N-worker decode on an identical core mix.
3. **Steady idle residual**, 4.28 ms at 10T and 3.16 at 8T over output frames
   8-29, down from 4.83 and 4.53, against 0.53-0.73 cores of idle in true
   steady state. It is therefore depth fill and drain inside the benchmark
   window rather than a steady term, and it is item 1's mechanism measured
   later in the run.
4. **The fixed `--limit=1` cost** is no longer a term at any width: it is a
   credit of **-6.60 ms at 10T and -2.07 at 8T** and only +4.22 / +4.69 at 4T
   and 3T, both of which are won outright. SCALE-056 already showed the width
   dependence is dav2d's `--framedelay max(T, 3)` and not splot's, and LTO
   deepened splot's credit by a further 2.62 and 1.93 ms.

**Best next candidate per lost width: none.** Both lost widths name the fill,
and after SCALE-073 the fill has no successor at any level — SCALE-054 closed
every reachable shape, SCALE-055 priced the row-granular handoff, SCALE-068
re-priced it at the post-LTO ceiling, SCALE-069 closed the parse's symbol
side, SCALE-070 its object side, SCALE-071 the ABI and cold-fill residue, and
SCALE-073 the chain's length itself, at a measured zero conversion rate. The
fill's unreachable ceiling leaves 1.029x and 1.045x, so no fill candidate
wins either width alone; the two remaining losses need the fill *and* item 2,
which is entirely parallel inflation with no member above the bar.

### Normalized steady-state CPU ledger (SCALE-049)

Both decoders' marginal steady frame, split by one rule table applied to both
symbol sets. Core-ms per frame, `--limit=120` minus `--limit=60` over 60 frames
for the totals and `/usr/bin/sample` at 1 ms over the steady window for the
shares. Film grain is absent from the stream. At parity occupancy 1 core-ms per
frame is 0.113 ms of steady wall, **3.3% of splot's 3.405**, so the 1%-of-wall
stacking bar is 0.30 core-ms. **SCALE-068 refreshed splot's columns on the
shipped artifact** (6 reps at ten workers, 3 at one); dav2d's are SCALE-049's,
unchanged binary and build. `LTO` is the same-source ten-worker move from a
plain-release build of this exact tree to the shipped fat-LTO one, 6 reps each.

| Stage | splot 1T | dav2d 1T | splot 10T | dav2d 10T | 10T delta | LTO | pre-LTO 10T delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| entropy / parse | 0.306 | 0.595 | 0.204 | 0.832 | −0.628 | −0.06 | +0.791 |
| MV / projection | 2.877 | 2.480 | 4.484 | 3.508 | **+0.976** | −0.08 | +2.194 |
| prediction + MC | 6.943 | 2.566 | 9.437 | 3.532 | **+5.905** | +0.48 | +6.140 |
| residual / transforms | 0.223 | 0.825 | 0.181 | 0.653 | −0.472 | −0.23 | +0.087 |
| deblock | 1.448 | 1.056 | 2.058 | 1.671 | +0.387 | −0.06 | +0.382 |
| CDEF + CCSO | 2.209 | 3.423 | 2.404 | 4.098 | −1.694 | **−1.14** | −0.663 |
| LR / wiener | 4.724 | 7.233 | 5.817 | 8.758 | **−2.941** | +0.77 | −4.553 |
| GDF | 0.260 | 1.872 | 0.305 | 2.203 | −1.898 | +0.05 | −1.355 |
| publication / copies | 2.079 | 0.112 | 2.398 | 0.061 | **+2.337** | **−0.82** | +3.581 |
| scheduling overhead | 0.019 | 0.011 | 2.862 | 0.325 | **+2.537** | **+0.52** | +0.724 |
| **total** | **21.097** | **20.173** | **30.157** | **25.641** | **+4.516** | **−0.56** | **+7.327** |

**Read the `LTO` column as attribution as much as work.** Fat LTO inlines
across crates, so a helper that had its own frame is now charged to its caller;
that is why CDEF, residual and the copies fall while prediction, LR and
scheduling rise. Only the total, **−0.56 core-ms**, is instrument-independent,
and it reproduces the −0.67 measured directly. Two readings do survive the
caveat because they move the largest named non-kernel terms: **publication and
copies fell 25%** and now sit at +2.34 against dav2d where SCALE-049 had them
at +3.58, the biggest single improvement in the ledger since that row; and
**scheduling rose 22%**, the one sub-term that moved the wrong way, landing at
+2.54 and 0.32 ms of steady wall — exactly on the 1%-of-wall bar and inside
SCALE-032's coordination bound, with every scheduler shape already closed by
SCALE-024, SCALE-042 and K10.16/K10.25/K10.31/K10.36. Totals carry a <0.01
core-ms unmatched residue. The splot 1T column crossing below dav2d's is the
same result the marginal-frame table reports, seen stage by stage.

### Ten-thread timeline attribution (SCALE-043)

Measured on `10293339f`, before SCALE-045; its splot column and idle attribution
are superseded by SCALE-047 and SCALE-068 above. **Its dav2d reference stands**,
carried into the steady table above (`P` 3.38 ms, `W` 29.87 core-ms, `C` 7.13,
`k` 2.11, busy 8.83-9.02 cores, 12-15% above its work floor), plus three terms
nothing has re-measured: dav2d holds 3.68 frames with a running task against
splot's 3.62, so **ten dav2d frame contexts buy it nothing**; it keeps 9.6-10.5
live `(frame, stage)` chains against splot's 6.7, so its supply exceeds the pool
width; and its head-of-frame cycle is 3.24 ms, **96% of its own period**.

## Delivery discipline

Only one implementation candidate is active at a time. For each candidate:

1. Start from the current `origin/main`.
2. State the bottleneck, evidence, expected ceiling, and acceptance threshold.
3. Implement the smallest architecture change that tests the hypothesis.
4. Check exact output before timing.
5. Run the full paired thread matrix.
6. Reject and fully revert a regression or immaterial result.
7. For a material result: `rust-simplify`, correctness gates, commit, PR, merge, refresh `origin/main`, then the next candidate.

Every newly discovered bottleneck or rejected experiment is recorded in the open-task checklist before work moves on; completed and rejected evidence is retained below.

## Task ledger

| ID | Status | Finding / task | Evidence and next action |
|---|---|---|---|
| SCALE-001 | Confirmed | dav2d uses a global queue over frame contexts and three row-progress passes: entropy, MV resolution, and reconstruction/filter progress. | With the canonical command, dav2d creates 2/4/8/10 persistent worker threads and the same number of frame contexts; the caller remains a separate admission thread. One tile task advances one superblock row, publishes, and continues or requeues. Use this as the reference model. |
| SCALE-002 | Rejected | Enable split entropy/reconstruction at two workers. | Lowering the four-worker bound to two improved 2T by 17.0% in a 9-pair diagnostic run and was byte-identical at 1/2/4/8/10T, but the full gate proved that the split path raises peak live reference storage from 110,592 to 122,880 bytes at both 2T and 3T. The source change was reverted and no code PR was opened. Superseded by SCALE-022: the serial-peak condition was unachievable by construction, and main already paid the higher peak at four or more workers where the three-thread test never looked. |
| SCALE-003 | Rejected standalone | Queue entropy work from multiple frame contexts instead of synchronously parsing one whole frame on the driver. | A three-context POC derived pending-slot geometry from the header, provisionally refreshed references with terminal CDF/CCSO handles, and scheduled entropy under exact primary/blend/CCSO dependencies. It was output-identical in focused 1/4/10T checks and frame-delay 1/2/3/10 tests, but an 11-pair 10T screen moved the median only from 294.8 ms to 286.5 ms, 2.8%; ten entropy contexts regressed. SCALE-004 did not unlock more value, so revert rather than merge this large foundation. Reintroduce pending entropy products only as part of a row-progress design that materially shortens the dependency chain. |
| SCALE-004 | Rejected | Replace the all-at-once reconstruction task dump with a bounded dav2d-style wavefront that advances and republishes one unit at a time. | An exact POC removed mutex-held commit replay with an owned baton, admitted four-unit precomputes lazily under a global pool-width budget, charged permits only after row conditions became ready, and prioritized entropy/commit before speculation. The existing precompute/shadow-surface plus serial replay split is the wrong unit: bounding it removes useful work without advancing reference/filter publication. Revert and pursue SCALE-008's direct resumable row owner instead of tuning this scheduler. |

In 11 alternating pairs, 1T moved from 0.962320 s to 0.968369 s (paired +0.38%), 8T from 0.264656 s to 0.258346 s (-2.94%), and 10T from 0.267150 s to 0.261461 s (-1.92%). Its raw 30-frame output matched main at SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`. In 11 alternating paired samples, 1T was neutral (0.979014 s baseline, 0.979486 s candidate), while 8T regressed from 0.265387 s to 0.480559 s (paired median +80.98%) and 10T from 0.267624 s to 0.487116 s (+82.35%). The final post-CI alternating matrix measured 0.996527 s versus exact base 0.996898 s at 1T (+0.04%), 0.241250 s versus 0.272401 s at 8T (+12.91%), and 0.242316 s versus 0.275443 s at 10T (+13.67%). Dav2d remains faster at 10T (0.152885 s), so the final 50%-faster target remains open. A GeneralIntra band-target extension was byte-exact but regressed parallel performance and was fully reverted. |

| SCALE-011-K10.35 | Rejected; no PR | Measure whether portable scalar Rust is leaving a material backend scheduling/code-layout gain on Apple Silicon before changing more source architecture. | The exact-source `-C target-cpu=native` Apple M2 build produced byte-identical 1T/10T raw output, but five-pair screens regressed 8T by 1.071% (0.247375 s versus 0.244559 s) and improved 10T by only 0.271% (0.244506 s versus 0.242773 s); three 1T pairs regressed 0.550% (1.020669 s versus 1.016043 s). Host-native scheduling/code layout is neutral and Splot already narrowly wins this stream at 1T; keep generic AArch64 code generation. |

Focused parallel/context/pipeline tests passed and 1T/10T raw output was exact. Five-pair screens improved 8T by only 0.369% and regressed 10T by 0.766%; it remained 52.83%/57.40% slower than dav2d. Rayon also permanently registers/leaks the joined caller, forcing later reusable-context calls to fall back. A worst-case 1080p edge list adds about 3.1 MB/frame even at an unrealistic eight bytes per entry, or 4.7–6.2 MB at 12–16 bytes, plus another traversal and cache traffic. A private clipped-MC direct-write proof could target the 31-sample subset, while an established prevalidated LR window could target 10, but neither is a qualifying candidate and mixing them crosses domains. |

| SCALE-011-K10.58 | Completed | Re-rank the remaining exact 10-thread profile after the K10.12–K10.57 closures. | Resumed with a fresh two-stage diagnostics-only instrumentation pass on exact main. First, a settle census over the scheduled row-reference gate measured zero settle-collapsed batches out of 954 in all three 10T runs, closing the settle-bounding hypothesis. Second, a frame-lifecycle timeline decomposed the steady state: pool utilization is 54% (1,255 worker-ms against a 231 ms wall), scheduler cost is 0.029 ms per commit link, and the 7.26 ms/frame steady pace is set by two nearly equal recurrences. The recon-lane recurrence L/3 = 7.11 ms: admission fires a median 0.04 ms after frame N−3's lane release, and depth is capped by `ReconAdmissionLane::new(ring.capacity().min(3))`, which `--frame-delay` never widens. The publication recurrence W = 7.48 ms: a frame's reference-row watermark first rises only after its commit spine advances 5.35 ms and the stripe-0 final-filter job runs, while consumers need those rows 5.62 ms after their own admission; this lands as the spine's 5.14 ms precompute wait (row-bounded batches wait 6.78 ms/batch on high-dependency frames versus 0.66 ms on low, r = 0.79). Entropy is exonerated in the tail — each frame's parse ends a mean 41.7 ms before its recon admission, so the 52 ms of parse-chain gaps measured in the first stage are driver back-pressure, not an upstream constraint. The ordered commit spine spans 14.36 ms of the 21.32 ms lane occupancy: 8.25 ms execution across 34 links, 5.14 ms precompute wait, 1.00 ms scheduler. Depth-alone experiments (A3, K10.16) were neutral because W remained binding. Successor: SCALE-013. |
| SCALE-013 | Completed | Publish filter stripes one superblock row earlier by replacing the whole-superblock-row deblock back-off with the exact six-mode-info-row reconstruction read lead. | Committed in `d1eb9ad0e`. `safe_deblock_mi_end` previously returned `(completed_rows - 1) * sb_h4`, so the first final-filter stripe waited for two complete superblock rows (30 of 135 units) and the first reference-row publication landed a median 13.1 ms after admission. Ordinary current-frame prediction reads only one row above a superblock boundary (luma forces reference line 0, chroma has no MRL, cross-component references clamp to the superblock's first row); the binding writer is the vertical deblock pass spanning two mode-info rows on subsampled chroma, giving an exact six-row lead. A five-row lead diverges — derivation and falsification agree. First publication drops to 6.9 ms. Byte-exact 1T/10T (186,624,000 bytes, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`); corpus oracle and full `cargo xtask ci` green. Eleven alternating pairs: 10T +5.64% (0.238086 s to 0.224667 s, 11/11 positive), 8T +5.52% (0.240687 s to 0.227410 s, 11/11), 4T +1.36%, 1T neutral, 2T −0.07% (noise). Lane-depth 4/5 on top was byte-exact, passed the reference-storage peak gate, but showed no consistent direction (+0.53% median, 4/7 positive) and was dropped; `ring.capacity().min(3)` stands. Performance caveat: stripe readiness now lands on exact equality at several superblock rows, so future changes to the stripe window margin, deblock pass reach, or pass-0 lane count can silently forfeit the win without breaking exactness. |
| SCALE-014 | Completed | Narrow the subpel MC intermediate arithmetic to 16-bit lanes where the normative ranges allow. | Committed in `de4d4d6df`. § 7.13.3.16 fixes `InterRound0 = 3` with 8/10-bit conformance (§ 6 Table 6.3), so the horizontal intermediate lies in `[-7161, 23529]` and fits i16; the vertical accumulator stays i32 (widening multiply-accumulate). The fused compound scratch, compound horizontal kernel, bilinear pair, clipped-edge/compound/copy variants, and TIP overlap followed; `ACTIVE_TAP_SPANS` pruning retained; warp untouched. A new extreme-contrast test drives both proven bounds against an i64 oracle across all 256 phase pairs. Byte-exact at 1T/10T; 239-fixture decoder oracle green. Paired medians versus the stacked base: 1T -0.64% (9/11), 2T -1.13% (7/11), 4T -0.60% (3/5), 8T -0.55% (10/15), 10T -0.85% (15/21) — consistently positive, retained under the stacking policy. Honest magnitude: ~1%, not the estimated 4-8%, because a fresh 1T sample profile attributes only ~13% of CPU to all subpel MC kernels; wienerns, CDEF, warp, pc_wiener, find_mv_stack, and GDF are each comparable or larger. The 1T scalar queue must be re-ranked against that profile. |
| SCALE-022 | Completed | Enable the scheduled split parse/reconstruction path at two and three workers. | Committed in `851bf8816`; the candidate is one character (`splittable_inter_frame` width bound 4 to 2) plus the retargeted storage test. Eleven alternating pairs: 2T +20.07% (11/11 positive, 0.782585 s to 0.625525 s), 3T +39.39% (5/5), 1T/4T/8T/10T noise; 2T occupancy 1.35 to 1.80 cores for 7% more total CPU; 2T goes from 1.4x to ~1.15x dav2d and 3T to ~1.08x. Byte-exact at 1/2/3/10T; full `cargo xtask ci` green. Storage finding that unblocked SCALE-002's rejection: the split path's peak live reference storage is inherent (a frame evicted from the reference slots can still be a selected reference of a not-yet-run reconstruction because refresh is recorded from parse products) and is bounded by serial peak plus one decoded frame per resolved frame delay — proportional to pipeline depth, not worker count, exactly tight at (2 workers, delay 2) and (3, 3), and already paid by main at four or more workers where the old three-thread gate never looked. That gate asserted the bound, not an invariant; `pipelined_hash_decode_bounds_reference_storage_by_the_resolved_frame_delay` now asserts the depth-proportional cap at 2/3/8 workers with delays 2 and 3. An early sub-finding was corrected during landing: first-breach values had suggested a constant plus-one-frame peak; true peak probing (24 repeats per configuration) established the depth-proportional form. |
| SCALE-016 | Completed | Filter LR/PC-Wiener blocks directly into the post-LR stripe and delete the per-block staging traffic. | Committed in `aaab561b3`. The post-LR stripe is destination-only until GDF reads it, so LR kernels write their rectangles through `rect_mut` with the stripe stride, removing the per-block zero-fill, the `write_lr_block` copy, and PC-Wiener's private staging vector (~4.2 ms of a 1008 ms 1T run; `filter_lr_stripe` 115.6 to 111.3 ms). GDF's dual pre-LR/post-LR source requirement keeps both stripe planes structurally necessary, and instrumented ceilings for the surviving bulk copies (CDEF deblocked-to-stripe 3.73 ms, post-LR seed copy 2.05 ms) close rolling-CDEF and copy-coverage variants as disproportionate. Byte-exact at 1T/10T; oracle and full `cargo xtask ci` green. Paired medians versus the stacked base: 1T +0.48%, 2T +0.82%, 4T +0.00%, 8T +0.27%, 10T +0.96%, no level negative. |
| SCALE-023 | Completed | Admit a fourth reconstruction frame context after the stacked tree made the lane recurrence strictly binding. | Committed in `541eb1ffc`. A re-decomposition on the stacked tree first showed SCALE-013 delivered its W reduction cleanly (first publication 13.1 to 5.8 ms median; W 7.48 to 4.76) while the lane edge stayed exactly tight, so depth again had headroom: `min(3)` to `min(4)` at the recon admission lane measured +1.506% at 10T (9/11) and +0.814% at 8T (9/11), 4T +0.64%, 1T/2T provably identical code path (ring clamps), byte-exact, depth-proportional storage test green, full `cargo xtask ci` green. Depth 5 was dropped: −1.395% head-to-head against depth 4 (1/5 positive). Instrumented depth-4 trace: admit-to-pre0 waiting doubles while real residency grows only 16.18 to 17.81 ms, so L/D is near-invariant (6.75 to 6.62) — extra lanes add no ready work; the win came from collapsing the publication slack (2.57 to 0.21 ms), making the pace publication-bound. This bounds every lane-yielding scheme: SCALE-021 is closed as covered by the depth sweep until S falls. |
| SCALE-028 | Committed; 10T/8T/2T clear the bar | Take the § 7.17 deblock frontier and its stripe-window extraction off the ordered commit baton and run them on a per-frame ordered frontier chain. | Committed in `20f25c390` (seal), `5a99a068d` (chain) and `006c2603b` (recycled copy). Slice 1 seals each completed superblock row into a per-frame filter copy so the chain owns the pixels it filters; slice 2 hands each sealed row to a `frontier(c)` chain that advances deblock, takes the stripes, and whose terminal link runs deblock finish, record restore, terminal stripes and the finish submission, while the terminal commit still sets `recon_done` and recycles the spine workspace. Byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10T (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`); focused suites, 9/9 `frame_pipelining` including the untouched depth-proportional storage gate, `check-duplication` at budget, bare `cargo xtask ci` exits 0 but is **structurally blind to this change** — `run_ci` never builds `--release`, so it is evidence of no non-release regression and nothing more. Paired medians against frozen `0200bf08f` with same-source A/A nulls: 10T **−3.385%** (10/11) against null −0.572%, 8T **−1.160%** (10/11) against null −0.894%, 2T **−2.987%** (5/5) against null −0.057%, 4T +0.497% (1/5) against null +0.210%, 1T −0.279% (3/3, and the split path needs two workers so 1T runs unchanged code). Pooled over three matrices per level the picture is stable: 10T ≈ −3.0% (26/27 pairs positive), 8T ≈ −1.5% (30/33). **The 8T result is below the 2%-beyond-null retention bar this candidate was set**; 10T and 2T clear it, and no level regresses beyond null noise, so retention is a maintainer call under the 2026-07-31 stacking policy — `git reset --hard 0200bf08f` drops both commits. Slice 2b, `006c2603b`, removes the copy's construction cost: every sealed row is fully overwritten, so the copy is built over recycled plane storage with no fill, and the retained plane pool now hands a plane a buffer it already fits instead of the last one pushed — taking a chroma buffer for a luma plane reallocated it and left the next chroma plane the oversized one, which is why the copy almost never found reusable storage. Over a 30-frame run at ten threads the copy takes pooled storage for 63 of its 75 planes instead of 19, plane fill falls 34.9 to 27.2 ms and plane allocation 3.1 to 0.6 ms summed over the workers. Marginal against frozen `291bdad00` over four 11-pair matrices: 10T **−0.87%** (31/44 pairs) against nulls −0.233/+1.063/−0.042%, 8T **−0.70%** (29/44) against nulls +0.331/+0.092%, 1T −0.774% (3/3), 2T −0.759% (4/5), 4T a wash (−0.359% over nine pairs against a −0.507% null). Cumulative against `0200bf08f`: 10T −2.934% (10/11), 8T −2.113% (8/11), 2T −2.101% (5/5), 1T −0.659% (3/3), 4T +0.971% (1/5), so **8T now clears the 2% bar** the chain alone missed. Slice 3, relaxing the frontier for the sealed path from the SCALE-013 six-row read lead to deblock's own reach (`F = 32c − 4`, refusing `32c − 3`), was implemented, measured and reverted: it is byte-exact and the derivation holds, but it releases no stripe earlier and measures null (10T −0.717/+0.169/−0.780%, 16/33 pairs; 8T −0.252/+0.447/+0.004%, 17/33; nulls +0.541/−0.237%). The mechanism is arithmetic: a stripe is ready when `final_luma_rows` reaches its end plus a `16 << subsampling_y` halo, stripe ends sit 64 luma rows apart, and the two leads differ by 8. Under the six-row lead the frontier already lands exactly on a stripe boundary (`128c − 40` equals the `88 + 64s` requirement at `s = 2c − 2`), so both leads make stripes `0..=2c − 2` ready after `c` superblock rows — verified over 128/64-superblock, 4:2:0/4:4:4 and 1080p/720p geometries. **The six-row lead costs the frontier nothing and is not a lever.** Two mechanism findings. First, the design's intended shape — seal into `OwnedFrameBands` and reuse `advance_bands` — is dead on arrival: the segmented deblock kernel costs 2.8x the contiguous one (`deblock_plane_pass` 106.0 to 241.7 ms over the same 1266 calls, 10T +10.2% over five pairs), the same 2.2x SCALE-011-K10.9 measured. Sealing into a second contiguous `CurrentFrameWorkspace` keeps the fast kernel and costs only 4.5–5.3 ms of copy plus 8.4 ms of workspace allocation per 30-frame run. Second, the chain must filter its three planes in sequence: a nested Rayon section inside the frontier link executes another queued job on the same worker, and a sampled hang proved that job can be the very commit waiting for the storage the link holds. Slice 4 re-ran the SCALE-023 recon-lane depth sweep on the shortened chain and rejected it. `ReconAdmissionLane::new(ring.capacity().min(4))` to `min(5)` is byte-exact at 10T (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14…`) and 9/9 on `frame_pipelining` with the depth-proportional reference-storage gate untouched and green, but against frozen `006c2603b` it measures 10T −0.298% (6/11) and +0.514% (4/11) against a −0.944% A/A null, and 8T −0.306% (8/11) against a −0.457% null — inside or behind the null at both levels, nothing like SCALE-023's +1.506%/+0.814%. Depth 6 was therefore not built, and no other level can move: `ring.capacity()` is the resolved frame delay, so at 1/2/4 threads `min(5)` and `min(4)` clamp to the same depth and run identical code. The prediction that a shorter per-frame commit chain re-opens depth does not hold; the lane edge is not what binds after the frontier split. Slice 4 also skipped the `OwnedFrameBands`-style `release_through` of consumed sealed rows rather than building it, on three grounds. The construction cost it would target is already gone: slice 2b builds the copy over pooled plane storage with no fill (6-buffer process pool, 63 of 75 planes recycled, 0.6 ms of allocation per 30-frame run). The 4.5–5.3 ms that remains is the row copy itself, and releasing a row after the frontier consumes it does not remove the copy that put it there. And the footprint it would save is both small and structurally unreachable — at most four in-flight sealed copies of 6.22 MB against a 601 MB peak RSS at ten threads, and a contiguous plane allocation cannot free its own prefix, so a ~3-superblock-row cap is a rolling banded buffer: exactly the segmented shape this row already measured at 2.8x on the deblock kernel. |
| SCALE-029 | Rejected; no source change | Publish reference-row watermarks as the § 7.21 guided detail filter finalizes rows inside a filter stripe, instead of only at stripe end. | Step-1 diagnostics on exact `f1a0ac1ae` logged, per pending frame, every `FrameProgress::row_condition` registration with the watermark it saw, every stripe publication, the guided-detail start and end of each stripe chain, and an eight-row progress marker inside both § 7.21 row loops; three ten-thread 30-frame runs were reconstructed offline. Consumers do wait: 22 to 36 row-gate registrations per steady frame block on a threshold above the published watermark, for 72.4 to 80.3 ms/frame summed over blocked consumers. But the part of that wait **sub-stripe publication can recover** is only **0.546 / 0.553 / 0.604 ms per steady frame** summed over every blocked consumer, and **0.285 / 0.288 / 0.303 ms per steady frame** as the union of the intervals in which any consumer was blocked purely by stripe-end granularity — under the 1 ms/frame kill line in all three runs, and about a fifth of the 1.5 ms/frame implement line. Two mechanisms cap it. First, § 7.21 runs on only 7 of 28 steady frames (136 of 544 stripes; `gdf_config` yields nothing on the rest and `apply_stripe` returns in about 200 nanoseconds), so on 21 of 28 frames the last writer of a luma row is § 7.17 loop restoration and guided-detail row granularity recovers exactly zero; where it does run it is worth 2.18–2.42 ms/frame summed and 1.14–1.21 ms union, which the 25% duty cycle amortizes away. Second, even there the window is bounded by one stripe's guided-detail pass — 0.85–0.98 ms measured, matching `filter_gdf_stripe` at 119.6 ms over its 136 active calls — because CDEF, CCSO and loop restoration are whole-stripe passes that must all finish before the stripe's first row can be finalized. The premise that the filter stripe publishes late is right; the premise that stripe-end **granularity** is what makes it late is wrong. The same reconstruction located the term that does dominate, recorded as SCALE-030: publication lagging the stripe chain that produced it, worth 9.0–9.6 ms/frame summed over consumers and 2.36–2.67 ms/frame as an interval union. All diagnostics were reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `f1a0ac1ae`, so no exactness or timing matrix was run. |
| SCALE-030 | Committed in `c46a24085`; retention call open | Publish a filter stripe from the reader whose prefix borrow made its `try_write` fail, instead of leaving it for the next stripe or the terminal drain. | **Mechanism, measured on exact `f1a0ac1ae` with an env-gated timestamp on every queued stripe over three 10T 30-frame runs (544 stripes each).** Before: median publication lag 16–19 us, but **27.8 / 29.4 / 27.8%** of stripes deferred past 100 us, p90 **1192 / 1243 / 1047 us**, p99 **3024 / 3801 / 4071 us**, max 5089 / 7189 / 6226 us, **169.6 / 194.4 / 174.0 ms** summed, and **55 / 44 / 41** stripes landing only at the terminal `BeforeFreeze` drain. The cause is retry cadence, not lock occupancy: 73% of stripes win `try_write` on the first attempt, so the lock is free most of the time — but a stripe that loses it has no further retry until the *next* stripe finishes, roughly 1 ms later, which is exactly the p90. The filter side cannot fix that; it offers only 17–26 retry points per frame. **Design.** The backlog moves out of `OwnedFilterSetup::filtered` into `FrameProgress` as `StripeCopy` closures, and `PublishedFrame`'s `Drop` releases the shared borrow and then calls `drain_pending`. Readers take the prefix once per predicted block — thousands of times per frame — so the retry cadence becomes the consumer cadence, and each attempt fires at the instant a blocking borrow ends. **The non-blocking rationale is preserved literally**: every attempt on the hot path is still `try_write`, so no stripe and no reader ever queues behind a writer, and the cross-frame cycle the borrow rules warn about (two readers each holding the other frame's prefix) stays impossible because neither ever waits. The one blocking write is the terminal drain, which already blocked and runs only after the phase is over. Exactly-once and fail-atomicity are unchanged: the queue is emptied by `mem::take` under the exclusive lock the copies need, so a stripe is taken by one drain and lands once; a failed copy abandons the rest of its batch, publishes no watermark for itself or anything behind it, and records its diagnostic for the next `publish_stripe` or the terminal drain to return. **After, same three runs:** **15.6 / 19.3 / 20.0%** deferred, p90 **211 / 305 / 279 us**, p99 **1666 / 1327 / 1447 us**, **52.6 / 55.0 / 51.8 ms** summed, **6 / 5 / 2** stripes at the terminal drain. Split by drainer: **72.6 / 75.9 / 74.8%** of stripes still land on their own worker at median 0 us, **26.3 / 23.2 / 24.8%** land on a reader releasing the prefix at median 96–122 us, and 0.4–1.1% reach the freeze. **Timing.** Byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10T (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14…`). Paired medians against frozen `f1a0ac1ae` (same code as `89d0eb15b`), 11 pairs at 8/10T against same-source A/A nulls, 5 at 2/4T, 3 at 1T: **10T -3.309%** (9/11 faster, null **-0.090%** 6/11), **8T -2.567%** (11/11, null **-0.158%** 6/11), 4T **-0.716%** (5/5), 2T **-0.160%** (4/5), 1T **-0.239%** (3/3). Cumulative against `0200bf08f`: 10T **-6.456%** (10/11), 8T **-4.044%** (11/11), 2T **-2.938%** (5/5), 1T **-0.841%** (3/3), 4T **-0.362%** (4/5) — the +0.971% 4T cost SCALE-028 carried is gone. Suites: `frame_pipelining` 9/9 with the reference-storage gate untouched, `frame_progress` 10/10, decode `filters` 211/211, `scheduled` 4/4, decode and parallel `admission` 25/25 and 9/9; `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy -D warnings`, `check-duplication` at budget (2619), bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. **Not exhausted.** 15.6–20.0% of stripes still wait, because a reader release only frees the lock when it is the last reader; with ten workers reading a pending reference that is often false. The remaining lever is the hold itself — `RefFrameSlot::hold_samples` takes one shared borrow per predicted block and keeps it across the whole of that block's motion compensation — not another drain trigger. SCALE-031 measured that lever and closed it. |
| SCALE-031 | Rejected; no source change | Shorten the per-block reference-sample hold, or defer arriving readers, so a queued filter stripe finds the progressive workspace free instead of waiting to be the last reader out. | **Why the family reopened.** K10.32 closed progressive-workspace lock contention on *blocking*: all 75 `semaphore_wait_trap` samples belonged to that one `RwLock`, 69 were reader acquisitions, and nonterminal publication used `try_write` and could not park. That reasoning is untouched. SCALE-030 changed what a *hold duration* costs rather than what a wait costs: reader release became the publication trigger, so the time a block spends inside `RefFrameSlot::hold_samples` now decides when a queued stripe lands, and the hold has a measured consumer for the first time. This row measures that consumer and closes it. **Step-1 mechanism on exact `ddb14def1`**, with env-gated per-hold, per-arrival, per-stripe and per-row-gate timestamps, three 10T and three 8T 30-frame runs. The premise reproduces: 544 stripes per run, publication mode own 396–408 / reader 131–141 / freeze 2–12 at 10T, median lag 0 us, p90 **161 / 248 / 289 us**, p99 **1355 / 1709 / 1904 us**, **14.2 / 17.8 / 18.4%** deferred past 100 us, **37.1 / 58.8 / 62.4 ms** summed; 8T p90 181–192 us, p99 958–1184 us, 14.7–16.2% deferred, 37.6–40.6 ms summed. **Hold attribution**, site-tagged over one 10T run: 4,823 filtering holds, about 150 per frame, 187.7 ms held in total, mean 38.9 us — TIP batched output 935 holds / 72.4 ms / 77.4 us mean, TIP motion grid 986 / 53.3 / 54.0, pure inter MC 1,213 / 40.3 / 33.2, deferred compound grid 932 / 9.8 / 10.5, placed inter MC 346 / 4.7 / 13.5, § 7.13.3.25 BAWP 24 / 0.12 / 5.2. **§ 7.13.5 TIP owns 67% of all hold time**, and the mean hold is 39 us rather than one small block's convolution. **Occupancy.** Only 6.1–7.3% of a pending frame's span has any reader at 10T (7.8–8.4% at 8T) — but conditioned on a queued stripe it is effectively total, because a stripe only stays queued when its own immediate `try_write` already lost: of 803–974 reader arrivals that occur while a stripe is queued, **11–16 (1.3–1.8%) find the lock free**, and 88.4–92.8% of the 907–1,250 releases that see a queued stripe still leave peers behind. **Ceiling.** Instrumenting every `FrameProgress::row_condition` registration (691–717 per run, about 23 per steady frame, matching SCALE-029's 22–36) against the perfect-publication counterfactual — a stripe visible the instant its copy is queued — the recoverable consumer wait is **2.63 / 3.31 / 3.69 ms/frame summed and 0.64 / 0.68 / 0.86 ms/frame as an interval union** at 10T, and **1.08 / 1.14 / 1.22 ms/frame summed and 0.27 / 0.28 / 0.34 ms/frame union** at 8T. Both levels are under SCALE-029's 1 ms/frame kill line, and the metric is calibrated by SCALE-030 on this same path and stream: it removed 2.36–2.67 ms/frame of the same union and realized 3.3%, or 0.257 ms/frame of a 7.0 ms/frame pace, about 10% realization. Applying that gives at most **−0.9 to −1.2% at 10T and −0.4% at 8T with a cost-free mechanism**, against a 2%-beyond-null retention bar and a host A/A null band of about ±0.9% at 10T. 8T misses the bar by roughly five times before any mechanism is paid for. **The three candidate designs close individually.** Narrowing the hold to a bounded per-block window copy has to happen inside the two TIP kernels that own two thirds of the hold time, and it moves those convolutions off the contiguous reference reads: K10.23 (MC is required arithmetic) and K10.12/K10.12C (segmented or scratch reference views measured 2.2–2.8x on the hot kernels) both apply, for a sub-1% prize. Batching holds per four-superblock precompute is wrong twice over — acquire/release count is not the constraint, since only 1.3–1.8% of arrivals find a free lock and extra retry points therefore publish almost nothing, while a longer hold raises occupancy in exactly the windows where a stripe is queued and removes the last-reader releases that publish 24–26% of stripes today. Publication-priority handoff splits into a free variant and a paying one: draining once on arrival can publish only those 11–16 arrivals in 900, and a deferral that actually waits must wait the residual life of a pending window, which is heavy-tailed (p90 161–289 us, p99 1.4–1.9 ms) and sampled length-biased by about 900 arrivals per run, so a bounded spin captures only the median-20-us windows that already cost nothing while paying on every arrival. **Thread coverage.** 1T builds no `FrameProgress` at all — no pending slot, no hold and no queued stripe in a whole 30-frame run — and 2T reaches it with 4 holds and zero arrivals-with-pending, so the term exists only at 4T (343 arrivals, 3 free), 8T (525–678, 11–15 free) and 10T. All diagnostics were reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `ddb14def1`, so no exactness or timing matrix was run. |
| SCALE-032 | Investigated; no source change | Explain why splot's CPU matches dav2d's at `--limit=30` but exceeds it by 20% at `--limit=120`. | **Frame counts are equal**, so the comparison is sound: both decoders emit exactly 30/60/120 output frames (splot `frame.output_index` runs 0..N−1, dav2d reports `Decoded N/N`). Splot decodes 32 coded frames for 30 outputs and 121 for 120 — one hidden key frame, plus a one-frame overshoot at limit 30 that counts *against* splot at the short limit. **The datum reproduces and keeps going.** 10T CPU (user+sys, `/usr/bin/time -l`, median of 3 alternating pairs) splot versus dav2d is 1.400/1.340 s = **1.045x** at limit 30, 2.600/2.260 = **1.150x** at 60, 4.670/3.830 = **1.219x** at 120, 9.040/7.280 = **1.242x** at 240. **Nothing accumulates.** The marginal cost of one steady frame is flat across every window: 40.00 versus 30.67 ms CPU (**1.304x**) over frames 30–60, 34.50 versus 26.17 (**1.318x**) over 60–120, and 36.29 versus 28.67 (**1.266x**) over 120–240. The aggregate walk is pure mix: `(1.400 + 0.03633(L−30)) / (1.340 + 0.02767(L−30))` predicts 1.147 at limit 60 and 1.219 at limit 120 against measured 1.150 and 1.219, asymptote **1.313**. The 30-frame benchmark is dominated by an intra-heavy prologue costing splot 46.7 ms/frame against dav2d's 44.7; **the mission's headline 30-frame CPU parity understates the steady-state CPU gap by about seven times**, and the "+20% at 120" is that mix converging, not a late-frame defect. **It is threading, not content or state.** At 1 thread the same steady frames cost splot 24.00 ms CPU against dav2d's 22.78 (**1.054x**), and splot executes **fewer** instructions per steady frame (430.5 M versus 453.3 M). The whole gap opens under threading: at 10T instructions are at parity (463.2 versus 454.6 M/frame, +1.9%) while cycles are **113.8 versus 86.3 M/frame (+31.8%)**, IPC 4.07 versus 5.27. Going 1T to 10T, splot adds 7.6% instructions and **34.7% cycles**; dav2d adds 0.3% and 9.4%. Average clock is identical for both at each width (3.53 GHz at 1T, 3.13 GHz at 10T), so core-type mix is controlled and the term is stalled cycles, not frequency. Sys time is a minority: marginal sys is 2.0 ms/frame for splot against 0.44 for dav2d, 18% of the 8.66 ms/frame CPU gap. **It is not working-set footprint.** The 10T frame-delay sweep moves peak RSS from 240 MB to 710 MB while marginal cycles/frame stay 114.8 / 114.5 / 114.6 at delay 2/4/10 (delay 1 is *worse* at 124.4). Splot's RSS does grow with run length (590/669/729/802/837 MB at 30/60/120/240/360 frames against dav2d's 366/413/423/429/439) but that growth buys no cost. **It is not contention inside one decode.** Holding 8 busy cores fixed and regrouping the workers changes almost nothing: marginal cycles/frame are 106.4/108.7 as 1 process x 8 threads, 108.5/104.8 as 2 x 4, 103.8/104.6 as 4 x 2 — so deleting every lock, queue and shared structure between workers of one decode is worth **at most about 3%**. dav2d is flat the same way (81.6/81.2 at both 1 x 8 and 2 x 4). **The step is the split parse/reconstruct path itself.** At identical 8-core hardware pressure, 8 independent 1-thread processes (fused walk, `splittable_inter_frame` requires `current_pool_width() >= 2`, `crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/frame_walk.rs:274`) cost **92.2** M cycles/frame, while 4 independent 2-thread processes (split path) cost **104.2** — **+13.0%, about 12 M cycles/frame**, paid at the first extra worker and nearly independent of worker count thereafter. Independent-process runs also bound the pure memory-system term: 8 concurrent 1-thread decodes cost splot +8.0% and dav2d +8.1% over solo, so shared-hardware pressure is equal for both and small. dav2d's own threading is nearly free — 78.9 M cycles/frame at 1 thread to 81.4 at 8 threads in one process, **+3.2%**. **What the split path makes slower.** With `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING` at 2 workers versus 1 (its overhead is +0.8% cycles at both 1T and 10T, so the ratios are not an instrumentation artifact), the streaming kernels are indifferent — `filter_lr_stripe` 181.4 to 184.6 us/stripe, `filter_cdef_stripe` 195.0 to 197.3, `wiener_ns_luma` 18.43 to 18.61, `pc_wiener_classify` 17.34 to 17.72, `filter_gdf_stripe` 45.98 to 46.69, `inter_tip_prediction` 60.02 to 61.47, all +1 to +2% — while the entropy and intra path inflates on identical item counts: `block` 3.13 to 4.28 us (+37%), `intra_leaf` 3.12 to 4.50 (+44%), `coeff` 4.97 to 7.29 (+47%), `commit_intra` 4.43 to 5.09 (+15%). At 10 workers the same asymmetry widens to `block` x2.16, `intra_leaf` x2.45, `coeff` x1.95 against filters x1.22–1.26. The split path also multiplies units for the same pixels: `deblock_plane_pass` runs 39.4 times per frame instead of 4.8 (+17% total time) and `mode_record` writes 1,480 records per frame instead of 987. Honest accounting gap: the instrumented kernels explain about 1.3 ms/frame of the +4.1 ms/frame that 2 workers add, and the remaining ~2.8 ms/frame sits in the uninstrumented prepass and publication work that replaced the fused `commit_inter` (7.62 to 0.81 ms/frame, 288.8 to 83.7 items). A 4T-versus-10T sample profile shows no leaf moving more than 0.55 percentage points of busy time, so the extra cycles are spread across the whole kernel set rather than concentrated in one contended site; the blocking symbols do grow (`swtch_pri` 47 to 1,715 samples, `__psynch_mutexwait` 234 to 1,777) but total under 4.4% of busy samples. **The wall gap decomposes multiplicatively.** Steady-state 10T occupancy is 6.96 cores for splot against 8.59 for dav2d, so the marginal wall ratio is CPU 1.313 x occupancy 1.234 = **1.62**, matching the measured 6.29/3.38 = 1.861 (30–60), 4.66/2.96 = 1.575 (60–120) and 4.22/3.09 = 1.369 (120–240) as the pipeline fills. Per-frame CPU is therefore the larger of the two factors and has never been this mission's target. **Ceiling.** Closing the 12.0 M cycles/frame split-path step is 10.5% of splot's 113.8 M at 10T: steady CPU/frame 36.33 to 32.5 ms and, at unchanged 6.96-core occupancy, wall 5.22 to 4.67 ms/frame. Closing splot's whole parallel penalty down to dav2d's proportional one (113.8 to 92.4) is −18.8% cycles and 5.22 to 4.24 ms/frame. Conversely the coordination bound above caps every remaining scheduler, publication and lock candidate at about 3% of per-frame **CPU** — SCALE-030 already took 3.3% of wall from that family, and it did so by removing idle, so the bound constrains CPU-side candidates and not further occupancy work. **Side finding, recorded because it blocks verification:** raw and Y4M output of this stream fail past ~35 output frames. Retained output frames count against `max_reference_store_bytes` (268,435,456; `crates/splot-decode/src/limits.rs`), and `actual` reports 273,715,200 — exactly 44 frames of 6,220,800 bytes — identically at `--frame-delay` 2 and 10, so it is output retention and not pipeline depth. `--limit=35` succeeds; 40, 42, 43, 60 and 120 all raise `decode/resource-limit`. Only the discard-hash benchmark path runs beyond 35 frames, so today no byte-exactness check longer than 35 frames is possible. All measurements used existing env-gated instrumentation; no source changed and the worktree is byte-identical to `abd883e4f`. |
| SCALE-033 | Investigated; rejected; no source change | Attribute the split parse/reconstruct path's +12 M cycles/frame precisely, and remove the removable part. | **The hardware counters settle the shape before any phase timer touches it.** Marginal steady-frame cost (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60`, over 60 frames, `/usr/bin/time -l`, median of 3, frozen `8ea93605f`) is 23.33 / 26.50 / 28.00 / 32.17 / 35.50 ms CPU at 1/2/4/8/10 threads, on 412.4 / 436.3 / 435.3 / 440.4 / 444.9 M instructions and 79.3 / 90.4 / 93.4 / 103.0 / 110.9 M cycles, IPC 5.20 / 4.83 / 4.66 / 4.28 / 4.01. **Instructions step once and then stop**: the second worker adds 23.9 M/frame (+5.8%), and going 2 to 10 workers adds only 8.6 M more (+2.0%) while cycles add 20.5 M (+22.7%). Priced at the single-thread IPC of 5.20, the whole 1T-to-10T instruction growth of 32.5 M/frame buys at most **6.25 of the 31.6 M cycles/frame** the width costs, so **at least 80% of the 10-thread penalty is stall on constant work**; at 2 threads the same arithmetic gives 4.6 of 11.1 M cycles, 41% work and 59% stall. Extra work is therefore fully paid at the first extra worker, exactly as SCALE-032 predicted, and it is not what grows with width. **Attribution.** Env-gated `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING` phases were placed at the lowest points both walks share — `PendingInter::derive_motion` and `PendingInter::reconstruct_from_motion`, `crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/deferred_recon.rs:206` and `:280` — so the fused and split paths are timed on identical item counts, plus a scope on every split-only step. Medians of three `--limit=30` runs, ms summed over workers for the whole run; the instrumentation costs +2.1% CPU at 1T, +1.4% at 2T, +0.4% at 8T and +1.4% at 10T, so none of the ratios is an artifact. Against the CPU each width adds over 1T (140 / 330 / 470 ms per run at 2/8/10 threads): **(a) work the fused walk never does** 57.1 / 96.7 / 129.8 ms (41% / 29% / 28%); **(b) identical work running slower** 17.7 / 164.5 / 253.2 ms (13% / 50% / 54%); **(c) duplicated publication bookkeeping** 9.9 / 14.2 / 15.0 ms (7% / 4% / 3%); uninstrumented scheduler, pool and driver residual 55 / 55 / 72 ms (40% / 17% / 15%), so the 2-thread shares are lower bounds while the 8- and 10-thread ones are firm. **(a), itemised** (ms/run at 2T / 8T / 10T): the per-superblock rect publish `OwnedFramePlaneRect::publish_into` (`crates/splot-recon/src/workspace/owned_rect.rs:93`) 14.5 / 19.1 / 21.7 over 3,645 rects; the sealed filter copy `ScheduledTileRecon::seal_committed_rows` 6.8 / 11.5 / 23.4; the whole of `prepare_scheduled_tile` less its duplicated grid 33.0 / 39.3 / 41.3, of which the rects' allocation and zero fill are 6.6 / 9.1 / 9.1, the owned deblock records 7.6 / 10.9 / 10.9 and the second whole-frame workspace 5.3 / 2.8 / 2.6; the banded deblock cascade, which runs `deblock_plane_pass` 1,270 times per run instead of 136 over the same pixels, +9.5 / +21.0 / +33.9; stripe window and publish +2.7 / +9.0 / +10.8; frame prologue +1.8 / +7.9 / +9.9; less the fused path's own deblock grid and overlay work, −9.1, and its terminal freeze, −3.6. **Top mechanism, named: the split path materialises each frame's pixels three times** — into a per-superblock `OwnedFrameRect`, then into the frame workspace, then into the sealed filter copy. Rect allocation, rect publish, seal copy and seal workspace together are 34.1 / 42.5 / 48.8 ms per run = 1.07 / 1.33 / 1.53 ms per coded frame ≈ **3.6 M cycles/frame at 2 threads**, and a 6-second `sample` of a `--limit=400` run puts `_platform_memmove` at 6.82% of busy CPU at 2T against 3.23% at 1T, with `publish_into` its largest single caller (96 of 483 samples). **(b), on identical item counts** (us per item, 1T → 2T → 8T → 10T): `block` over 18,046 leaves 2.71 → 3.35 → 3.73 → 3.65 (x1.35 at 10T); `coeff` over 1,512 x1.34 / x1.57 / x1.49; `intra_leaf` over 10,311 x1.30 / x1.38 / x1.34; `commit_intra` over 10,311 x1.10 / x1.60 / x1.66; `motion_derive` over 7,676 x1.02 / x1.24 / x1.46; `reconstruct_from_motion` over 7,676 x1.01 / x1.15 / x1.30; `filter_cdef_stripe` over 544 x1.01 / x1.14 / x1.25; `filter_lr_stripe` x1.02 / x1.12 / x1.19; `filter_gdf_stripe` x1.01 / x1.09 / x1.14; and `deblock_plane_pass` over the identical 1,270 banded passes x1.13 / x1.27 from 2T to 10T. **The inter reconstruction core is thread-indifferent at two workers** (+2% motion, +1% prediction) and only degrades with width, while the entropy parse degrades immediately. **(c) is small, and SCALE-032's lead was a red herring.** The 14,203 replayed `record_flags` calls (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/find_mv_stack/neighbour_grid.rs:298`) cost 2.8 / 3.7 / 4.0 ms per run — 0.09 ms/frame at 2T — so the `mode_record` 987-to-1,480 inflation is worth about 2% of the step and is not a lever. The larger half of (c) is the second `NeighbourMvGrid` that `prepare_scheduled_tile` allocates per frame beside the parse pass's own, 6.5 / 9.6 / 10.0 ms per run. **SCALE-032's unattributed 2.8 ms/frame is now attributed, and it is not extra work.** `commit_inter` 310.4 ms/run at 1T becomes `prepass_row` 200.8 plus `prepass_motion_pass` 83.0 plus `commit_inter` 22.8 = 306.6 at 2T, on identical 7,676 motion derivations and 7,676 predictions: the reconstruction moved, it did not grow. **Why nothing was implemented.** The only (a) subset above the 3 M cycles/frame implementation line is the triple materialisation, and removing it means handing the prepass borrowed rects of the frame workspace instead of owned ones. `ReadyReconSurface<'static, T>` is owned precisely because the workspace is moved into `ScheduledCommit` for the frame's lifetime while the ordered commit writes into it concurrently, so that is a restructuring of scheduled-reconstruction surface ownership, not a smallest reduction. Every individually small candidate measured under the line: rect allocation 0.8 M cycles/frame, second neighbour grid 0.7, sealed-copy workspace 0.6. Two sub-candidates were measured and disproven outright — the banded temporal projection is a wash against the fused whole-field one (leaves 67.9 ms/run fused against 68.7 banded, `proj_scan` 56.0 against 56.4), and raising `MAX_RETAINED_RECON_PLANE_BUFFERS` from 6 to 32 is neutral (`sched_seal` 5.9 to 6.3 ms/run), so the sealed workspace's cost is its fill and not a pool miss. Skipping the publish for units the prepass left untouched was also bounded and rejected: 3,392 of 4,189 units precompute every entry, only 546 precompute none, so the copy is carrying real pixels. **Verification.** No candidate; the worktree is byte-identical to `8ea93605f` and the rebuilt release binary hashes identically to the frozen base (`a8830a49a544…`). Output was verified byte-exact anyway at 1/2/8/10 threads: raw 30 frames, 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be5…`. A/A null over eleven alternating pairs is −0.139% at 10T (4/11) and +0.207% at 8T (6/11), so the noise floor is about ±0.2 to 0.4%. |
| SCALE-034 | Investigated; closed | Discriminate host core heterogeneity from per-worker working-set pressure in the 10-thread CPU penalty, and decide whether a layout candidate survives. | **Width sweep.** Marginal steady frame (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60` over 60 frames, `/usr/bin/time -l`, median of 3, rebuilt release binaries, host Apple M2 Max, 8 performance cores plus 4 efficiency cores). splot cycles/frame 79.6 / 90.4 / 93.7 / 98.1 / 102.1 / 110.5 M at 1/2/4/6/8/10 threads on 412.4 / 436.2 / 436.9 / 437.2 / 440.2 / 445.1 M instructions, IPC 5.18 / 4.82 / 4.66 / 4.46 / 4.31 / 4.03, CPU 22.83 / 26.50 / 28.00 / 30.17 / 31.83 / 35.33 ms, occupancy 1.01 / 1.87 / 3.73 / 5.32 / 6.82 / 7.57 cores, peak RSS 141 / 282 / 438 / 551 / 654 / 727 MB. dav2d on the same host and build: 73.8 / 75.0 / 74.9 / 75.2 / 77.0 / 82.2 M cycles on 424.9 / 424.6 / 424.6 / 423.6 / 424.7 / 428.6 M instructions, occupancy to 9.35, RSS 68 / 135 / 210 / 284 / 356 / 423 MB. **Cycle inflation is already present and monotone well inside the performance-core count**: splot is +13.6 / +17.7 / +23.3 / +28.2 / +38.9% over its own 1T against dav2d's +1.6 / +1.4 / +1.9 / +4.3 / +11.3%, and dav2d's 1T-to-8T cost of +4.3% confirms SCALE-032's +3.2% on this host and build. Per added worker splot's slope is +10.84 M at the split-path switch, then a flat +1.62 / +2.23 / +1.96 M across 2-to-4, 4-to-6 and 6-to-8, and +4.23 M for 8-to-10 — about twice the sub-eight slope, so the boundary step is real but is not where most of the penalty is made. **Performance-core lever.** macOS exposes no performance-core affinity, but `taskpolicy -b` confines a process to the efficiency cluster, and a 1T decode there retires identical instructions (32.33 against 32.27 G) for 12.686 G cycles against 6.105 G, IPC 5.29 to 2.55: **an efficiency core costs 2.08x the cycles for the same work**. Saturating all four efficiency cores with background-priority spinners while splot runs at ordinary priority approximates a performance-core-only run, and moves splot only 98.3 to 97.9 M cycles/frame at 6T (−0.4%) and 102.6 to 101.5 at 8T (−1.1%). **At eight workers splot is already effectively performance-core-only**, so its entire +28.2% inflation there is internal to the performance cluster. **Hardware pressure against software sharing — the decisive control.** N independent single-thread decodes hold the same cores busy with zero shared software state, in separate address spaces. Marginal cycles/frame at eight busy cores: splot solo 1T 79.6, eight independent 1T processes 88.6 (**+11.3%, hardware**), one 8-thread process 103.1 (**+16.5% beyond that, software**); dav2d 73.8, 78.2 (+5.9%), 76.9 (−1.6%, its threading is free). At ten busy cores, where both shapes carry the identical core mix so heterogeneity cancels on both sides: solo 79.6, ten independent 1T processes 95.1 (+19.5%), one 10-thread process 111.0 (+16.7% beyond). **The software term is 14.5 M cycles/frame at eight workers and 15.9 at ten — it does not grow with width**, so the 8-to-10 step in the plain sweep is host topology, not further sharing. Splitting it by instructions (412.5 to 445.3 M/frame at ten, priced at the concurrent-hardware IPC of 4.34) gives 7.6 M cycles of extra work and **8.4 M cycles of stall on constant work**, against 8.45 by the same arithmetic at eight workers. **Decomposition of the 31.4 M cycles/frame 1T-to-10T penalty**: host topology and memory-system pressure at ten busy cores **15.5 M (49%)**, the split path's extra work **7.6 M (24%)** — already named and closed by SCALE-033 as the triple pixel materialisation — and stall on constant work inside one address space **8.4 M (27%)**. splot pays roughly twice dav2d's hardware term at eight cores (+11.3% against +5.9%), tracking its 1.7 to 2.0x larger footprint at every width. **Where the stall lands.** Per-item phase cost with `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING` at `--limit=30`, comparing one N-thread process against N independent 1-thread processes so hardware pressure cancels, on identical item counts. The hardware column is uniform across every phase alike (+1 to +4% at two busy cores, +5 to +6% at four, +8 to +13% at six, +11 to +19% at eight) — streaming kernels and metadata phases pay the same, which is the signature of memory-system pressure rather than a shared structure. The software column is not uniform: **every streaming filter and motion-compensation kernel is flat at every width** — at eight workers `filter_cdef_stripe` +0.2%, `filter_lr_stripe` +1.2%, `filter_gdf_stripe` +0.7%, `wiener_ns_luma` −0.5%, `pc_wiener_classify` −0.0%, `inter_tip_prediction` +2.9% — while the entropy parse and intra reconstruction carry all of it: `block` +19.4 / +17.4 / +17.4 / +19.0%, `coeff` +33.3 / +33.8 / +35.1 / +42.4%, `intra_leaf` +23.2 / +19.2 / +20.7 / +18.5%, `commit_intra` +5.0 / +19.8 / +20.1 / +33.0% at 2/4/6/8 workers. `block`, `coeff` and `intra_leaf` step at the first extra worker and then stay flat, so they are a property of the split path rather than of width; **`commit_intra` is the only member that grows with worker count**. Summed over identical-item phases the software term is 44.1 worker-ms per 30-frame run at eight workers, 88% of it in those four: `commit_intra` 16.7, `block` 10.8, `intra_leaf` 6.3, `coeff` 4.1. **Instrumentation hazard checked and cleared.** `PHASE_COUNTERS` (`crates/splot-decode/src/timing.rs:192`) is one shared atomic pair per phase, and a nested scope's two read-modify-writes land inside the enclosing scope's measured interval, which would fake exactly this result. Enabling the instrumentation costs +1.32% cycles in the eight-process shape against +1.14% in the eight-thread shape, so the counters are not contending and the attribution stands. **The shared structures, read in source.** The CDF bank is not shared and is not the target: `TileCdfWorkUnitBoundary` (`crates/splot-decode/src/bitstream/tile_payload/cdf.rs:306`) clones a private 47 KiB `TileCdfSubset` per tile through `tile_copy` (`cdf.rs:267`) and keeps only an immutable `Arc` frame bank; every neighbour-context array is per-tile as well. What the entropy and intra paths do share across workers is process-global pooling: `static TRANSFORM_COEFF_QUANT_BUFFERS: Mutex<Vec<Vec<i32>>>` (`crates/splot-decode/src/bitstream/tile_payload/coeff_state.rs:49`) is acquired twice per transform block behind a best-fit scan of up to 2,560 entries, and its buffers migrate structurally from the parse worker to the commit worker because `into_quant` (`coeff_state.rs:241`) moves them into the `ReconCommand` while `Drop for LumaCoeffBlock` (`coeff_state.rs:138`) frees them from the other core; `static PARSED_RESIDUAL_PLANES` (`crates/splot-decode/src/residual/pipeline/plane_execution.rs:91`) adds one or two acquisitions per general-intra leaf; and the ordered commit containing `commit_intra` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/pixel_commit.rs:135`, inside `replay_recon_row` at `:60`) runs on one worker at a time but not the same one, mutating the single per-tile `TileDecodeScratch.ordered` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/tile.rs:898`, used at `:1218`) drawn from a pool that never pins a scratch to a worker (`InterReconScratchPool::with_scratch`, `tile.rs:874`). **Verdict.** The addressable term is 8.4 M cycles/frame, **7.6% of the 111.0 M cycles/frame at ten threads**, below the 10 M line this row was set and flat from eight to ten workers. Its largest single member, `commit_intra`, is 38% of it — about 3.2 M cycles/frame, 2.9% of ten-thread per-frame CPU — level with SCALE-033's 3 M cycles/frame implementation line and only if the saving converts to wall at unchanged occupancy. **The CPU-side lever is closed at about 7.6% of per-frame CPU**: 49% of the penalty is host topology no decoder layout can reach, 24% is the split path's extra work already closed behind a surface-ownership restructuring, and the remainder spreads over four phases with no member large enough to carry a candidate on its own. Reopen only for a design that pins the ordered commit and its scratch to the worker that produced the row, and only if a fresh sweep puts the software stall term back above 10 M cycles/frame. **Verification.** No candidate and no source change; all measurements used shipped env-gated instrumentation and the worktree is byte-identical to `6d459c440`. |
| SCALE-035 | Rejected; no source change | Let the parallel prepass reconstruct in place in the frame workspace, deleting the per-superblock `OwnedFrameRect` plus `publish_into` plus `seal_committed_rows` triple materialisation. | **The prize is bounded at 3.4% of steady-frame CPU before any mechanism is paid for.** Exact worker time at `--limit=120` (121 coded frames, 10 threads, median of 3, phase counters): `OwnedFramePlaneRect::publish_into` is 101.3 ms over 16,200 rects, 0.84 ms per coded frame, and the sealed filter copy is 50.8 ms, about 0.42 ms per frame, against a marginal steady frame of **35.83 ms CPU** (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60`, user+sys, `/usr/bin/time -l`, median of 3, 10 threads). **Ownership finding: in place is not expressible here, and the batch geometry the task assumed does not hold.** `superblock_row_batches` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/tile/admission.rs:945`) only stops a batch from *crossing* a superblock row; a 1920-wide frame has 15 units per row and four 4-unit batches inside it, so batches within one row are column ranges of the *same* luma rows and a row-range split is not disjoint. The disjoint claim therefore has to be a 2-D rect partition, which is exactly `CurrentFrameWorkspace::rect_surfaces` (`crates/splot-recon/src/workspace.rs:1346`) — and it borrows `&mut self` for the lifetime of every rect, while the ordered commit needs `&mut CurrentFrameWorkspace` concurrently for `replay_recon_row` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/pixel_commit.rs:60`), whose general-intra and IntraBC reconstruction read neighbours across superblock boundaries. Holding both is a self-referential borrow; the only ways out are the already-closed shapes — segmented storage for the commit's own kernels (K10.9: 11.71x CfL, 2.2x deblock), a scope that fences the prepass (K10.11), or overlapping halos (K10.7). The per-block variant collapses to the same thing: a "direct" write still needs a second concurrent `&mut` into one contiguous workspace. **POC, byte-exact, measured, reverted.** The nearest safe approximation was built: the prepass publishes its own batch's rects on the worker that produced them, under a non-blocking `try_lock` of the commit mutex, with the ordered commit publishing whatever it found busy, gated off for IntraBC frames. Raw output was byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10T (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`). Mechanism, per 30-frame run: rects published early **3,088 / 2,705 / 2,287 of 3,645** at 2/8/10 threads (84.7 / 74.2 / 62.7%), and the ordered spine's publish fell from **14.9 / 19.8 / 22.1 ms to 2.3 / 4.6 / 8.0**, moving 0.41–0.49 ms per frame off the ordered chain at unchanged total work. It did not convert. Two 11-pair matrices against frozen `5c732817d` with same-source A/A nulls: 10T −0.070% (5/11) then +0.292% (6/11) against a null of −0.195%; 8T −0.189% (4/11) then −0.734% (4/11) against +0.182%; 4T −0.151% (1/5) then −0.370% (2/11) against +0.016% and +0.149%; 3T −0.248% (2/5); 2T +1.123% (4/5) then +0.231% (6/11) against +0.409% and +0.452%; 1T −0.129% (2/3) on provably identical code, since 1 thread never builds a `ScheduledFrame`. Pooled: 10T +0.11% over 22 pairs (11/22 positive), 8T −0.46% (8/22), 4T −0.32% (3/16). **Nothing is consistently positive and 4T is consistently negative.** This corroborates SCALE-024 from the other side: the spine's own execution is not the constraint — it already waits 6.2 ms/frame on its own row-gated precompute — so copy work moved onto the precompute workers lands *on* the constraint, and at two to four workers there is no spare lane at all. **Decisive control: the scheduled path already contains a zero-copy in-place shape, and it is worth exactly zero.** `supports_owned_bands` (`admission.rs:872`) lets an eligible frame reconstruct straight into `OwnedFrameRowBand` storage with no rect, no `publish_into` and no seal, its § 7.17 frontier deblocking those bands. It takes 4 of 31 coded frames at `--limit=30` and 21 of 121 at `--limit=120`; the rest are rejected as `current_frame`.
