A one-line diagnostic that forces every frame onto the copying path is byte-exact and prices the trade with the identical binary, phase counters at `--limit=120`, 10 threads, median of 3: publish 83.7 to 101.3 ms (+17.5), seal 44.7 to 50.8 (+6.1), `deblock_plane_pass` 268.7 to 244.7 (**−24.0**) over the identical 4,812 calls. **In place saves 23.6 ms of copying and pays 24.0 ms of segmented deblock: a net 0.4 ms over a 120-frame run.** Its wall A/B is null too (11 pairs, same binary, env-toggled: 10T +0.684%, 6/11, against a −0.258% null; 8T +0.040%, 5/11, against +0.014%), and marginal steady-frame CPU is 35.83 ms/frame banded against 36.50 forced-legacy, inside that metric's own noise at 3 reps. **Sub-candidates bounded and closed.** Coalescing the per-superblock rect into one rect per batch: publish moves 796 MB in 101.3 ms (7.9 GB/s) while the seal moves 752 MB of the same storage in 50.8 ms (14.8 GB/s), so the entire gap between a 256-byte strided row copy and a full-width contiguous one is 47.5 ms per 120-frame run, 0.40 ms/frame, **1.1% of steady-frame CPU and only for infinite coalescing** — a full-width rect needs one batch per superblock row, which is SCALE-011-D6's rejected whole-row tasking; four-superblock coalescing captures part of that for about 0.55%, under the resolution of the paired matrix. Skipping the rect's zero fill with a recycled buffer is worth about 9 ms per 30-frame run but has no airtight proof: `publish_into` copies the whole rect including the suffix the prepass's prefix rule left untouched, so it would rest on "every sample of every superblock is written by the prepass or the replay" — true of a correct decoder, but with a nondeterministic failure mode, unlike `CurrentFrameWorkspace::new_recycled`'s "seals whole rows upward and reads only below them". **Verification.** No source change survives; the worktree is byte-identical to `5c732817d` and the rebuilt release binary hashes identically to the frozen base (`d65e831c364a…`). |
| SCALE-036 | Rejected; fully reverted | Replace process-global pooling in the entropy and intra paths SCALE-034 named with ownership that fits the measured buffer lifetimes. | **Per-pool measurement first**, on exact `5c732817d` with env-gated take/recycle timers, per-worker attribution and pool-length sampling, one 30-frame run per width. Only one of the three named pools pays. `TRANSFORM_COEFF_QUANT_BUFFERS` (`crates/splot-decode/src/bitstream/tile_payload/coeff_state.rs:49`) runs 13,793 take/recycle pairs for **4.34 worker-ms at 1T, 33.29 at 8T and 27.64 at 10T** — an **eight-fold inflation of identical operation counts**, the SCALE-034 signature exactly. The mechanism is the best-fit scan, not the lock alone: the pool saturates its 2,560-buffer cap under the split path where 1T reaches only 409, so the take scan averages **537 entries at 8T and 317 at 10T** (7.41 M and 4.37 M entries per run) and the full-pool eviction pass runs on every recycle once saturated. `PARSED_RESIDUAL_PLANES` (`residual/pipeline/plane_execution.rs:91`) costs **3.5 worker-ms per run** over 20,622 pairs and the inter scratch pool (`prediction/inter/block/tile.rs:874`) **0.4 ms** over 1,035 — two orders under the line. The "rotating unpinned" ordered-commit scratch **is not rotating**: over 4,226 links the worker changes only 934 times at 8T and 891 at 10T, so the commit already keeps its scratch on one core for 79% of the chain and there is no migration to remove. **The task's proposed shape is refuted by its own measurement.** Worker-local free lists cannot serve the quant pool because takes and recycles are structurally on different workers — the parse worker builds the buffer, `into_quant` (`coeff_state.rs:241`) moves it into the `ReconCommand`, and `Drop for LumaCoeffBlock` (`coeff_state.rs:138`) frees it on the commit worker. At 10T one worker takes 6,594 buffers and recycles 126 while another takes none and recycles 1,769; per-worker lists would drain on the taking side and overflow on the recycling side. **What was built** kept the pool shared and made both ends O(1): buckets by `floor(log2(capacity))`, take from the first bucket whose buffers all hold `len`, recycle into the buffer's own bucket, at most 11 buckets fixed by `MAX_RETAINED_COEFF_BUFFER_CAPACITY`. Selection order, the 2,560-buffer retention cap and the keep-the-larger eviction policy were preserved, and buffer choice is allocation policy only because `zero_buffer` clears and resizes whatever it returns. Pool cost after: **0.55+0.79 worker-ms at 1T, 0.87+1.76 at 8T, 1.03+2.24 at 10T**. It was byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10T (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`) and fast: paired medians against frozen `5c732817d`, **10T -3.413%** (11/11, A/A null -0.491%), **8T -2.657%** (10/11, null -0.448%), 4T -0.992% (5/5), 2T -2.021% (5/5), 1T -0.166% (2/3), reproduced within noise by a second matrix on an earlier build (10T -3.158% 11/11, 8T -2.973% 11/11); marginal steady-frame CPU **36.25 to 35.50 ms/frame at 10T and 32.67 to 31.50 at 8T** over 10 alternating reps. `fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `check-duplication` (2619) and `check-comment-density` (248) all pass. **It was rejected on the reference-storage gate.** `pipelined_hash_decode_bounds_reference_storage_by_the_resolved_frame_delay` fails intermittently at two workers: **8 failures in 80 runs against 60 of 60 clean on exact base**, always at resolved frame delay 2, always `actual 147456` against a 135168 ceiling — serial peak plus **three** decoded frames where the gate allows two. **The mechanism is the parse lead, and a control proves it.** Rebuilding the identical bucketed pool with a `black_box` scan restoring the old take-side cost inside the lock — same structure, same lifetimes, same eviction, only the parse-side saving removed — passes the gate **40 of 40** and is still byte-exact. So no lifetime, ownership or retention change causes the extra frame: accelerating the entropy parse relative to reconstruction does, by letting parse products refresh reference slots further ahead of the reconstructions that still select them. **This bounds a whole class of future candidates.** SCALE-022 landed the depth-proportional bound as "exactly tight at (2 workers, delay 2)", and it is: the configuration has no headroom, so any parse-side speedup at two workers is rejected by an empirical bound rather than by a structural invariant. **The parse-neutral half is worth having and is priced**: the restored-scan control, whose only remaining saving is the commit-side eviction pass, measures **10T -1.351%** (11/11), **8T -2.230%** (10/11) and **2T -3.408%** (5/5) against the same frozen base while passing the storage gate 40/40 — roughly 40% of the 10T win and most of the 8T win, from the end that never accelerates parse. A shippable version of it needs O(1) eviction over the existing flat pool without touching the best-fit take scan, which the bucket structure cannot express. **Verification.** Fully reverted: the worktree is byte-identical to `5c732817d` and the rebuilt release binary hashes identically to the frozen base (`d65e831c364a…`). Suites run green on the candidate before the gate failure was found: `frame_pipelining` 8/9 (that one gate), decode and parallel `admission` 25/25 and 9/9, `scheduled` 4/4, `coeff_state` 26/26, decode `residual` 123/123. Peak RSS on the candidate was unchanged at 1T over 120 frames (135 MB) and at 10T over 30 frames (560 against 561 MB), and rose 682 to 694 MB (+1.8%) at 10T over 120 frames — pipeline fill, consistent with the same lead the gate caught. |
| SCALE-037 | Committed in `7591ce56d` and `48a41e7b7` | Derive the true structural bound on live decoded frames under the split path, then re-land SCALE-036's bucketed coefficient-quant pool on top of it. | **The extra frame was a real defect, and it was not in the pipeline.** Instrumenting `reclaim_unowned_frames` with the per-frame retention reason gives, in decoded frames, a serial live set of 8 (peak 9 = `SERIAL_PEAK`) and split peaks that carry frames tagged only `H` — storage with a second handle and no reference-slot, scheduler, emission-queue or ring owner. `SharedFrame::share` has exactly two call sites, and the one that runs here is `decode_hash_frames_pipelined` (`crates/splot-decode/src/output/hash.rs`), which hands every emitted frame's storage to a pool task with **nothing bounding how many such handles are outstanding**. Since the driver retires a frame only when its storage has a single handle, each unfinished hash task keeps one more frame charged against `max_reference_store_bytes` — so a documented memory limit held or failed by how far the pool trailed the driver, and a faster parse made it fail. **A handoff-depth sweep isolates the term exactly.** Max live frames over 6 repeats per configuration at (1,1)/(2,2)/(2,3)/(3,3)/(8,3)/(8,8)/(10,10): synchronous hashing 8/8/8/9/9/14/16, one outstanding 8/8/8/9/9/14/16, two 8/9/9/10/10/15/17, four 8/11/11/10/10/15/17, unbounded 8/11/11/10/10/15/17. The pipeline's own term is 8 + at most the resolved frame delay at every width; only the handoff term grows without a bound. **Derivation, now recorded on the test.** The peak is the live set plus the frame being admitted. Serial leaves reference slots and the display scheduler alone: `FinishSpawner::Inline` puts nothing on the ring, every frame is settled when pushed so the emission queue drains at each scheduling point, and a one-worker pool hashes inline. The split path adds the in-flight ring, which `InflightRing::reserve` holds at exactly the resolved frame delay because it harvests only at capacity; the entropy-context limit (`ring.capacity().min(3)`) and the reconstruction lane depth (`ring.capacity().min(4)`) add nothing on top, since both are at most that delay and every frame they hold reserved its slot through `reserve_pending_slot`, which pushed it on the ring, and unsettled frames are ring frames because a harvest waits for the slot to settle. The remaining term is the output handoff. **Enforcement at serial plus delay was priced and rejected.** Synchronous hashing costs +3.65% at 10 workers and one outstanding handoff +2.95% assisted / +3.56% parked at 10 and +9.3/+11.0/+12.7% at 8/4/2; the mechanism alone (bounded, never blocking) is −0.07%. Four outstanding is free at every width — 4T +0.23% against an A/A null of −0.18%, 10T +0.82% against a null of +0.93% — so the cap is four and the honest bound is `SERIAL_PEAK + (delay + 4) x DECODED_FRAME`. Measured peaks at the cap over the six gate configurations are 12/12/10/11/10/11 against ceilings of 15/16/15/16/15/16. Note the benchmark contract sets `SPLOT_DECODE_DISCARD_HASH=1`, which never reaches this code, so the cap cannot move any mission timing. **Stress.** With the cap and the derived bound: 80 of 80 clean in release and 40 of 40 in debug at the SCALE-036 stress shape (bucketed pool applied), and 40 of 40 release plus 40 of 40 debug plain. The falsification control on the same host — uncapped handoff, old `serial + delay` bound, bucketed pool — fails **4 of 80** with the SCALE-036 signature verbatim: `2 workers at frame delay 2 exceeded 135168: actual 147456`. **SCALE-036 re-landed unchanged on top.** Buckets by `floor(log2(capacity))`, take from the first bucket that guarantees the requested length, recycle into the buffer's own bucket; selection order, the 2,560-buffer retention cap and the keep-the-larger eviction are preserved. Raw output byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`), and the hash report is identical to base at all five widths. Paired medians against frozen `5c732817d`: **10T -3.279% (11/11)** against an A/A null of -0.200%, **8T -3.062% (11/11)** against +0.267%, 4T -1.645% (5/5), 2T -1.488% (5/5), 1T -0.311% (2/3). Marginal steady-frame CPU (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60`, user+sys, `/usr/bin/time -l`, 5 alternating reps): 34.00 to **32.83** ms/frame at 10 workers and 30.67 to **29.17** at 8. `fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `check-duplication` (2619), `check-comment-density` (248), `check-ai-slop` (0) and bare `cargo xtask ci` all pass (exit 0). |
| SCALE-038 | Investigated; no source change | Profile the 2/3/4-thread widths specifically, where the remaining gap is smallest and every previous profile targeted 1 or 10 threads. | **Fresh matrices on frozen `223906d7a`**, 11 alternating splot/dav2d pairs per width, `SPLOT_DECODE_DISCARD_HASH=1`, 3 warmups per side, delay equal to threads on both decoders, idle host, four matrices taken over one session: 2T **1.038 / 1.059 / 1.073 / 1.050x**, 3T **1.058 / 1.069 / 1.074 / 1.067x**, 4T **1.096 / 1.097 / 1.094x**, 0/11 at every width and every matrix. Coldest-host absolutes: 2T splot 0.5411 s / 1.005 s CPU / 1.86 cores against dav2d 0.5211 / 1.030 / 1.98; 3T 0.3954 / 1.083 / 2.74 against 0.3739 / 1.099 / 2.94; 4T 0.3128 / 1.116 / 3.57 against 0.2854 / 1.108 / 3.88. **Host-state caveat, recorded because it decides what the gap is made of**: 4T is stable to 0.3 points across matrices but 2T/3T drift by up to 3.5, and the reason is one-sided — splot's 30-frame CPU moves 1.005 to 1.072 s at 2T as the host warms while dav2d's moves only 1.062 to 1.065. Same-binary paired A/B deltas are unaffected; only cross-decoder ratios are, so a sub-3% cross-decoder claim needs matrices interleaved on one host state. **The occupancy term, by contrast, is invariant**: splot measured 1.86 / 2.74 / 3.57 cores and dav2d 1.98 / 2.94 / 3.88 in *every* matrix, so the occupancy ratio is fixed at 1.065 / 1.073 / 1.087 while the residual CPU ratio swings between 0.976 and 1.008 at 2T. Occupancy therefore accounts for the entire cold-host gap at 2T/3T and 91% of it at 4T, and it is the only term that reproduces. **Where the idle is, by output-frame region** (wall and CPU at `--limit` 1/2/3/5/10/20/30, median of 5; idle = threads x wall − CPU): at 2T splot's 30-frame idle is 78.2 core-ms — 24.4 in the first output frame, 20.2 more through output 5, 33.6 across outputs 5–30 — against dav2d's 12.2 core-ms total, **all** of it in the first five. At 4T splot is 133.8 core-ms — 77.8 / 30.4 / 25.6 by the same split — against dav2d's 34.2, again all in the first five. **So 57% of splot's 2T idle and 81% of its 4T idle is pipeline fill**, and dav2d is essentially perfectly occupied once filled. **Steady state is nearly saturated and is not what the earlier 10-thread profiles would predict.** Marginal steady frame (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60`): splot 12.892 / 9.106 / 6.996 ms wall on 24.210 / 25.851 / 26.506 ms CPU at 2/3/4T = 1.878 / 2.839 / 3.789 cores, 94/95/95% of ideal, against dav2d 1.997 / 2.994 / 4.009. A 4-second `/usr/bin/sample` of a `--limit=400` run agrees: at 2T worker-0 is 96.3% busy and worker-1 93.9%; at 4T all four are 96.0–96.3%. **The driver is not an overhead lane at two workers**: it spends 2,836 of its 2,868 samples inside `InflightRing::harvest_oldest`, of which only 107 are the assist park — the rest is `wait_with_pool_assist` executing pool jobs. Idle sites are 60% Rayon `Sleep::sleep` (the pool has no job) and 23–40% that park; both mean the pool ran dry, not that a lock is held. **Largest single fill item: the key frame's fused walk.** Frame 0 is intra, so `splittable_inter_frame` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/frame_walk.rs:274`) keeps it on the serial whole-frame walk, and `walk_ms` is 16.1–17.0 ms at 2/3/4T with every other worker idle — 2.9% / 4.1% / 5.4% of the benchmark wall, an idle charge of **1.47% / 2.76% / 3.97% of the pool**. Its post-filters add 8–10 ms more (already 2.6x parallel at 4T) and start only after the walk ends. **Steady-state CPU premium, and it is not the memory system.** Marginal steady-frame CPU at 1T is 21.77–22.52 ms against dav2d's 21.00–21.41; one N-thread process costs splot **+11.2 to +11.5% at 2 workers and +17.4 to +21.8% at 4** over its own 1T, against dav2d's +1.8 to +3.2% and +6.2 to +8.3%. N independent 1-thread processes, which hold the same cores busy with no shared software state, cost splot +1.75% at two cores and +9.58% at four while dav2d pays +3.41% and +12.57%, so **the memory-system term is comparable for both decoders — dav2d's is larger, since its multi-process shape duplicates what its threaded shape shares — and the residual is splot's own coordination**, +9.6% at two workers and +7.1% at four against dav2d's −1.6% and −5.7%. Per-phase per-item cost at `--limit=30` puts about 15% of that residual on identical items: at 4 workers, above a uniform +10% hardware term that every streaming filter and MC kernel pays alike (`filter_lr_stripe` +11%, `filter_cdef_stripe` +12%, `filter_gdf_stripe` +9%, `wiener_ns_luma` +10%, `inter_tip_prediction` +11%), only `block` +13, `intra_leaf` +14, `coeff` +11 and `commit_intra` +6 percentage points stand out — SCALE-034's signature, materially smaller after `#1147`. **Pipeline depth was never testable at these widths, and it binds at two workers.** `FrameDelay::resolve` (`crates/splot-parallel/src/frame_delay.rs:44`) clamps a requested depth to the worker count, so every frame-delay sweep at 2/3/4 threads has been a no-op: `max_in_flight` stays 2 at two threads for `--frame-delay` 2, 4 and 8 alike, and the sweep is flat to 0.3% exactly because nothing changed. Rebuilt with the clamp removed, and re-swept: **2T depth 3 is −3.546% (11/11 positive)** against a same-binary A/A null of +0.065% (3/11), depth 4 −2.910% (11/11), depth 6 −1.54%; 3T depth 4 −1.085% (8/11); 4T depth 6 −0.338% (7/11) with depths 5 and 8 neutral-to-worse. Two-thread occupancy goes 1.860 to 1.921–1.928 for +0.2 to +0.7% CPU. Raw output is byte-exact at every depth tested — (1,1), (2,2), (2,3), (2,4), (4,4), (10,10) all 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945` — and the depth-proportional reference-storage gate needs no retarget because it derives its ceiling from the *resolved* delay; with the clamp gone its `(2, 3)` case becomes a genuine two-worker depth-3 run, the exact shape SCALE-036 died on, and `frame_pipelining` passes **40 of 40** in release. Against dav2d at matched delay in one session, 2T splot(3)/dav2d(3) is **1.0334x** where splot(2)/dav2d(2) is 1.0591x on the same host state, and dav2d is flatly insensitive to `--framedelay` above its thread count (2T 0.5371 / 0.5384 / 0.5383 at framedelay 2/3/4, occupancy 1.978 throughout), so the comparison stays fair at depth 3. The clamp's own justification does not survive: `InflightRing`'s doc (`crates/splot-decode/src/pipeline/inflight.rs:386`) rests its no-strand argument on filter tasks never blocking on a slot and every driver wait running pool jobs, which holds at any depth; the clamp carries only the efficiency claim that more in-flight frames than workers "cannot buy additional concurrency", and that claim is false at two workers. **Not implemented, deliberately.** The benchmark contract pins `--frame-delay=T` and `--frame-delay=N` is documented as clamped to the thread count, so the win is reachable only by changing the resolved-depth policy (`Auto`, and the `Fixed` ceiling, to `max(threads, 3)` for two or more workers, one worker staying serial) *and* re-spelling the contract's splot arm as `--frame-delay=auto` — a protocol and user-facing-semantics decision. The candidate is also positive at 2T only, so it misses the "positive at 2/3/4T" bar. Handed to SCALE-019, which asks exactly this. **Also rejected here**: shortening `ASSIST_PARK` (`crates/splot-parallel/src/completion.rs:15`) from 100 us to 10 us measured 2T +0.072% (2/7) and 4T −0.504% (6/7), inside the host null band — the park is not a lost wakeup, since `CompletionCell::set` notifies, and it is only 3.7% of one worker at 2T. **Ceilings for the successors, as a share of the 30-frame benchmark wall at 2/3/4T**: pipeline depth 3 at two workers **3.5% / 0 / 0**; the key frame's post-filters overlapped into its own walk (SCALE-028 sealing applied to the legacy path) about **1.6% / 2.2% / 2.6%**; the key frame's walk itself split two-way into parse and reconstruct **1.5% / 2.1% / 2.6%**, rising to the full **1.5% / 2.8% / 4.0%** idle charge only if it parallelised N-way, and either shape needs intra frames admitted to the scheduled path; splot's whole threading premium brought down to dav2d's — +11.2 to +11.5% against +1.8 to +3.2% of per-frame CPU at two workers, +17.4 to +21.8% against +6.2 to +8.3% at four — **7.7% / — / 10.8%**, which is SCALE-033/034/035's closed family that `#1147` already bit into. **Verification.** No source change survives; all measurements used shipped env-gated instrumentation plus two throwaway one-line rebuilds, and the worktree is byte-identical to `223906d7a`. |
| SCALE-039 | Completed | Make `--frame-delay` mean what it says and give `Auto` a multiworker depth floor, so the two-worker pipeline SCALE-038 measured as fill-bound can actually run deeper. | **Policy.** `FrameDelay::resolve` (`crates/splot-parallel/src/frame_delay.rs`) no longer clamps a requested depth to the worker count: `Fixed(n)` is honored as given, and `Auto` resolves to 1 at one worker and `max(threads, 3)` at two or more. Nothing structural bounded the old clamp — `InflightRing` allocates nothing per depth, and its no-strand argument (filter tasks never block on a slot, every driver wait runs pool jobs) holds at any depth — so the only remaining bound on an explicit depth is the decoder's own `max_reference_store_bytes`, which fails closed. The benchmark contract's splot arm is re-spelled `--frame-delay=auto`; dav2d keeps `--framedelay T` because SCALE-038 measured it flat above its thread count. **Floor tiebreak, 3 against 4.** Only 2T and 3T can tell them apart (`max(T, 3)` and `max(T, 4)` agree from four workers up), and the candidate binary honors explicit depths, so both were measured head-to-head on one binary, 11 alternating pairs each. At 3T depth 4 beats depth 3 by **-0.553% (9/11)** and **-0.893% (10/11)** against a 3T A/A null of +0.311%. At 2T depth 4 *loses* to depth 3 by **+0.507% (1/11 faster)** and **+0.698% (2/11)** against a 2T A/A null of +0.159%. A floor of 4 therefore buys about 0.7% at three workers by giving back about 0.6% at two, on 9-to-10 of 11 pairs in both directions; the rule for this task was the depth that is better at 3T *without hurting 2T*, so the floor is **3** and 3T keeps the depth it already resolved. **Byte-exact before timing** at (1,auto), (2,auto), (3,auto), (4,auto), (8,auto), (10,auto): raw 30-frame output 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945` at all six. **Timing, auto against auto on both binaries** (the candidate's auto is deeper only at two workers; that is the change), against frozen `223906d7a`, `SPLOT_DECODE_DISCARD_HASH=1`, 3 warmups, alternating slots: **2T -3.121% (11/11 positive)** against a same-source A/A null of +0.343% (5/11); 3T +0.731% (4/11) and +0.356% (4/11) against a null of -0.260%, with the sign flipping to -0.205% when the binaries swap slots and a cand/cand null of -0.002% — 3 workers resolve to depth 3 in **both** binaries, so that spread is slot bias, not the change; 4T +0.049% (3/5) against -0.078%; 8T -0.150% (7/11) against +0.239%; 10T +0.373% (4/11) against a *larger* +0.467% null; 1T -0.038% (3 pairs) on a provably identical resolved depth of 1. A second 2T matrix on the final rebuilt binary reproduces the headline: **-2.983% (11/11)** against a cand/cand null of +0.229%. **Two-thread ratio restated**, three 11-pair sets on one host state: old contract splot(2)/dav2d(2) **1.0707x** (splot 0.5763 s / 1.069 s CPU / 1.855 cores against dav2d 0.5383 / 1.063 / 1.975); new contract splot(auto=3)/dav2d(3) **1.0397x** (splot 0.5587 / 1.072 / 1.919 against 0.5374 / 1.062 / 1.977); and splot(auto=3) against dav2d at its old framedelay 2 **1.0332x**, dav2d measuring 0.5390 against 0.5374 — flat, as SCALE-038 found. Occupancy is where the win lands: 1.855 to 1.919-1.924 cores for +0.3% CPU. Splot is still behind at two workers, 0/11 in all three sets. **Storage.** The depth-proportional gate already derives its ceiling from the *resolved* delay, so no plumbing assumed the clamp; its `(2 workers, requested 3)` case is now a genuine two-worker depth-3 run, the shape SCALE-036 died on, and the bound is unchanged at serial peak plus `(delay + 4)` decoded frames. Stress: **40/40 clean in release and 20/20 in debug**. Suites: `frame_pipelining` 9/9 release and debug, `splot-parallel` frame-delay 6/6, parallel `admission` 9/9, decode `admission` 25/25, decode `scheduled` 4/4. `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy -D warnings`, `check-duplication` and bare `cargo xtask ci` all exit 0. |
| SCALE-040 | Completed | Attack the pipeline-fill idle SCALE-038 attributed to the key frame's fused serial walk. | **The fill measurement first, on exact `fad149dca` with an env-gated timeline: every admission job's start/end and worker, the walk's start/end and phase deltas, the finish job, and the driver's per-candidate mark.** The key frame's walk is 15.47 / 15.95 / 16.31 / 15.84 / 16.04 ms at 2/3/4/8/10 workers, and the admission scheduler runs **exactly 0.00 job-core-ms** between decode start and the walk's end at every one of those widths — the other `T - 1` workers have nothing to run, by construction, because no job has been submitted yet. **Walk decomposition** (same run with `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING`, warm, 16.02 ms instrumented total): the parse is `row` 6.10 ms over 10 units, nesting `block` 4.44 over 1849 leaves (`intra_leaf` 3.86 over 1798, `coeff` 0.22, `mode_record` 0.38) and `resolve_row` 0.58; reconstruction is `commit_intra` 6.78 over the same 1798 leaves plus `commit_intrabc` 0.05 over 51; the remaining ~3.1 ms is the frame prologue — workspace allocation, tile plan derivation, the per-tile CDF subset clone and the filter-sink setup. So the walk is ~19% serial prologue, ~38% entropy parse, ~43% intra reconstruction. **Two structural facts decided the candidate.** First, `s040 walk` fires exactly **once** in a whole 30-frame pipelined run at every width above one worker: `splittable_inter_frame` takes every other frame, so the fused walk *is* the key frame. Second, this key frame carries `allow_global_intrabc`, which independently disqualifies it from `splittable_inter_frame` **and** turned `parallel_prepass` off at every width — the walk had no concurrency of any kind. **Candidate (a), starting the next frame's entropy parse during the key walk, is closed by the CDF chain, not by a gate.** Frame 1's entropy job is submitted with `entropy_dependencies`, and its terminal-CDF condition is published only when frame 0's fused walk ends; the driver is inside that walk until then as well. Measured, frame 1's entropy starts 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.2 ms after `key_done` at 2/3/4T — there is no admission slack to recover. **Candidate (b), overlapping the key frame's post-filters into its own walk, has the larger ceiling and is deferred with its blocker named.** The finish job runs 12.96 / 9.86 / 8.99 / 5.76 / 5.80 ms at 2/3/4/8/10T and starts 0.1–0.2 ms after the walk ends, so `finish_end - walk_end` is 13.1 / 9.5 / 9.0 / 5.8 / 5.8 ms = **2.4 / 2.4 / 2.9 / 2.8 / 2.8%** of the benchmark wall. The blocker is dataflow: the filter phase needs `cdef_grid`, `ccso_grid`, `gdf_grid` and the LR unit filters, and the fused walk finalizes all four only at tile end (`parser.into_output()` then `merge_tile_filter_state` / `append_lr_records`, `crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/tile.rs`). The scheduled path can filter incrementally precisely because its parse is complete before reconstruction starts. **Candidate (c) was taken and the mechanism already existed.** `run_ready_row_prepass_with_commit` pulls units from one parser task while a second task commits them in parse order; `decode_tiles` bound that coordinator to `parallel_prepass`, which also owns the shadow-surface precompute and therefore demands four workers and no global intra block copy. The two are independent: `select_prepass_entry` admits only `ReconDependency::ReferenceOnly` entries, so an intra frame precomputes nothing whatever the flag says, and the ordered commit never reorders. A new `PARSE_AHEAD_POOL_WIDTH = 2` now gates the coordinator, `parallel_prepass` still gates the shadow workspace, and an empty surface list makes the precompute a no-op — no second `CurrentFrameWorkspace` is allocated and no `publish_into` runs. One worker resolves the new flag to false and keeps the previous `run_ready_row_pipeline_serial` loop literally. **Mechanism, measured on the shipped binary** (`walk_ms`, median of 3, instrumented on both arms): 17.13 / 17.02 / 17.17 / 17.15 / 17.41 ms to **10.72 / 11.27 / 11.47 / 11.75 / 11.76** at 2/3/4/8/10 workers; uninstrumented the same walk goes 15.5 to 11.2 ms at two workers. **Byte-exact before timing** at 1/2/3/4/10 workers with `--frame-delay=auto`: raw 30-frame output 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945` at all five, on the candidate binary that was then timed. **Timing**, auto against auto, frozen base `fad149dca`, `SPLOT_DECODE_DISCARD_HASH=1`, 3 warmups, alternating slots, two independent matrices per level on two builds of the same source: 2T **-0.814% (11/11)**, **-0.925% (9/11)**, **-0.758% (9/11)** against a same-source A/A null of +0.039% (6/11); 3T **-1.549% (11/11)** and **-1.008% (10/11)** against a null of -0.084% (6/11); 4T **-2.190% (11/11)** and **-1.519% (11/11)** against a null of +0.068% (3/11); 8T **-3.487% (5/5)** and **-2.049% (5/5)** against nulls of -0.006% and +0.338%; 10T **-1.776% (5/5)**, **-2.427% (11/11)** and **-2.659% (5/5)** against nulls of -0.420% and -0.591%; 1T +0.039% and -0.080% over 3 pairs each, on a provably identical code path — `current_pool_width()` is 1 both on a one-worker pool and off-pool, so `parse_ahead` is false and the serial loop is unchanged. Pooled: 29/33 positive at 2T, 21/22 at 3T, 22/22 at 4T, 10/10 at 8T, 21/21 at 10T; nothing regresses at any level. The magnitudes track the mechanism: the walk saving is ~4.3 ms uninstrumented, which is 0.77% of the 2T wall and 2.2% of the 10T wall, and the measured results sit at or slightly beyond that as the whole downstream pipeline starts earlier. **Gates.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exit 0, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings` exit 0, `cargo xtask check-duplication` ok at budget 2619, `cargo xtask check-comment-density` ok at budget 248, bare `cargo xtask ci` **exit 0**. Suites: `frame_pipelining` 9/9 with the depth-proportional reference-storage gate untouched, decode `admission` 25/25, `scheduled` 4/4, `filters` 211/211, `ready_row` 14/14, `tile` 730/730, `splot-cli` `decoder_oracle` corpus green including `SPLOT_DECODER_ORACLE_STRICT_XPASS=1`. Recorded so it is not re-investigated: `cargo test --release -p splot-decode --lib pipeline::frame_engine::intra` fails 4 of 4 on **exact base** as well as on the candidate (`payload_source_mismatch`); the same four pass in the dev profile, which is what `cargo xtask ci` runs. |
| SCALE-040-B | Rejected; no PR | Overlap the key frame's post-filters into its own fused walk. | **Killed by its own measure-first gate; no runtime source changed.** An env-gated timeline on exact `f4e1b96c3` tagged every pool task with the admission order key of the job that spawned it (nested tasks inherit their parent's tag) and marked walk start/end, finish start/end, filter setup, the deblock prime, each of the 17 stripe windows, and the freeze. Three warm captures per width. **The tail is real but already overlapped.** `finish_end - walk_end` is 12.8 / 10.1 / 9.0 / 5.6 ms at 2/3/4/10 workers, and pool occupancy *inside that window* is 94.4 / 93.9 / 94.3% at 2T, 89.7 / 90.6 / 90.1% at 3T, 85.0 / 84.7 / 83.0% at 4T and 51.3 / 49.1 / 50.2% at 10T; frames 1 and 2 already run 6.9 / 9.0 / 10.2 / 6.9 core-ms of entropy inside it. What remains serial is deblock, not the stripes: the 10T finish body is 0.85 setup + 1.36 vertical prime + 1.98 serial per-stripe horizontal deblock and window extraction + 1.12 trailing parallel stripes + 0.13 freeze, i.e. **80% serial**; at 2T it is 0.82 / 1.88 / 1.80 / 7.99 / 0.13. **SCALE-040 consumed the capacity SCALE-040-B was going to spend.** During the walk the non-driver workers are now busy 7.5 / 8.6 / 8.5 / 8.4 core-ms running `parse_ahead`, so spare capacity during the whole walk is **2.5–4.9 core-ms at two workers** against roughly 17 core-ms of filter work, versus 13.3–13.7 at 3T, 24.5 at 4T and 91 at 10T. SCALE-040's "fits inside the walk's idle capacity above two workers" holds; its two-worker case is refuted. **Direct ceiling probe.** An env-gated build that *deletes* the key frame's whole filter phase — strictly more than any overlap can achieve, since it removes the CPU instead of relocating it, and the fused `into_filtered_frame_inner` fires exactly once in a pipelined run — measured against frozen `f4e1b96c3` in alternating pairs: 2T **-0.907% (11/11)** and **-1.120% (9/11)** against same-binary A/A nulls of +0.442% (3/11) and +0.088% (5/11); 3T **-0.702% (9/11)** against +0.679% (4/11); 4T **-0.069% (7/11)** against -0.381% (9/11); 5 pairs each at 8T **-1.185% (5/5)** and 10T **+0.485% (2/5)**. The entire prize is about 1.0% at 2T, about 0.7% at 3T, and indistinguishable from null at 4T and 10T, where the tail is fully hidden. An overlap candidate can only relocate that work into the 2.5–4.9 core-ms of two-worker walk slack, so the predicted 2T gain is **0.15–0.3%**, below the 0.8% kill line this candidate was set. **The blocker is also deeper than the four grids.** Besides `merge_tile_filter_state` / `append_lr_records`, the key frame's general-intra multiblock walk releases `active_source_blocks` and `unit_filters` only at `walk.into_output()` on its last parser step (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/tile.rs:506` and `:530`), and `into_owned_filter_setup_inner` (`crates/splot-decode/src/filters/wienerns_lr/recon.rs:464`) derives whole-frame products — the CDEF-skip and tx-skip grids, `prepare_ccso`, the coalesced LR source rows and the GDF stripe ranges — before any stripe may run. Row-readable grids alone would not unblock it, and the frame's `allow_global_intrabc` is why its parse cannot finish before its reconstruction at all. All diagnostics were reverted; bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0 on the reverted tree. |

That is the hard ceiling on any candidate that moves reconstruction work onto spare workers, and it is reached only by making the *entire* post-parse reconstruction tail free. (d) IntraBC is 51 of 1,849 leaves (2.8%) and 0.6% of the reconstruction chain. **Direct ablation control.** An env-gated build that skips every general-intra reconstruct inside the fused walk — strictly more than any offload can achieve — moves the walk **10.44 to 8.90 ms, -1.55 ms**, and 0.74 ms of that is the *parse* chain speeding up (8.03 to 7.29 ms busy) once the second worker stops competing for memory, a benefit an offload candidate cannot claim because it keeps the work on another core. **Its wall probe is contaminated and is not usable as a ceiling**: it measured 3T -1.696% (10/11) and 10T -0.867% (9/11), but deleting the key frame's pixels makes the whole 30-frame decode's content degenerate, and phase totals show it also removes about 14 worker-ms of downstream filtering (`filter_cdef_stripe` 139.2 to 137.3, `filter_lr_stripe` 134.7 to 131.8, `filter_gdf_stripe` 121.2 to 114.3, `wiener_ns_luma` 59.9 to 58.4) and 9.6 worker-ms of downstream parse (`block` 60.0 to 54.8, `intra_leaf` 36.9 to 32.5). **Arithmetic against the kill line.** At SCALE-040's measured walk-to-wall conversion of 1.0-1.55x, the 1.02 ms structural ceiling is 0.26-0.40% at 3T, 0.33-0.51% at 4T, 0.52-0.80% at 8T and 0.54-0.83% at 10T, and only if the whole tail vanished. The one neighbour-independent slice inside that tail is per-transform-block dequantization plus inverse transform, so a real candidate captures a fraction: **0.1-0.3% at 3T and 0.2-0.3% at 10T**, against same-source A/A nulls over eleven pairs each of **+0.216% at 3T** (spread -0.79 to +1.69) and **+0.285% at 10T** (spread -1.21 to +1.25). Both are under the kill line and inside the null band. **Option (i), scheduled-path admission for intra frames without IntraBC, is dead code on this stream.** `splittable_inter_frame` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/frame_walk.rs:274`) excludes the key frame twice over: its `ObuType` match admits only `LeadingTileGroup`, `RegularTileGroup`, `Switch` and `RasFrame` while the frame is `ClosedLoopKey`, and `global_intrabc_enabled` is measured **true** on it. `inspect --json` over the whole stream counts **exactly one key frame in 6,481 coded frames** (1 `ClosedLoopKey`, 6,317 `RegularTileGroup`, 164 `RegularTip`), so an intra-without-IntraBC admission would never fire and could not be verified by the mission benchmark. **Option (iii), deriving exact IntraBC source liveness, unlocks nothing.** IntraBC's only structural cost on this frame is turning `parallel_prepass` off (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/tile.rs:1803`), but `select_prepass_entry` (`tile.rs:910`) admits only `ReferenceOnly` entries and an all-intra frame has none — the empty surface list is already what runs, and lifting the flag would change no work. **Successor note.** The largest single serial item left in the walk is not reconstruction: `TileParser::new` is 1.37-1.40 ms of one-worker head before the first symbol is decoded, 13% of the walk, with every other worker idle and no dependency ahead of it to overlap with. Halving it is 0.18% at 3T and 0.37% at 10T, still under the bar, and it is first-touch cost on a cold process. **Verification.** All diagnostics reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `41afff63c` and the rebuilt release binary hashes identically to the frozen base (`d34f6f33c3ae8a00902314e0bf553d0b1f8cd634b373a7059b7f8dd0c9b7dfc1`). Raw 30-frame output re-checked on that binary at ten workers: 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`. |
| SCALE-042 | Rejected; fully reverted | Keep the ordered commit spine and its scratch on the worker that produced the row, by continuing into an already-runnable successor inline instead of returning it to the admission scheduler. | **Built, byte-exact, mechanically successful, and null on the wall; runtime and diagnostics fully reverted.** **Two of the three premises were already shipped.** Inline continuation exists on the fused-walk/`parse_ahead` path: `take_next_ordered_done` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/tile/ready_rows.rs:110`, consumed at `:185`) keeps the frontier on the finishing worker's stack, merged in `#1126` on 2026-07-28, so SCALE-034's 79%-of-4,226-links figure already included it. And the ordered scratch is already keyed to the frame, not rotated: `TileDecodeScratch.ordered` (`tile.rs:900`) is a dedicated field that `prepare_scheduled_tile` moves into `ScheduledCommit.ordered` (`tile/admission.rs:1155`) for the frame's lifetime, so its buffers never change identity — only the worker does. The pool SCALE-034's note named, `InterReconScratchPool::with_scratch` (`tile.rs:874`), serves the parallel precompute tasks, not the commit. **Step-1 census** on exact `41afff63c` with env-gated per-link diagnostics (`SPLOT_DECODE_COMMIT_LINKS`, 30-frame decode, three reps per width): 31 frames split 27 on the scheduled copying path (37 commit links each, 999 links/run) and 4 on the zero-copy `owned_bands` path (9 links each). On the scheduled path at ten workers the successor's precompute was already complete when its predecessor's commit body ended in **776-811 of 972 handoffs (79.8-83.4%)**, the next link ran on the predecessor's worker in **621-654 of 972 (63.9-67.3%)**, and **20.5-20.9% of the runnable handoffs still moved worker**. Aggregate inter-link idle was **8.00-8.67 ms/frame**, of which only **1.91-2.13 ms/frame** sat on an already-runnable successor; the rest is the spine waiting on its own precompute, SCALE-024's `spine_prewait`. At eight workers: ready 82.6-85.3%, same-worker 68.8-71.4%, runnable-handoff latency 3.08-3.82 ms/frame. On the `owned_bands` path the successor was ready **0 of 32 times**, by construction: `run_batch` fuses precompute and commit, so `prepared[i+1]` cannot be set before commit `i` ends, and the family is unreachable there without also moving precompute. **The candidate.** `ScheduledFrame::commit` claimed each link exactly once through one `AtomicBool` per batch and, after publishing a link, continued into its successor on the same worker whenever that successor's precompute had already landed, up to `COMMIT_INLINE_LIMIT = 4` consecutive links. The claim is taken *before* `committed[i]` is published, so the drain that publication triggers can never win a link this worker will run; the scheduler still spawns the job it submitted, and that job finds the claim taken and returns without touching the spine. Sixty-five lines in one file, no new abstraction, no condition change. **It did exactly what it promised.** At ten workers same-worker links **63.9-67.3% to 75.7-77.9%**, runnable handoffs that change worker **20.5-20.9% to 4.9-5.0%**, runnable-handoff latency **1.91-2.13 to 0.59-0.81 ms/frame** (52.9-57.6 ms to 16.1-21.7 ms per run); at eight workers same-worker **68.8-71.4% to 78.5-78.9%**, moved **18.1-18.9% to 2.6-3.6%**, latency **3.08-3.82 to 0.71-0.94 ms/frame**. Raw 30-frame output was byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`), and `frame_pipelining` 9/9, decode `admission` 10/10, `splot-parallel` `admission` 9/9, `scheduled` 4/4 and `tile` 730/730 passed. **The wall did not move.** Paired medians against the frozen `41afff63c` base: 10T **+0.126%** (5/11) and **-0.006%** (6/11) against same-source A/A nulls of **-0.810%** and **+0.252%**; 8T **-0.183%** (8/11) against a **-0.031%** null; 4T -0.511% (3/5) against -0.257%; 2T -0.524% (3/5) against -0.049%; 1T +0.072% (0/3) against -0.178% on a provably identical code path, since one worker never builds a `ScheduledFrame`. Nothing clears the 2%-beyond-null retention bar, and every level sits inside the +-0.3-1.0% A/A band SCALE-017 recorded for this host. **Why it cannot convert.** The ordered commit chain is not the binding recurrence. Its whole per-frame span is **13.15 ms median** (6.45-18.34, commit execution 4.48 ms of it, 37.8%) inside a frame reconstruction lifetime of about **25 ms** — four `ReconAdmissionLane` contexts at the 6.3 ms/frame benchmark pace — so the chain carries roughly **12 ms of slack**, nine times the 1.3 ms/frame of handoff latency the mechanism removed. Of the chain's own 9.0 ms median idle only 2.1 ms is reachable by any handoff change at all. Removing it shortens a chain that was already finishing early. **Relation to K10.20.** That row closed "same-frame fallthrough" on transition *gaps* — 14.620 ms aggregate across 758 transitions — not on scratch or cache locality, and it predates SCALE-013, SCALE-028 and SCALE-030, which all reshaped this chain. Re-opening it was legitimate; this measurement closes both readings at once, at 972 handoffs per run instead of 758, with the gap term now attributed and the locality term built and measured. **Verification.** All runtime and diagnostic edits reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `41afff63c` apart from these documentation rows. |
| SCALE-043 | Investigated; no source change | Build both decoders' ten-thread steady-state task timelines on the same stream and host, align them per frame, and attribute the 1.2-core occupancy difference to named structural differences. | **Method.** Env-gated task timelines on both decoders, ten threads, `--limit=90`, steady window = coded frames 30-60, three runs each. Splot: a diagnostic recorded every `TaskScope::spawn` interval (`crates/splot-parallel/src/pool.rs:38`) with its admission order key, job kind, worker, nesting depth and the slot's submit and ready instants (`crates/splot-parallel/src/admission.rs`, kinds tagged at each job body in `crates/splot-decode/src/pipeline/frame_pipeline.rs`); only depth-0 intervals are used and the trace covers essentially all worker time (7.40 busy cores in the window). Dav2d: an instrumented **copy** of the tree in the scratch directory — the maintainer's `~/Devel/dav2d` source and `build/` were never touched and are byte-identical after the session — logging every `dav2d_decode_tile_sbrow`, `filter_slice_deblock_cols/rows`, `filter_slice_cdef`, `filter_slice_lr`, `dav2d_decode_frame_init` and `dav2d_decode_frame_init_cdf` interval plus every worker park (`src/thread_task.c:838`, `:905`, `:912`, `:943`, `:951`, `:711`), with a monotonic coded-frame sequence stamped in `dav2d_task_frame_init`. Both instrumented binaries are wall-neutral (splot 0.49 s traced and untraced at `--limit=90`; dav2d 0.34 s both, three reps) and the instrumented dav2d is output-identical to the maintainer's binary (`--muxer md5` `e88dd812b981f96fb66b0fe4b30f0e58` over 90 frames at ten threads). **No timing claim in this row comes from an instrumented run**; the wall and occupancy ground truth stays the confirmed matrix above. **Configuration correction.** At the contract's `--threads 10 --framedelay 10` dav2d runs **ten** frame contexts, not four: `n_fc = umin(max_frame_delay, n_tc)` (`src/lib.c:127`) and `n_passes = 1 + (n_tc > 1) + (n_fc > 1) = 3` (`src/lib.c:252`). SCALE-019's "four at 10T" is the default-`max_frame_delay` branch the contract does not use. **The extra contexts buy dav2d nothing, and cross-frame lead distance is not the difference.** Mean frames with a running task: splot **3.62**, dav2d **3.68**; mean coded-frame index spread among active frames splot 5.73 against dav2d 3.67. **Work mix per coded frame** (core-ms, median over the window, three reps agreeing to +-4%): splot parse **3.35** / MV **3.92** / recon **15.88** / filter **12.46**, total **37.10**; dav2d parse **0.47** / MV **3.09** / recon **6.29** / filter **19.99**, total **29.87**. Splot puts **43% of the frame in reconstruction and 34% in filters; dav2d puts 21% in reconstruction and 67% in filters**. **Within-frame concurrency** (mean while the stage is live / peak): splot parse 1.00/1, MV 1.00/1, recon **1.75/5**, filter **1.92/4**; dav2d parse 1.00/1, MV 1.00/1, recon **1.00/1**, filter **1.97/4** (CDEF 1.37/2, LR 1.64/3, deblock-rows 1.00/1). Tasks per frame: splot 1 entropy, 9 resolve, 37 precompute, 37 commit, 10 frontier, 17 stripe; dav2d 9 entropy, 9 MV-resolution, 9 reconstruction and 17 each of deblock-cols, deblock-rows, CDEF and LR, on one tile. **Supply is the visible difference.** Mean live `(frame, stage)` chains: **dav2d 9.6-10.5 against splot 6.7**. Dav2d's supply exceeds the pool width, so its idle is **0.93-1.01 cores** of short parks (960-970 parks per window, median 74-80 us, p90 324-344 us); splot's supply is under ten for about 80% of the window and its idle is **2.51-2.64 cores**. Splot spends **21.0% of the window at three or fewer busy workers and that carries 63.2% of all its idle core-time** (113 trough runs, 3.6 per frame period, p90 943 us, max 8.03 ms); dav2d spends 1.5% there, carrying 10.7% of its idle (22 runs, p90 161 us). **Splot's idle, attributed to the releasable frontier of each dependency edge** (three reps; a chain-internal edge is capped at one releasable job per frame so a whole blocked chain does not count 37 times): `precompute <- previous frame's filter stripe` **0.51 / 0.59 / 0.56 cores (19.5 / 23.4 / 21.5%)**; the ordered commit spine, `commit <- commit/frontier of its own frame`, **0.43 / 0.42 / 0.42 cores (16%)**; `prepare <- frame N-4's recon` (the depth-4 lane gate) **0.31 / 0.31 / 0.32 cores (12%)**; `commit <- its own precompute` (SCALE-024's `spine_prewait`) **0.19 / 0.18 / 0.22**; the intra-frame recon chain `precompute <- precompute/commit of its own frame` 0.31 / 0.32 / 0.39 summed; `resolve <- frame N-2's stripe` 0.06. Nothing is queued: ready-but-unstarted jobs average **2.95 per instant** against **49.1 submitted-but-blocked**, and the median queueing wait is 0.002-0.070 ms per kind, so this is a dependency-supply problem and not a scheduler-priority one, as K10.25 already found from the other side. **The lane gate is nominal, not binding — and this names the cause of three null depth sweeps.** 86% of the per-frame `prepare` jobs (`frame_pipeline.rs:575`, gated by `ReconAdmissionLane::new(ring.capacity().min(4))`, `pipeline/mod.rs:765`) have the lane gate as their last-cleared condition (`commit@-4` 53%, `frontier@-4` 33%), blocked a median **16.1-16.6 ms**. But the frame's first real work is `resolve[0]`, submitted at the end of `prepare` and itself blocked a median **3.9-4.6 ms** on the motion bands and stripes of frames N-1..N-3, so admitting earlier only lengthens dead time. Lane residency prepare-to-finish is **20.8-21.0 ms, of which 6.4-7.5 ms (31-36%) is dead time with no task of that frame running**, 4.2-4.8 ms of it the prepare-to-`resolve[0]` gap. That is the mechanism behind SCALE-023's "L/D near-invariant", SCALE-011-A3, and SCALE-028 slice 4's null depth-5 result: **the depth family is now closed with a named cause rather than three measured nulls.** **The reference gate is already exact and is not the lever.** Splot derives per-list luma-row requirements and admits on `RefFrameSlot::row_condition(need)` (`crates/splot-decode/src/prediction/inter/block/row_gate.rs:190`, bounds at `:228`), the same shape as dav2d's `lowest_px[n] + 8` (`src/thread_task.c:435`), and the whole-frame `settle` fallback counted **zero** warp, TIP and slot fallbacks on this stream. **The recurrence, measured head to head.** Dav2d's period is **3.38-3.50 ms per coded frame** and its head-of-frame cycle — reconstruction of superblock row 0, then deblock cols, deblock rows, CDEF, LR, then the next frame's first reconstruction — sums to **3.24 ms, 96% of the period**, of which only **1.42 ms is execution** and **1.81 ms is frame N+1's first reconstruction waiting on frame N's first filtered superblock row**. **Dav2d is at its physical floor.** Splot's period is **5.18-5.33 ms** and the same head cycle — `precompute[0]`, `commit[0]`, frontier, `stripe[0]`, next frame's `precompute[0]` — is only **1.88 ms**: splot's own publication recurrence is **1.7x shorter than dav2d's**, and splot runs **2.8x above it**. **What splot actually runs at.** A backward critical-path walk from frame 60's finish through last-releaser edges spans 133-153 ms of the ~205 ms window over 24-28 frames and is **83% execution, 17% waiting**. Its edges are overwhelmingly the ordered commit spine and the § 7.17 frontier chain of one frame at a time (`commit <- commit` 131 and 176 of 364 and 400 edges, `commit <- frontier` 43 and 57, `frontier <- commit` 25 and 36), hopping frames through `stripe -> precompute@+1` (16 and 13 edges). **The exact wall decomposition.** Write `C` for the strictly ordered per-frame chain work and `k = C / P` for how many frames' ordered chains run concurrently. Splot: `C = 5.85` core-ms (commit 3.68 over 37 links plus frontier 2.17 over 10), `k = 1.13`. Dav2d: `C = 7.13` core-ms (reconstruction 6.29 over 9 superblock rows plus deblock-rows 0.84 over 17), `k = 2.11`. Then `P_splot / P_dav = (C_splot / C_dav) x (k_dav / k_splot) = 0.820 x 1.867 = 1.531`, against the measured 5.18 / 3.383 = **1.531**. **The entire ten-thread steady-state wall gap is chain-overlap depth**: splot's ordered chain is 18% *shorter* than dav2d's, and it overlaps **1.87x less deeply across frames**. Equivalently, splot runs 1.81 commit chains at once at 39% density (work 3.68 over a 9.39 ms span) where dav2d runs 3.20 reconstruction chains at 58% density (6.29 over 10.84). **Ceiling arithmetic.** Splot's ten-worker work floor is **3.71 ms per coded frame** against its 5.18, so it is 28-30% above floor where dav2d is 12-15%. Raising `k` from 1.13 to **1.58** puts splot on that floor: 10T wall 0.1895 s to about **0.136 s**, which is below dav2d's 0.1525 s and is the whole remaining gap. Raising it to dav2d's 2.11 changes nothing further, because the pipeline becomes work-bound. **Verdict, stated as asked.** No scheduling change reaches this. More lanes are closed above with a cause; publication latency is closed at under 1 ms/frame by SCALE-029/030/031; the order key is closed by SCALE-024 at +-1%; handoff placement is closed by SCALE-042; the reference gate is already exact. `k` rises only by putting more of the frame's CPU somewhere that has no ordered chain, and **that is exactly what dav2d's shape does**: 56% of its frame (CDEF 5.17 plus LR 11.62 core-ms) sits in 17 per-superblock-row tasks that depend only on a 0.84 core-ms intra-frame deblock sweep and on nothing cross-frame at all, while every one of splot's 15.88 core-ms of reconstruction is behind the ordered commit spine. Moving splot's reconstruction off that spine is SCALE-011-B4, K10.9, K10.10, K10.11, K10.34 and SCALE-035 — all closed under safe Rust with one contiguous mutable workspace — so **the honest answer is that the remaining ten-thread difference is intrinsic to the superblock-row-granular three-pass design, and capturing it means rebuilding splot's per-frame pipeline around superblock-row tasks whose reconstruction writes disjoint row bands, not another scheduler candidate.** **Smallest discriminating pre-measurement if the maintainer wants that rebuild scoped before it is built**: on exact main, log per commit link the batch's exact per-list `need` in luma rows and the producing frame's published watermark at that instant, then replay the schedule with reconstruction of each superblock row treated as an independent task gated only on that `need` and on the previous row's § 7.17 frontier. If the replayed `k` does not reach 1.58 the rebuild cannot pay, and the mission's ten-thread target is unreachable on this stream. **Verification.** All splot diagnostics reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `10293339f` apart from these documentation rows and the rebuilt release binary hashes identically to the frozen base (`d34f6f33c3ae8a00902314e0bf553d0b1f8cd634b373a7059b7f8dd0c9b7dfc1`). The dav2d instrumentation lives only in a scratch copy of the tree; `~/Devel/dav2d` is untouched. |

The rebuild loses because it moves splot's 11.56 core-ms of already-fanned-out per-frame precompute onto the ordered chain, taking it from 5.08 to 17.23 core-ms, 3.4x dav2d's 7.13, while the measured per-list needs cap cross-frame overlap near 2 — dav2d's own depth. Sensitivity is flat: +-20% on the calibrated 10 us per-task overhead moves `k` by at most 0.004, the filter chain is already superblock-row-chased (0.06%), lane depth 4 to 6 is 3.6%, and a 10% reconstruction saving reaches only 0.700. Byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10T (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`), bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0 (239 fixtures, 239 must_pass), `check-duplication` at budget, 9/9 `frame_pipelining` including the depth-proportional storage gate. Head to head after the change, eleven interleaved pairs: 10T 0.1782 s against dav2d 0.1537 s, ratio **1.159x** from 1.243x; 8T 0.1862 against 0.1590, **1.171x** from 1.238x — about a third of the eight- and ten-thread gap. The gain is well under SCALE-044's -18.4% because the replay priced the steady window, where 29% of frames fall back, while the 30-frame benchmark contains only 2 IntraBC frames in 30. **Remaining fallback**: the 2 deblock-disabled frames, both banded and both in the prologue.
