Headline: **8T 1.1714 / 1.1781x and 10T 1.1703 / 1.1703x** over three sweeps (11 alternating pairs each), 1T **0.9305 / 0.9400 / 0.9394x** at 11/11, with sweep 1 identified as a cool-host control 1.1-1.6 points low at 1/2T and sweeps 2/3 agreeing to 0.1-0.4 points everywhere. **The marginal steady frame moves -12.2%** at ten threads (4.166 to 3.658 ms of wall) against -6.5% on the benchmark, at unchanged per-frame CPU (32.914 to 32.947 ms), and buys **+1.107 cores of occupancy, 7.900 to 9.007 against dav2d's 8.979** — **steady-state occupancy is now at parity and the whole steady gap is work**, 32.95 against 25.65 core-ms per frame, wall and CPU ratios agreeing to 0.3% at both widths. Re-traced steady window (frames 30-60 of `--limit=90`, three reps, dav2d terms carried from SCALE-043 on an untouched binary): `P` 5.18 to **4.14 / 3.94 / 4.07 ms**, busy cores 7.16-7.40 to **9.21-9.42**, idle 2.51-2.64 to **0.58-0.79**, `C` 5.85 to 6.46-6.82 core-ms, **`k` 1.13 to 1.61-1.68 — past SCALE-043's 1.58 go line without SCALE-044's rebuild**, and the period identity still reproduces exactly. `C` grew because sealing the filter copy is charged to the commit chain on the newly sealing frames (commit 3.68 to 4.18-4.56), which `W` absorbed at -1.6%. **The benchmark and the steady state now disagree about what binds**: at `--limit=30` the prologue (coded frames 0-5) is 30-31% of the traced pool window and carries **75-80% of its idle**, frame 0's interval alone 117-124 of 276-286 core-ms at 2.70-2.82 busy cores, while all 26 steady frames together carry 59-72. Non-pool process residue is 13.6 ms, fixed, and not a gap term (a `--limit=1` control puts splot's whole fixed cost at 24.8 ms against dav2d's 28.7). Idle-core accounting of the benchmark gap: occupancy is **71% at eight threads and 83% at ten**, CPU the rest. **Re-opened on a changed condition: SCALE-033, SCALE-034 and SCALE-035** — SCALE-043 closed the CPU side on "raise occupancy rather than delete CPU", and at parity occupancy each core-ms per frame now converts at 1/9.0 ms of wall. **Not re-opened**: SCALE-044 (its replayed `k` came from needs and per-frame work SCALE-045 did not touch), SCALE-041, SCALE-042 and the depth family (mechanisms unchanged; only their share of a 3.4x smaller idle moved). **Discovered, unrelated to this mission's throughput work**: one worker and two-or-more workers produce different output from coded frame 45 onward, deterministically, on `648d5581f` **and identically on pre-SCALE-045 `1225a5b5c`**, so it predates the fallback removal; the 30-frame gate never reached it. Tracked as SCALE-048. **Verification.** All diagnostics reverted; the rebuilt release binary hashes `b7b2680e63f6090b33fb8deb5fce3d4c90f1dbd27b36fc5e409226f1ad8aa069`, identical to the frozen base every number here was measured on. |
| SCALE-046 | Rejected; no source change | Release filter stripes incrementally on frames with no active deblock plan. | **Killed on its own sizing gate.** An env-gated census on exact `648d5581f`, one line per prepared tile, finds exactly **2 of 92 coded frames with no deblock plan** — order hints 0 and 1, coded sequence 0 and 5, both banded, **both inside the 30-frame prologue and neither in the steady window**. The ten-thread `--limit=30` timeline prices them: frame 0 is the key frame, whose interval is SCALE-041's closed pair of normatively sequential chains and which has no filter chain to overlap at all, and frame 5's whole interval already runs at **9.89-9.96 of ten busy cores** carrying 0.23-0.68 core-ms of idle. Ceiling **under 0.1 ms** on the benchmark and **exactly zero** in steady state, against an extraction-API change to `extract_ready_window`/`extract_ready_band_window`. Do not build it. |
| SCALE-012 | Open correctness task | Make deferred finish reporting happen-before in-flight harvest. | `PendingFinish::run_finish` in `crates/splot-decode/src/pipeline/inflight.rs` currently lets `FrameSlotWriter::complete` (or its failure Drop) settle the slot before writing `FinishOutcome.records` or `FinishOutcome.error`; `InflightRing::harvest_oldest` waits only for that slot and can therefore wake, observe an empty outcome, and lose recyclable records or the real filter diagnostic. Add a separate one-shot finish-report completion owned by `PendingFinish` and `InflightEntry`; publish it only after the outcome write, and make harvest wait for the report before consuming the outcome and slot. Cover success, failure, and exactly-once settlement in `crates/splot-decode/src/pipeline/inflight_tests.rs`. Keep this separate from SCALE-011 filter-seam work. |
| SCALE-049 | Investigated; no source change | Build the definitive normalized cross-decoder steady-state CPU ledger at ten threads, attribute every stage where splot exceeds dav2d by 0.5 core-ms per frame, and re-price the closed CPU families now that steady CPU converts to wall one for one. | The ledger is the table above; this row carries its method, its cross-checks and what it re-prices. **Method.** Same stage boundaries on both sides, from one rule table applied to both symbol sets: the deepest frame of each sampled stack that matches any rule names the stage, copy rules sit in the same table as the kernel rules, and bare `memmove`/`memset`/allocator leaves match nothing so they are charged to whatever called them on both sides. Shares from `/usr/bin/sample` at 1 ms over a steady window (`--limit=500`, 0.40 s of warmup then 1.10 s sampled, six reps at ten threads; `--limit=400`, 1.5 s then 4.0 s, three reps at one), blocked stacks excluded, normalized by the marginal steady frame from `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` at `--limit=120` minus `--limit=60` over 60 frames, seven alternating reps. **That denominator reproduces SCALE-047 independently**: 32.968 against 32.947 core-ms per frame for splot and 25.641 against 25.646 for dav2d, 0.06% and 0.02% apart, at 8.83 against 8.97 busy cores. Per-rep spread of a stage's share is 4.8-13.3% for every stage at or above 1 core-ms, so nothing below about 0.3 core-ms per frame is resolvable and no such claim is made. **Instrumented cross-check, dav2d.** An instrumented scratch **copy** of the dav2d tree (never the original) with an env-gated exclusive per-thread stage stack pushed at `dav2d_decode_tile_sbrow` by pass and at `filter_slice_deblock_cols`/`_rows`, `filter_slice_cdef` and `filter_slice_lr` in `src/thread_task.c`; output byte-identical to the original binary at ten threads and **wall-neutral to +0.05%** over seven pairs. Marginal steady frame, task-inclusive: LR with GDF 10.121 against the sampler's 10.961, CDEF with CCSO 4.967 against 4.098, recon pass 5.155 against prediction plus residual 4.185, MV 3.153 against 3.508, deblock 1.664 against 1.671, parse 0.356 against 0.832 — the two instruments differ only by which side of a boundary the per-task bookkeeping falls on, and **agree to 1.6% on the total** (25.415 accounted of 25.841 measured, leaving 0.426 against the sampler's 0.394 for copies and scheduling). **Instrumented cross-check, splot.** Shipped `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING` phases, no source change, +1.15% wall: filter stripes sum to 10.25 core-ms per frame against the sampler's 10.54 for deblock plus CDEF plus LR plus GDF, 2.8%. **What the ledger settles.** Splot's filters are already ahead — LR 4.205 against 8.758 and GDF 0.848 against 2.203, together **-5.9 core-ms per frame** — and the whole +7.33 gap is three stages: prediction and MC **+6.14**, publication and copies **+3.58**, MV bookkeeping **+2.19**, less the filter credit. **Attribution.** (a) Algorithmic, visible with no threading at all: at one worker prediction is 6.243 against 2.566 (**2.43x**), publication 1.484 against 0.112, MV 3.442 against 2.480, and the one-worker total is only +1.32 core-ms — so **the 1T gap is 18% of the 10T gap and 82% is parallel inflation**, splot paying +11.47 core-ms per frame from 1 to 10 workers against dav2d's +5.47. (b) Parallel inflation by stage: prediction +3.43 against +0.97, publication +2.16 against -0.05, MV +2.26 against +1.03, deblock +0.81 against +0.62, LR +1.07 against +1.53 — **the filters inflate no worse than dav2d's, and all of splot's excess inflation is on the same three stages that are already algorithmically behind**, which is SCALE-034's software-stall term seen from the work side. (c) Pure extra work: the publication stage is 3.642 against 0.061 core-ms per frame and dav2d has no analogue at all — it reconstructs straight into `f->cur.p` and filters in place through per-superblock-row line buffers (`f->lf.lr_db_line`, `tc->lf.cdef_line`, `backup_row`/`backup_row_lpf` at `~/Devel/dav2d/src/looprestoration_tmpl.c:91` and `:112`) — while splot pays rect materialisation and publication 1.32 (`OwnedFrameRect::new` 0.229, `OwnedFramePlaneRect::write_rect` 0.447, `publish_into` 0.544, `crates/splot-recon/src/workspace/owned_rect.rs`), filter stripe source windows 1.10 (`DeblockedWindow::extract` 0.347, `StripePlane::copy_from` and `copy_rows` 0.361, `CurrentFrameWorkspace::copy_rows_into` 0.141, `LrSourceWindow::materialize` 0.185, `crates/splot-decode/src/filters/source.rs`), workspace allocation and zero fill 0.52 (`CurrentFramePlane::new` 0.367, `DecodedFrame::try_new` 0.151) and **release-build validation 0.43** (`u16_samples_exceed` 0.316, `validate_write_source` 0.117, `crates/splot-recon/src/workspace.rs`). Inside prediction, warp is 2.04 against 0.78 (`warp_predict_block_prepared_into` 1.450 and `build_output` 0.542 in `crates/splot-recon/src/warp_prediction.rs` against `warp_filter_horz_neon`) and subpel with compound is about 4.2 against 1.71 (`subpel_predict_block_compound_average_2d_validated` 0.923 plus its 1.369 intermediate callee, `subpel_copy_compound_average_u16_into` 0.766, `subpel_vertical_only_into` 0.577, `crates/splot-recon/src/subpel_mc.rs`, against `prep_8tap_neon` 0.432, `prep_16bpc_neon` 0.278, `put_8tap_neon` 0.088 and `put_6tap_neon` 0.059) — both families 2.4-2.6x, on a splot side that already uses `std::simd`, plus 1.03 core-ms of per-block dispatch and thread-local scratch (`predict_compound_from_grid` 0.692, `McScratch::with_installed` through `LocalKey::with` 0.333) against dav2d's 0.148. Inside MV, **the § 7.9 temporal projection is at parity** — splot 3.27 (`project_temporal_motion_field` 1.008, `TrajectoryBand::check_intersection` 1.124, `observe_projection_at` 0.362, `MotionFieldUnits::fold_unit` 0.443, `prepare_tip_field` 0.337) against dav2d's 3.15 `dav2d_refmvs_load_tmvs` — and the excess is neighbour-grid maintenance, 1.42 (`NeighbourMvGrid::record_motion` 0.725, `mv_grid_pool::take_neighbour_mv_planes` 0.531, `record_flags` 0.166) against 0.245 (`splat_mv_c`, `dav2d_refmvs_reset_sb`). **Re-pricing the closed rows at 1 core-ms = 0.113 ms = 3.0% of steady wall** (SCALE-033/034 priced in cycles on a 110.9 Mcyc, 35.4 core-ms frame, so 1 Mcyc = 0.319 core-ms there): SCALE-033's triple materialisation 1.53 core-ms per frame is **4.6% of wall** and crosses; its 3 Mcyc implementation line was itself **2.9% of wall**, so the line was drawn an order of magnitude above the 1% bar and its three sub-candidates — rect allocation 0.26 core-ms (0.8%), second neighbour grid 0.22 (0.7%), sealed-copy workspace 0.19 (0.6%) — now stack to **2.1% together**. SCALE-034's addressable software stall, 8.4 Mcyc = 7.6% of per-frame CPU, is **7.6% of steady wall, 0.28 ms per frame, 32% of the whole steady gap**, and its largest member `commit_intra` alone is **2.9%**; that row's own reopen condition (a fresh sweep putting the term above 10 Mcyc) is not met, but its 10 Mcyc line is now worth 9% of wall and should be retired in favour of the 1% bar. SCALE-035's headline in-place prepass **stays closed on its own control, which the re-pricing does not touch**: it saves 23.6 ms of copying and pays 24.0 ms of segmented deblock over a 120-frame run, a CPU-for-CPU trade that converts to nothing at any exchange rate; its sub-candidates re-price to 1.1% (infinite rect coalescing), 0.9% (recycled rect skipping the zero fill) and 0.55% (four-superblock coalescing), the first two at the bar and the third under it. SCALE-044 and SCALE-046 do not move: one is a `k` argument and the other is prologue-only. **The three rows that cross the bar by more than 3x are the ledger's own, and they are SCALE-050, SCALE-051 and SCALE-052 in the checklist.** **Verification.** No splot source changed; the tree is docs-only against `7bad43ad2` and the rebuilt release binary hashes `95267fbff7ec17b88efdbe54e797aa68f591ca36247da5b3f0e0124efd8eb4f9`, identical to the frozen base every number here was measured on. The dav2d instrumentation lives only in a scratch copy; `~/Devel/dav2d` is untouched and its binary is the one every dav2d timing here used. |
| SCALE-050 | Committed; retention call open | Close the prediction and motion-compensation kernel gap named by the ledger above. | **Structure and touch counts are equal on both sides; the gap is lane width and code generation.** Warp is 15x8 horizontal plus 8x8 vertical taps per 8x8 section in both decoders and sub-pel is one `h + 7`-row separable intermediate in both, over a marginal steady frame of 9,851 warp sections, 3,535 two-pass sub-pel blocks and 6,753 compound plane calls. The one fixable difference: SCALE-014's `i16`-lane narrowing never reached warp, so `warp_predict_block_prepared_into::<u16>` emitted 0 `smlal`, 32 `mul`/`mla` and 48 widening instructions against `build_output`'s already-narrowed 32 `smlal`. `warp_source_lanes` plus `warp_tap_mac` fold the widening into the multiply-accumulate; marginal steady-frame CPU falls **-0.675% at one worker and -0.480% at ten, -0.155 core-ms per frame at both**, byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10 workers and 1T = 10T over 545 frames, while the 30-frame benchmark stays inside its A/A null at every width and regresses nowhere. Two sub-candidates rejected: the pre-approved thread-local-scratch deletion is worth 0.034 core-ms per frame at ten workers, not this ledger's 0.333 sampler attribution, and transposing the sheared-column warp taps regressed the kernel +1.04%. MC-stage 1T-to-10T inflation is 31% host and memory pressure, 69% splot's own threading, measured as ten independent one-worker decodes against one ten-worker decode on identical call counts.<br><br>Full histogram, disassembly counts and pair tables are in the checklist row. Retention is a maintainer call: the marginal-CPU reduction is clear and nothing regresses, but no benchmark width is consistently positive. Successors are SCALE-051 and SCALE-052. |
| SCALE-051 | Committed; retention call open | Delete the publication, filter-source-window and validation copies dav2d has no analogue for, after pricing each slice the ledger above named. | **Step 1 measured all three slices before any implementation and re-priced the row's own ceiling downward.** An env-gated per-site census of the marginal steady frame (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60`, ten threads; the frame is 3,110,400 samples) reports, in samples per frame: `validate_write_source` **2,180,139 over 10,549 calls**, `validate_prediction_range` 236,171, chroma Wiener NS source validation 265,937, `write_u16_rect` 512 and both PC-Wiener validation sites **0** — the release-build validation family is `validate_write_source` and nothing else on this stream, and at 207 samples a call its cost is as much per-call shape as scanned volume; `CurrentFramePlane::new` zero fill **6,721,920, 2.16x the whole frame**, over 8.2 calls, which is one full fill each for the filtered-output workspace and the prepass shadow; `OwnedFrameRect` fill 3,058,560 with `publish_into` 2,436,480; and `filters::source` **14,346,214, 4.61x the frame** — stripe windows 4,991,872 (workspace 3,976,576 plus segmented bands 1,015,296), CDEF seed 3,058,560, post-LR seed 1,779,840, LR padded windows 2,079,462, sealed row copy 2,436,480. **The over-copy ratio this row asked for: the stripe windows carry 1.60x the deblocked frame their filters consume**, and the entire 0.60x excess is `STRIPE_WINDOW_MARGIN = 16` plane rows duplicated between neighbouring stripes — 96 luma rows copied per 64-row stripe and 64 chroma rows per 32 — so a borrow rather than a copy is worth 3.11 M of the 4.99 M and right-sizing the margin is worth the rest. **Ablation ceilings** (marginal steady CPU, core-ms per frame, medians of 7 at ten threads and 5 at one): validation off **-0.339 / -0.135**, every workspace fill off **-0.245 / -0.109**, margin 16 to 8 **-0.080 / +0.020**, margin 16 to 4 -0.186, all three together **-0.358 / -0.273**. **The joint ceiling is 0.36 core-ms per frame, 1.1% of steady wall, not this row's projected 2.05**: the 1.10 stripe-window term needs a borrow of storage the deblock is still writing, which K10.12 and K10.32 closed, leaving only its margin. **Committed in `5d2d16b48`, the validation slice, and it captures the whole family plus the freeze scan the ablation did not cover.** `u16_samples_exceed` accumulated a compare and a cross-lane reduction and a branch per eight samples; it now accumulates a lane-wise peak over sixteen and compares once, the same predicate against the same peak, with only the error path losing an early exit it does not need because it already rescans sample by sample to name the offender. `validate_write_source` scanned row by row even when the source is tightly packed, which every `write_rect_block` caller is, so a 16x16 block ran sixteen 16-sample scans; it now takes the whole run in one pass when `row_stride_samples == rect.width()` and falls through to the unchanged per-row walk to name the offending sample. `DecodedFrame::try_new` validated each frozen plane with a scalar `fold` over 3.11 M samples per frame and now shares the same scan; the two helpers moved into `sample_range.rs` because six modules already used them and `workspace.rs` was one edit from its 2500-line hard cap, and a 1T re-measurement puts the move at -0.172 before and -0.159 core-ms per frame after, neutral inside the metric's own +-0.05. **No public boundary moves and no witness type was needed**: every entry point still validates every sample it is given before the first target write, and a packed/strided equivalence test proves the two paths produce byte-identical errors and byte-identical fail-atomic targets for an offender in each of the sixteen positions of a 4x4 block. Coverage is mutation-checked: dropping the remainder walk or the packed pre-check fails the new tests. **Measurements.** Marginal steady-frame CPU against frozen `0166449a7`: **10T 32.667 to 32.332 ms, -0.335 core-ms per frame, -1.026%** over seven alternating reps; **1T 22.286 to 22.121, -0.165, -0.739%** over five. Byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`) and 1T = 10T over all 545 output frames the decoder supports. Benchmark paired medians: 10T **-0.054%** (6/11) against a +0.137% A/A null, 8T **-0.635%** (8/11) against -0.114%, 4T -0.815% (5/5) against -0.575%, 2T -0.123% (3/5) against +0.310%, 1T **-0.375%** (3/3) against a +0.008% null — 0.44% is what a 1.0%-of-steady-wall change predicts on a benchmark whose prologue is 30% of its wall, and nothing regresses. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0, `check-duplication` at budget. **Two slices rejected on their own numbers.** *Zero fill*: splitting the two full-frame fills at one worker, where the metric resolves to +-0.05, prices the filtered-output workspace at **-0.028** and the prepass shadow at **-0.056** core-ms per frame, 0.08% and 0.17% of steady wall; the shadow half is exactly the unwritten-sample nondeterminism SCALE-035 flagged, and `new_recycled` on the filtered workspace would need a stripe-coverage guard the § 7.21 partition does not carry. *Window right-sizing*: margin 16 to 8 is **null at one worker (+0.020)** and -0.080 at ten, so what it moves is deblock-frontier lead rather than copy volume; margins 12, 10, 8, 6 and 4 are all byte-exact on this stream and margin 1 fails closed loudly, which proves the failure mode is refusal rather than corruption, but the constant's own derivation (CDEF two rows, loop restoration one, eight for a tile end that is not stripe aligned) does not obviously bound 10, and a stream carrying that geometry would fail closed. Reopen only with the per-filter row reach derived from the spec. **Verification.** The step-1 census and every ablation are reverted; the tree carries only the shipped slice, and the base binary rebuilt from `0166449a7` hashes `f8678f3cfe85c35b4b4c793991d56150edae1ca57c6e19dcbad17f5496f1d057`, identical to the frozen base every number here was measured against. |
| SCALE-052 | Committed; retention call open | Collapse the duplicated neighbour MV grid maintenance. | **Step 1 answers this row's own question both ways, and names a third term larger than either.** Env-gated phase counters over the marginal steady frame (`--limit=120` minus `--limit=60`, per-frame medians over 60 frames) split SCALE-049's sampled 1.42 core-ms into `grid_new` 0.520, `record_motion` 0.657, `replay_flag_log` 0.130 and the parse pass's own `record_flags` 0.084, **summing to 1.391 against the sampler's 1.422**. Grid constructions are **2.0 per steady frame at ten threads against 1.0 at one**, `replay_flag_log` fires 133.7 times — once per parsed superblock — and `record_flags` doubles from 452.3 calls to 904.5, while **`record_motion` stays at 452.3 at both widths**: the same motion is not recorded twice, the same flags are, and the parse grid's motion plane is allocated, zero-filled and never touched. Attribution of the 1.391: **(a) the duplicated second grid and its replay 0.390 core-ms, 28%** — the extra construction 0.260 plus the replay 0.130; **(b) per-leaf write volume 0.741, 53%**, single-written; **(c) construction and clear 0.520, 37%, of which 0.260 is (a)'s**. Volume is the record's width, not its count: 452 leaves cover all 129,600 mode-info cells of this 1080p frame and `NeighbourMotion` was **72 bytes**, so publication wrote 9.33 MB and the two constructions filled 20.7 MB per frame, against dav2d's 12-byte `refmvs_block` over a two-superblock-row ring (`src/refmvs.c`, inspiration only). **Committed in `18015109f`, two changes, and the parse/resolve separation stands**: the resolve pass still rebuilds its own flag plane from the log, because `flags_at` carries no publication gate and would otherwise read leaves the resolve frontier has not reached. First, the motion plane is sized on its first publication rather than at construction, so a grid that only publishes flags never pays a fill it never reads; the pooled allocation survives because `take_neighbour_mv_planes` clears instead of resizing, and an unsized plane reads as unpublished exactly where an all-zero one did. Second, the plane stores a **20-byte `MotionCell`** — the publishing leaf's index plus the § 7.12.2.2 sub-MVs, the only motion that varies inside one leaf — beside a per-leaf table of the constants every covered cell shared, and `get` reassembles the unchanged `NeighbourMotion` on the read side, so **no reader outside the module moves** and the occupancy test becomes whether the cell names a leaf. Motion traffic falls from 18.7 to 2.6 MB per frame and the phase census to **0.604 core-ms at ten threads and 0.254 at one, -57% and -55%**, with `mv_stack` up 0.013 for the read-side assembly; the instrumentation was reverted before the timed build. **Measurements** against frozen `4602a8c74`, on the exact shipped binary `7559cba84b0ae82430bfb3dc72e90c1d446a2c7e87e853933795a0641e27bf27`. Marginal steady-frame CPU: **10T 32.597 to 31.286 ms, -1.311 core-ms per frame, -4.02%**, seven alternating reps with disjoint ranges (base 32.011-32.689, candidate 30.538-31.620); **1T 22.255 to 21.784, -0.471, -2.12%**, five reps, also disjoint. Steady wall per frame 3.593 to 3.471 ms, -3.40%, against the ledger's 0.113 ms per core-ms. Benchmark paired ratios: **10T 0.9618x, 11/11, against an A/A null of 0.9990x; 8T 0.9632x, 11/11, against 0.9960x**; 4T 0.9712x 5/5, 2T 0.9797x 5/5, **1T 0.9837x 3/3**. An earlier build of the same source measured -1.333 and -0.429 core-ms and 0.9587x/0.9610x, so the effect reproduces across builds. Byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`) and 1T = 10T = base over all 545 output frames. `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. **What stays open**: the replay's duplicated flag plane, 0.13 core-ms, needs the resolve pass to read the parse grid, which the separation forbids until every `flags_at` probe is proven decode-order safe. |
| SCALE-053 | Investigated; no source change | Re-measure the ground-truth matrix on exact `2f6a5d1fc`, then decompose every remaining gap into prologue fill and steady state and rank what is left. | The matrix, the region decomposition and the ranking are in the confirmed-matrix section above; this row carries the method and what it settles. **Method.** Two independent 11-pair sweeps per width after a throwaway warm sweep, a third at 2/8/10T, plus a 9-rep `--limit` sweep (1..8, 10, 12, 20, 30) as a region instrument that applies identically to both decoders — `wall(L) - wall(L-1)` is the marginal cost of admitting output frame `L` and `T x dwall - dcpu` its idle core-time, and a per-frame phase counter confirms the coded-frame step behind every one of its lumps — and the marginal steady frame at 1/8/10T. **Headline: splot now wins at one and two workers** (0.9173 / 0.9206x at 11/11 twice, and 0.9872 / 0.9916 / 0.9928x at 11/11, 9/11, 9/11, a width that read 1.0059 / 1.0220x before SCALE-050/051/052) **and loses at three and above**, 1.0119 / 1.0158x at 3T, 1.0188 / 1.0155x at 4T, 1.1249 / 1.1219 / 1.1233x at 8T and 1.1194 / 1.1341 / 1.1277x at 10T, all 0/11. The three retained candidates moved 8T 1.1714/1.1781 to 1.122-1.125x, 10T 1.1703 to 1.119-1.134x, 4T 1.049 to 1.016-1.019x and 3T 1.038-1.044 to 1.012-1.016x. **The marginal steady frame is 31.216 against 25.749 core-ms at ten workers** (was 32.947 against 25.646) at 8.900 against 8.935 busy cores, so occupancy is still at parity and the excess is 5.467 core-ms per frame; the three candidates predicted 5.526 and the independent measurement reads 5.467. **At one worker the steady gap is +0.422 core-ms, 2.0%**, against SCALE-049's +1.324 at 6.6%, so **92% of the steady gap is now parallel inflation** (splot +9.849 core-ms per frame from 1 to 10 workers against dav2d's +4.804) and the algorithmic side is effectively closed. **What the region split settles.** Splot's whole-benchmark CPU is *below* dav2d's at 3/4/10T and only +1.6% at 8T, so **every remaining benchmark loss is occupancy** — 107 / 89 / 337 / 324% of the gap at 10/8/4/3T. The largest identified term is the first pyramid, coded frames 1-5, which SCALE-047 named and no measurement had covered: **26.86 ms of wall on 171.7 core-ms at 6.39 of 10 busy cores against dav2d's 16.86 ms on 159.5 at 9.46**, +10.01 ms of the 17.17 ms ten-worker gap and +8.42 of 20.20 at eight, on work within 7.6% of dav2d's at ten workers and below it at three and four. At 3T and 4T the picture inverts: output frames 8-29 are **7.27 and 4.61 ms faster than dav2d's**, and the whole gap is the fixed `--limit=1` cost (+5.07 / +4.94, larger than either gap, and a -3.98 credit at 10T) plus the fill. **Verification.** No source changed; the tree is docs-only against `2f6a5d1fc` and the rebuilt release binary hashes `7559cba84b0ae82430bfb3dc72e90c1d446a2c7e87e853933795a0641e27bf27`, identical to the frozen base every number here was measured on. |
| SCALE-054 | Rejected; no source change | Raise occupancy across the first pyramid, coded frames 1-5. | **The fill's serialization is the § 8.2 CDF chain, and it is closed by spec on this stream.** An env-gated lifecycle trace (per-frame parse, admission, precompute, commit and publication marks plus a per-worker interval union of every pool task, at 3/8/10 workers, three reps at ten) puts coded frames 1-5 at 6.56 / 6.88 / 6.99 of ten busy cores and 112-143 core-ms of idle, reproducing SCALE-053's 6.39 on a second instrument. **Frames 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 each start their parse within 0.01-0.05 ms of their predecessor's parse end** (10T rep b: starts 18.30 / 21.70 / 23.95 / 26.07 / 27.62 ms against ends 18.29 / 21.69 / 23.94 / 26.07 / 27.62), and the recorded admission-condition counts confirm the cause is exact dependency rather than scheduling: frames 1, 2 and 8 carry zero CDF/CCSO conditions and admit immediately, while 3-7 carry 1-3 and are released precisely by their producer's publish. **The chain cannot be shortened**: `tile_cols = tile_rows = 1` and `context_update_tile_id = 0` on this stream, so § 8.2's `copyCdf = ( TileNum == context_update_tile_id )` saves the CDFs at the end of the frame's only tile, which is the end of its whole parse, and § 7.5 then scales them — there is no earlier publication point, and K10.58's observation that CDFs publish only at parse end is a spec consequence rather than an implementation choice. A counter-experiment prices the chain directly: forcing the first N inter frames onto the fused walk, which publishes CDFs only after reconstruction, costs **+3.34% at N=1 frame, +4.65% at 2 and +7.61% at 5** over seven 10T reps, against an inert N=0 control at +0.03%. **The other three named shapes are refuted by the same trace.** (a) Reference dependencies are not binding: every pyramid frame's reconstruction root starts 0.01-0.05 ms after its own parse ends. (b) The lane and entropy-context ramp is null-to-negative at the kill-line width — entropy depth 3 to 4 / 6 / 10 measures -0.14% (5/9), -0.61% (6/9) and +2.73% (0/9) at ten workers but **+0.34%, +0.50% and +4.13% at eight**, and the recon lane 4 to 5 / 6 / 10 measures -0.26% (5/9), +0.32% (2/9) and +1.41% (0/9) at ten; submission was never the binding term, with `esub` leading `ep0` by 3.0-5.4 ms through frame 7. (c) Priority cannot apply: in the deep trough the pool runs **1.0-4.3 of ten cores** under a work-stealing scheduler, so there were no ready-but-unstarted jobs to reorder — the opposite of the contention SCALE-024 addressed. (d) Output coupling is outside the region: `ring.reserve` first blocks at 44.2 ms, after the pyramid closes. **What the trough actually is.** At ten workers the whole fill deficit lives in ms 15-24 (10T rep b: 2.71, 2.68, 1.00, 1.69, 2.00, 3.63, 3.60, 4.30, 4.20 busy cores), where frame 2's parse — 7.20 ms, and 7.04-9.29 ms at every width, so its size and not contention — is the only unfinished work, frame 1's whole reconstruction is just 2.5 ms, and frames 3 and later can neither parse nor reconstruct. At three workers the same region is saturated at 3.00 cores, so this is a high-width term only. **The one lever with headroom is a within-frame row-granular parse-to-reconstruct handoff** — reconstruction starting on early superblock rows while the parse continues, and unlike the fused walk it would not lengthen the CDF chain. This is exactly the asymmetry SCALE-001 recorded on the other side: dav2d runs three row-progress passes, **entropy**, MV resolution and reconstruction/filter, and one task advances one superblock row and republishes, so its entropy pass feeds reconstruction within the same frame — which is how it reaches 9.46 busy cores in a region that only ever has five frames in it, where splot's cross-frame-only overlap cannot exceed the number of parsed frames. Its ceiling is SCALE-053's -8.72 ms at 10T and -6.83 at 8T. It is a mission-scale rebuild rather than a candidate: `prepare_scheduled` consumes a whole `DeferredInterWalk`, and the smallest honest test of within-frame overlap that exists today is the fused walk, which regressed. All instrumentation and knobs are reverted; the tree is docs-only against `ea6fa49f2`. |
| SCALE-055 | Rejected; no source change | **Owner:** `/root` + one measurement agent<br><br>Price the within-frame row-granular parse-to-reconstruct handoff SCALE-054 named, with SCALE-044's replay method, before anyone scopes the rebuild. | **Dependencies:** SCALE-054; SCALE-053; SCALE-044<br><br>**Go line, stated before the replay ran: go if the replay prices >=4% of the benchmark wall at eight *and* ten workers. It prices -2.04% and -2.97%, so NO-GO.** **Method.** An env-gated trace on exact `eb5611e68`, wall-neutral at **+0.83% at ten workers and +1.17% at eight** over nine alternating pairs each, records per coded frame: the parse's crossing time at every one of its 136 superblock units (15 per superblock row, 10 frontier rows, 9 resolve and motion bands, 17 filter stripes, 37 precompute batches on the copying path and 9 on the 4 of 31 banded frames); every batch's exact per-list reference need in luma rows **named by the producing coded frame**; every resolve link's exact motion-band sources named the same way; the exact AV2 SS 8.2 CDF and CCSO producer of every parse; the frontier-link to filter-stripe release map; the stripe row geometry; and every pool task's interval. Six traces per width at `--limit=30`. **The needs are exactly row-granular and have no slack**: 761 of 761 carry a named producer, **zero** settle fallbacks, stepping exactly 128 luma rows plus the motion-compensation halo per superblock row (136 / 264 / 392 / ... on the pyramid frames), which is SCALE-044's finding on a second instrument. **Replay.** About 3,300 tasks per run are rebuilt into a DAG that holds the CDF chain, the in-flight ring gate, the three-deep entropy-context bound, the depth-four reconstruction and filter lanes, the ordered commit baton, the SS 7.17 frontier chain, the stripe filters and every measured cost at their shipped shapes, then list-scheduled on `W` workers by the pool's own order key. The handoff changes exactly one thing: frame `N`'s superblock row `r` chain - its prepare slice, resolve band, precompute batches, commit links, frontier link and stripes - becomes admissible when parse(`N`) **crosses** row `r` instead of when parse(`N`) **ends**. **Validation.** At ten workers the control reproduces the traced pool window to **-1.0 to +2.6%**, the fill region's busy cores to **-1.4 to +0.5%** and the steady period to **-2.8 to +3.4%**; at eight it is +3.0 to +5.8%, -9.8 to -4.6% and +1.4 to +9.4% - the same ten-versus-eight asymmetry SCALE-044 reported at +2.4% and +7.2%. The replayed fill occupancy, **6.42 of ten**, also reproduces SCALE-053's independently measured 6.39 from the `--limit` instrument to 0.5%, and 5.29 of eight against 5.75. **Result** (medians of six traces per width; benchmark wall from the confirmed matrix, 172.7 ms at ten workers and 178.4 at eight; the replayed window is the whole pool window and everything outside it - the key frame's fused walk and the process floor - is untouched by the candidate). Ten workers: shipped control fill 34.98 ms at 6.42 busy cores, `P` 4.685 ms, `k` 2.025; fill-only handoff 28.32 ms at 6.99, benchmark **-1.47%**, `P` 4.697, `k` 2.014; full handoff 28.22 ms at 7.12, benchmark **-2.04%**, `P` 4.740, `k` 2.017. Eight workers: control 32.62 ms at 5.29, `P` 5.219, `k` 1.517; fill-only 28.05 ms at 5.96, **-1.06%**, `P` 5.370, `k` 1.502; full 28.14 ms at 6.04, **-2.97%**, `P` 5.187, `k` 1.548. **Bundling a parse re-key** - entropy first instead of frame-major, so the newest frame's parse stops being the pool's lowest-priority job - is the best realistic shape and still misses: **-2.71% at ten and -4.39% at eight**, `k` 2.030 and 1.591. The same re-key on the shipped structure alone is +0.86% at ten and -1.49% at eight, inside SCALE-024's +-1% bound on the order key. **Sensitivity is flat.** +-20% on the calibrated 10 us per-task overhead moves ten workers to -2.56 / -2.92% and eight to -4.80 / -4.27%; dropping the cross-frame motion-band chain entirely gives -3.49 / -6.78%; reconstruction lane depth 4 to 6 gives -2.73 / -4.27%; keeping the three-deep entropy-context bound changes nothing. **The unreachable bound settles it**: admitting a frame's whole reconstruction the instant its parse *starts* - no parse gate at all, which no handoff can beat - is **-3.28% at ten workers** and -4.99% at eight, and stacking that with no motion-band chain and 20% cheaper tasks reaches -4.14% and -6.08%. Ten workers never crosses the line under any assumption. **Why it does not pay.** The handoff does compress the first pyramid - fill wall 34.98 to 28.22 ms and 6.42 to 7.12 of ten busy cores - but that is only **23% of the occupancy gap to dav2d's 9.46** (31% of the eight-worker gap to 7.68), because the pyramid becomes a **diagonal** wavefront rather than a parallel one: frame `N`'s superblock row `r` still needs frame `N-1`'s filtered luma rows through `128 (r + 1) + 8` and frame `N-1`'s motion band `r`, so the five pyramid frames overlap about one row-band apart however early their parse products arrive. And the compression only partly reaches the benchmark: the -6.4 ms lead holds through coded frame 24 and erodes to -2.5 ms by frame 31, because the handoff makes the newest frame's parse latency-critical for the first time while the frame-major order key leaves it the lowest-priority job in the pool. **The row's second hypothesis is refuted where it mattered**: steady `k` does not rise at ten workers (2.025 to 2.017) and rises only 0.03 to 0.07 at eight. **One arithmetic point the go line exposed**: SCALE-053's own eight-worker ceiling, -6.83 ms, is 3.83% of that width's benchmark, so 4% was reachable at eight only by *also* raising steady `k`, which the re-key bundle does and the handoff alone does not. **Verification.** All diagnostics reverted; the tree is docs-only against `eb5611e68` and the rebuilt release binary hashes `7559cba84b0ae82430bfb3dc72e90c1d446a2c7e87e853933795a0641e27bf27`, identical to the frozen SCALE-052 build every confirmed-matrix number was measured on. |
| SCALE-056 | Rejected; no source change | Decompose the fixed `--limit=1` startup cost on both decoders per width, name the width-dependent component, and remove it. | **The width-dependent component is dav2d's, and the sign flip is its frame-context count.** Medians of 15 on frozen `d7229940b` (binary `7559cba8...`): splot's `--limit=1` wall is 36.80 / 26.53 / 25.81 / 24.93 / 23.35 / 23.74 ms at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers against dav2d's 34.20 / 23.81 / 20.69 / 19.51 / 23.83 / 27.99, reproducing SCALE-053's +5.07 / +4.94 / -0.14 / -3.98 within 0.5 ms. Splot's is flat; dav2d's is a U. Holding dav2d at `--framedelay 1` flattens it to **17.04-19.80 ms from three workers up**, so the contract's `--framedelay max(T, 3)` costs it **+0.71 / +1.61 / +6.94 / +10.93 ms of wall at 3/4/8/10T** on a one-frame decode, with CPU 39.2-41.1 core-ms at framedelay 1 against **45.8 / 55.1 / 96.1 / 136.4** at the contract depth - nine idle frame contexts, spinning. Splot's own depth costs it +0.74 / +1.00 / +1.59 / +1.46 over `--frame-delay=1` at the same widths. SCALE-038 justified the contract on dav2d's *30-frame* wall being flat in framedelay, which it is; at `--limit=1` it is not, and that is the whole 10T credit. **Splot's side, from an env-gated in-process timeline plus a `SystemTime` handshake with the harness** (marks at main entry, after clap, around the read, after `DecodeContext::new`, after decode, at main exit; all reverted): at 3T `--limit=1` the 26.30 ms splits **2.87 exec+dyld, 0.14 clap+tracing, 1.19 input read, 0.08 pool build, 20.77 key-frame decode, 1.13 teardown**, and at 10T **2.93 / 0.13 / 1.23 / 0.19 / 18.10 / 1.31**. **Pool spawn is not the term** (0.06 ms at one worker to 0.19 at ten), nor is per-worker arena or TLS init, nor frame-delay-proportional allocation. A `SPLOT_DECODE_PROFILE_REPEATS` fit over 1/2/3 repeats agrees: the one-time term is **7.14 / 8.37 / 7.88 / 7.77 / 7.65 / 8.25 ms**, flat in width, over a per-decode 29.61 / 18.92 / 18.15 / 17.27 / 15.68 / 15.68. **SCALE-053's `--version` process floor understates it.** `--version` is 2.395 ms against dav2d's 2.232 and `/bin/echo`'s 1.716, so splot's own image init is 0.68 ms against 0.52 - but the floor misses three real terms. (a) **The whole-file read**: the CLI slurps all 9,695,232 bytes when 30 output frames need 221,017, costing 1.13-1.34 ms at every width and every limit. (b) **The whole-file container plan**: `prepare_byte_stream` parses all 6,481 IVF records before the first frame decodes, 0.61-0.97 ms against 0.045-0.072 on a 60-frame prefix. (c) **Teardown**, which is address-space reclaim at **about 11 us per resident MB** (`--limit=30`: 1.83 ms at 125.7 MB, 3.78 at 290.7, 4.43 at 340.1, 5.50 at 462.2, 6.26 at 531.9), against dav2d's 64.0 / 154.7 / 183.3 / 299.2 / 349.1 MB and about 1.7 ms at 3T. Splot's 18,238 page reclaims at 3T against dav2d's 10,076 are the same 136 MB, charged again on the way in. So splot's process cost is **2.9-3.4 ms at `--limit=1` and 5.6-7.6 at `--limit=30`**, not 2.6, and the residual +2.5 ms at 3T is the key frame's own decode, which SCALE-040/040-B/041 close. **Rejected on the stated 1.5 ms kill line at three workers.** The unreachable bound for (a) plus (b) is a byte-identical 303,929-byte prefix of the same stream (same 30-frame hash), and paired against the shipped file at `--limit=30` it measures **-1.57 ms (9/11) then -0.59 (7/11) at 3T against an A/A null of -2.03, -2.30 (11/11) then -2.72 (11/11) at 4T against a null of -0.48**, plus -2.65 (5/5) at 8T, -2.78 (11/11) then -0.52 (3/5) at 10T: **not separable from null at three workers even as a bound**. The dependency-free subset predicts 0.9-1.4 ms - an exact-size read is 0.968 ms against the shipped `Take`-wrapped 1.265 in a standalone probe (plain `File::read_to_end` 1.083), a positional parallel read reaches about 0.4 at 3T, and bounding the plan is 0.6. **Both shapes that reach the bound are maintainer decisions, so neither was taken unilaterally**: a memory-mapped input needs a third-party dependency, and a limit-bounded incremental read stops validating the stream past the limit, which is a user-facing change to the documented transactional planning property - the SCALE-038 precedent. **Successor, sized here and not claimed**: the footprint round trip. Splot pays about 11 us/MB at exit and about 1.1 us per 16 KB page on the way in for a resident set 1.6-1.9x dav2d's, and `vmmap` puts 87.1 of 126.4 MB in MALLOC_SMALL on a repeated one-frame decode - pooled row, coefficient and filter scratch, which is open task SCALE-011-G, not a startup fix. **Verification.** All probes reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `d7229940b` and the rebuilt release binary hashes `7559cba84b0ae82430bfb3dc72e90c1d446a2c7e87e853933795a0641e27bf27`, identical to the frozen base. Raw 30-frame output re-checked on it at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers: 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945` at all six, and the 545-frame hash report is identical at one and ten workers. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. |
| SCALE-057 | Rejected; no source change | Census the pooled row, coefficient, motion and filter scratch SCALE-056 sized, cut whatever the census shows is hoarding, and price the teardown and parallel-inflation channels it was suspected to drive. This is open task SCALE-011-G. | **The census finds the 87 MB exactly, and the bound test kills the row.** An env-gated per-pool census on exact `2545d30ca` reports retained bytes for all eight process-global pools at the end of decode; the totals are **23.74 / 62.07 / 68.81 / 82.66 / 86.04 MB at 1/2/4/8/10 workers** (`--limit=30`) and 90.75 at ten workers over 120 frames, which is SCALE-056's 87.1 MB of `MALLOC_SMALL` to within 1.2%. Per pool at ten workers, in MB: **neighbour MV grid 24.77** (7 of 8 grids, 3.55 MB each), **window sample 18.97** (64 of 64), **recon plane 11.87** (6 of 6), **coefficient quant 7.66** (2,560 of 2,560), **deblock grid 6.43** (4 of 4), **stripe sample 5.77** (48 of 128), **parsed residual 5.77** (at its slot cap), **recon row 4.81** (128 of 128). Seven of the eight sit at their cap from two workers up, so every one of them is grow-to-cap retention. **But the pools are only 16-27% of the resident set**, which is 125.7 / 225.8 / 281.9 / 341.4 / 478.0 / 520.7 MB at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers. The rest is structural and measured directly: at `--frame-delay=1` the footprint is nearly flat in width (125.7 at one worker to 208.8 at ten, **9.2 MB per extra worker**), and at fixed width each extra in-flight frame costs **34.7 MB at ten workers and 30.5 at two** - width-independent, so it is per-frame surfaces, about 5.6 times the frame's own 6.22 MB of visible samples, not per-worker scratch. Growth with frame count is high-water, not a leak: 30 to 545 frames at ten workers is 511.9 to 748.3 MB, sublinear throughout. **Bound test, run before any right-sizing, as the task required.** `SPLOT_DECODE_POOL_SCALE` multiplies all eight caps. At scale 0 the census reads 0.00 MB, yet peak RSS falls only **532.3 to 493.8 MB at ten workers (-38.5), 348.3 to 329.2 at four (-19.1) and 288.0 to 271.5 at three (-16.5)**: 86 MB of retained buffers returns 38 MB of peak, because peak is set by concurrent live demand and the allocator does not return the rest. At SCALE-056's 11 us per resident MB that is **0.42 ms of teardown, 0.24% of the ten-worker 30-frame wall**, under every A/A null this session measured. It is not free either: marginal steady CPU at ten workers over seven alternating pairs is **31.319 to 31.547 core-ms per frame, +0.728%**, and the 30-frame benchmark over eleven alternating pairs regresses at **10T +0.466% (drop faster 4/11) against a +0.084% null, 8T +0.586% (3/11) against +0.167%, 4T +0.859% (1/11) against +0.101%**. Four workers is CPU-neutral (-0.113%), so the retention only pays at eight and ten. **The shipped caps are already on the knee**: over five pairs at ten workers, scale 4 reads 31.272 core-ms (-0.111%), scale 1 31.306, scale 0.25 31.460 (+0.490%) and scale 0 +0.728%, so quadrupling retention buys nothing and every reduction costs. **Footprint is not the parallel-inflation driver, measured without touching source.** Deepening `--frame-delay` at ten workers from 10 to 16 to 24 raises peak RSS 673.2 to 767.0 to 915.0 MB over 120 frames (+36%) while marginal steady CPU moves 31.318 to 31.452 to 31.434 (+0.43%, +0.37%), part of which is scheduling depth rather than memory. Recovering 1% of the steady frame at that sensitivity needs about 650 MB, more than the whole footprint, so the 5.467 core-ms per frame of parallel inflation is not a working-set-pressure term. **Page reclaim is a prologue term.** Steady state adds 93 reclaims per frame at ten workers (33,538 at `--limit=30` against 41,951 at 120), about 1.5 MB of first touch per frame and roughly 0.09 core-ms, 0.3% of the steady frame; SCALE-056's 18,238-against-10,076 gap is charged once, on the way in. **No source change shipped.** All probes reverted; the worktree is byte-identical to `2545d30ca` and the rebuilt release binary hashes `7559cba84b0ae82430bfb3dc72e90c1d446a2c7e87e853933795a0641e27bf27`, identical to the frozen base and to SCALE-053's and SCALE-056's timed build. Raw 30-frame output re-checked on it at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers: 186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...` at all six, and the 545-frame hash report is identical at one and ten workers on every frame record. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. **What is left for a successor, sized here and not claimed**: the only remaining lever is the 34.7 MB each in-flight frame holds, which is the pipeline depth SCALE-039 fixed at `max(T, 3)` for wall reasons, and the per-frame surface count that K10.12, K10.32 and SCALE-035 already closed as segmented reference storage. Do not re-open pool ownership, cap right-sizing, per-pool free lists, or `madvise`-style release for this gate. |
| SCALE-058 | Committed; retention call open | Reopen SCALE-050's closure that `ext`-style window reuse cannot be expressed in safe Rust, and take the warp horizontal load shape if it can. | **The closure was right about `core::arch` and wrong about `std::simd`.** All six intrinsics the design needs (`vextq_s16`, `vmull_s16`, `vmlal_s16`, `vmlal_high_s16`, `vget_low_s16`, `vaddvq_s32`) are *safe* `fn`s on nightly-2026-07-22 — the compiler reports "call to function with `#[target_feature]`", not "call to unsafe function" — but the attribute gates them anyway, and rustc explicitly refuses to honour the baseline: "the neon target feature being enabled in the build configuration does not remove the requirement to list it in `#[target_feature]`", although `target_feature="neon"` is in `--print cfg` here. A `#[target_feature(enable = "neon")]` helper does compile its body under `forbid(unsafe_code)`, so target-feature inheritance works, but the first plain-safe caller still needs `unsafe`; the `core::arch` route is therefore closed for this workspace, permanently and for reasons unrelated to memory safety. **The portable route the closure never considered is open**: `simd_swizzle!(lo, hi, [t..t+8])` needs no attribute, is safe, and lowers to `ext.16b`. Codegen, § 7.13.3.19 uniform-column horizontal pass: before, eight overlapping unaligned loads (`ldur q` at byte offsets 0,2,4,6,8,10,12,14) feeding 14 `smlal`; after, `ldp q0, q1` plus `ext.16b` by #2/#4/#6/#8/#a/#c/#e feeding the same 14 `smlal` — dav2d's two-`ld1`-plus-seven-`ext` shape, with LLVM fusing the two loads into one paired load. Three bounds-safe window shapes were compared before choosing: loads at 0 and 8 give 7 `ext`; loads at 0 and 7, which fit the old admission exactly, degrade to 6 `tbl` plus constant-table loads; and a masked 16-lane `load_or_default` scalarises to 35 `mov.h`. The chosen shape reads one sample past the taps' own reach, so admission reserves the column after `last_col` and sections at the final column fall to the clamped `build_intermediate`, which yields identical samples; the reservation carries a mutation-checked regression test. Isolated micro-bench on the SCALE-050 histogram, 9,851 sections x 15 rows, **-8.55%**, both shapes asserted bit-identical over 512 column positions. Marginal steady CPU as two-order averages, which cancel slot bias by construction: **1T 21.803 to 21.746 and 21.912 to 21.849, mean -0.060 core-ms per frame; 10T 31.260 to 31.123 and 31.203 to 30.978, mean -0.181** — both orders negative at both widths, against same-binary A/A nulls of +0.115% at 1T and +0.881% at 10T that bound what a single order can prove. Byte-exact at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`) and 1T = 10T over all 545 output frames. The 30-frame benchmark is inside its own null at every width and regresses nowhere. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0.<br><br>Retention is a maintainer call: direction is consistent at both widths and nothing regresses, but the win is a fifth of the 0.31 core-ms stacking bar. The shape does **not** transfer to sub-pel through a runtime select over constant windows, which spills — but **SCALE-059 corrects the scope of that closure**: the full-span (`K = 8`) phase needs no select, because its `tap_start` is zero and every index is a literal, and that case is 74.6% of the stream's horizontal-filtered samples. |
| SCALE-059 | Committed; retention call open | Sweep every remaining hot kernel for the constant-offset sliding-window loads SCALE-058's `simd_swizzle!` shape fits, ship the ones that measure, and close the technique. | **The census produced a pricing rule that closes the family, and one site the rule admits.** Rule: `K` overlapping windows over one contiguous span cost `2 loads + (K - 1) ext` against `K` loads — the shape never lowers instruction count, it trades `K - 2` load-port operations for `K - 1` vector-ALU operations, so it pays only when `K` is large and the kernel is load-port rather than ALU bound. CDEF is the clean counter-example: 13 windows per output row but at most 5 from any one source row against about 78 vector ALU operations, giving 8-10 loads plus 6-8 `ext` for 13 loads, and its direction index is runtime. Full site census, blocker taxonomy and per-site core-ms are in the checklist row; the four blockers are **runtime tap offsets** (Wiener-NS, PC-Wiener, GDF, CCSO — all four compute `dy * stride + dx` at runtime, and all four are stages splot already leads dav2d on), **runtime direction** (CDEF), **window multiplicity below break-even** (CDEF, optflow gradients, IDIF), and **shapes that are not windows at all** (deblock's `lane_stride` gather/transpose, the PC-Wiener classifier's scalar feature sums, the publication copies). **SCALE-058's sub-pel closure was wrong in one specific way and is corrected here**: a runtime *select among* constant windows does spill, but the full-span case needs no select. A weighted probe puts the § 7.13.3.18 active tap span at `K = 8` for **74.6%** of horizontal-filtered output samples, and at `K = 8` the span is `[0, 8)`, so `tap_start` is zero and every window index is a literal. Shipped: a `SlideLanes` trait over `Simd<i32, {4,8,16}>` used by all four horizontal sites, with an `admits` bound carrying the whole safety argument — the second load reaches past the taps' own reach, the extra lanes are discarded by the slide, and `admits` refuses the shape unless they are readable; `SLIDE_RESERVE = 9` widens the two window slices and every non-full-span phase, 8-bit source, and short row keeps the existing pruned loop. Isolated micro-bench against a const-unrolled control that separates the swizzle from the unrolling: **-30.98% at K=8**, -26.08% at K=6. Byte-exact at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers and 1T = 10T over 545 frames. Marginal steady CPU, two-order averages: **1T -0.085 core-ms per frame** against a +0.039 A/A null, **10T -0.062** against a -0.195 null. 30-frame benchmark paired medians: **1T -0.617% (11/11)**, **2T -1.126% (11/11)**, 3T -0.420% (5/5), 4T -1.106% (5/5), **8T -1.332% (9/11)**, **10T -1.242% (8/11)** — negative at every width, against nulls of -0.025 / -0.029 / +0.277 / -0.604%. `cargo xtask ci` exit 0.<br><br>Full census, K histogram, codegen counts and pair tables are in the checklist row. **This exhausts the swizzle technique and with it the mission's in-scope kernel space**: every other site is closed on runtime offsets, runtime direction, or multiplicity below break-even, and re-opening one needs a restructure separating constant column displacement from a runtime row stride, priced at 0.02-0.09 core-ms per site. |
| SCALE-060 | Investigated; no source change | Re-measure the ground-truth matrix on exact `a17b3d7ed` after the swizzle family and declare a WIN/LOSS verdict per width. | **Two 11-pair sweeps per width at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers, plus a third deciding sweep at three and four.** Timed on the shipped release artifact: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean, whole-workspace `cargo build --release`, binary `7aa1462f878752d846094d4ebb395b737070e82f1c0235d4567fd1ab176db98f` — byte-identical to the build SCALE-059 measured, and `a17b3d7ed`'s tree is source-identical to that branch head. Method per the confirmed-matrix section: a throwaway warm sweep first, then per width 3 warmups per arm and 11 alternating pairs with the slot order rotated each pair, `SPLOT_DECODE_DISCARD_HASH=1`, `--limit=30`, wall from `perf_counter`, CPU from `RUSAGE_CHILDREN`, splot `--frame-delay=auto`, dav2d `--framedelay max(T, 3)`; host up 3 days 9 hours, one-minute load 1.30 before the first sweep and 1.42 after the last. **Sweep agreement.** Per-sweep ratios are 0.9123 / 0.9150 at 1T, 0.9797 / 0.9824 at 2T, 1.0052 / 1.0084 / 1.0085 at 3T, 1.0089 / 1.0044 / 1.0075 at 4T, 1.1190 / 1.1154 at 8T and 1.1120 / 1.1131 at 10T, so **the largest sweep-to-sweep disagreement is 0.45 points, at four workers**, and the rule that a disagreement above one point buys a third sweep never fired. The third sweep was run under the other rule instead: 3T read +0.52% in sweep 1 and 4T read +0.44% in sweep 2, both on the +-0.5% line and inside their own per-pair spread. **Verdicts, pooled over every sweep at that width: 1T WIN 0.9135x at 22/22 pairs, 2T WIN 0.9810x at 22/22, 3T LOSS 1.0076x at 1/33, 4T LOSS 1.0065x at 0/33, 8T LOSS 1.1179x at 0/22, 10T LOSS 1.1131x at 0/22.** Every width moved toward parity against the post-#1159 matrix by 0.54 / 0.84 / 0.63 / 1.06 / 0.55 / 1.37 points at 1/2/3/4/8/10T, which reproduces SCALE-059's own paired medians (-0.617 / -1.126 / -0.420 / -1.106 / -1.332 / -1.242%) to within the widths' spreads and is the swizzle family arriving on the ground-truth instrument. **The 3T and 4T calls are close and are reported as measured, not rounded to a story**: the throwaway warm sweep read 3T at 0.9966x, *below* parity, and all three timed sweeps then read it above, at 1.0052 / 1.0084 / 1.0085x — one sweep at these widths proves nothing, which is what the paired counts are for, and 1 of 33 and 0 of 33 pooled pairs are consistent losses however small the medians. **Residual gap at each lost width, and its ledger-proven cause.** Absolute gaps are +2.83 ms at 3T, +1.88 at 4T, +18.77 at 8T and +17.47 at 10T. Splitting each whole benchmark into `T x wall - CPU`, splot's total CPU is **41.5 core-ms below dav2d's at three workers, 35.4 below at four and 18.5 below at ten, and only 9.6 above at eight**, while its excess idle is 50.0 / 42.9 / 140.5 / 193.2 core-ms — **588% / 572% / 94% / 111% of the respective gaps**, and occupancy is 2.80 / 3.73 / 6.54 / 7.61 against dav2d's 2.94 / 3.88 / 7.25 / 8.59. So SCALE-053's finding is reproduced exactly one swizzle family later: **every remaining loss is occupancy, not work.** Its named terms all carry closure rows. The first-pyramid fill is the largest at every lost width (SCALE-053's region split); SCALE-054 closed every reachable shape of raising its occupancy on the AV2 § 8.2 CDF chain plus this stream's single tile at `context_update_tile_id = 0`; SCALE-055 priced its one successor with headroom, the within-frame row-granular parse-to-reconstruct handoff, at -2.04% of the ten-worker benchmark and -2.97% of the eight-worker one against a 4%-at-both-widths go line, with an unreachable no-parse-gate bound of only -3.28% at ten. The fixed `--limit=1` term that dominates the small 3T and 4T gaps is **dav2d's** `--framedelay max(T, 3)` cost and not splot's startup (SCALE-056: dav2d is flat at 17.0-19.8 ms from three workers up at framedelay 1, and the contract's depth costs it +0.71 / +1.61 ms at 3T/4T against splot's +0.74 / +1.00). The steady CPU residual behind 8T and 10T is +5.467 core-ms per frame but only +2.38 / +2.04 ms of this benchmark, is 92% parallel inflation (SCALE-034's host-and-threading term), and has every named sub-term under the 1%-of-wall stacking bar (SCALE-053 item 2). **Verification.** No source or diagnostics touched: the tree is docs-only against `a17b3d7ed`, and the timed binary is the shipped one, hash-checked before and after the sweeps.<br><br>Closed as a measurement. **Two of six widths are won, each at 22 of 22 pairs, and four remain lost with no open candidate.** The goal's separate no-1T-regression clause is carried by SCALE-059's paired 1T median of -0.617% (11/11) against its own frozen base, not by this matrix, whose absolute walls sit on a different host state and cannot compare splot to itself. The occupancy family is closed at 3/4/8/10T by SCALE-053/054/055/056 and the steady-CPU family by SCALE-049/050/051/052 plus SCALE-057/SCALE-011-G, and SCALE-059 closed the swizzle kernel space, so the ranked list is empty at every lost width. Re-measure this matrix before, not after, any future retention call, and do not quote a single sweep at 3T or 4T as a verdict. |
| SCALE-061 | Rejected; no source change | Re-lay out the per-tile CDF bank for access locality, after SCALE-018 closed symbol-primitive SIMD on the packed variable-length rows and left layout untried, and SCALE-034/049 concentrated 92% of the steady parallel inflation on `block`, `coeff`, `intra_leaf` and `commit_intra`. | **Step 1's census measured a real layout inefficiency, step 1's discriminator refuted the mechanism that would make it cost anything, and two byte-exact controls then priced the whole family at zero.** **Census.** Env-gated `SPLOT_CDF_CENSUS` hooks on `TileCdfSubset::row` and `with_row_mut` record the returned row's byte offset from its `Box<TileCdfRows>` base, plus per-site clone counters; reverted after measurement, frozen base `7aa1462f87875...` (the shipped SCALE-059/060 artifact). The bank is 48,162 B = 753 lines and takes **482,502 row accesses over `--limit=30`, 16,083 per output frame**, which reproduces SCALE-018's `read_symbol_u16` call count exactly. **347 of 753 lines are ever touched** and four fields (1,068 B) never are. Cumulative: 50% of accesses on 35 lines (2.2 KiB), 80% on 85 (5.3), 90% on 120 (7.5), **95% on 153 (9.6 KiB)**, 99% on 213 (13.3). **The hot set is not contiguous**: those 153 lines span lines 0-749, the whole bank, at **20.4% density in 62 runs**, only 13 of them four lines or longer; a 64-symbol window touches **36.6 distinct rows totalling 332 B on 28.14 distinct lines spanning 709 of 753** — **18.4% packing efficiency**. **Layout order against frequency order.** `repr(Rust)` does not use declaration order — `block.coeff` is declared last but sits at offset 5,872 inside `BlockCdfRows` and `block.read_mv` at 40,754 — so the shipped layout is rustc's size-descending order, uncorrelated with access frequency. **But that order already does the one thing a re-layout could**: it clusters every small field into a dense tail, bytes 44,848-48,162 (53 lines) carrying **24.6% of all accesses on 48 touched lines**. **And 64% of the hot bytes cannot be compacted at all**: `coeff_base` (8,000 B, 18.9% of accesses) and `coeff_base_lf` (18,480 B, 17.4%) dominate, and the AV2 § 8.3.2 coefficient q-context is their *outermost* index, so the live part is already contiguous — only q 2 and 3 occur on this stream, 99.3% and 100% of each array's hits fall in its upper two quarters, and 95% of their hits sit on 20 and 37 lines respectively. A density-ordered field re-layout therefore moves the hot-95 span only **46.9 KiB to 40.6 KiB**. **Clone accounting, the row's other question.** 32 `tile_copy` calls over 30 frames — one per coded frame, this stream being single-tile — and **zero** bank clones from `plan_tile_partition_traversal_cursor` or `consume_tile_loop_restoration_root_frontier`, which are frontier and test paths, not the decode spine. That is 1.5 MB of clone traffic for the whole run, 50 KiB per frame. **There is nothing to save by cloning only a hot bank**, and the 12 KiB of never-touched q-context quarters cannot be shared immutably: each q-context adapts independently and which ones occur is stream-dependent. **Discriminator** (marginal steady frame, `--limit=120` minus `--limit=60` over 60 frames, shipped `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING` phase totals, median of 3, core-ms per frame). `1x1T / 10x1T / 1x10T / 2x5T`: `row` 1.850 / 2.335 / 2.720 / 2.805, **`block` 1.291 / 1.620 / 1.921 / 1.988**, `coeff` 0.398 / 0.486 / 0.543 / 0.560, `intra_leaf` 0.624 / 0.776 / 0.946 / 0.967, `commit_intra` 1.139 / 1.414 / 1.723 / 1.740, `mode_ctx` 0.024 / 0.030 / 0.029 / 0.031, `mv_stack` 0.076 / 0.106 / 0.099 / 0.109. **Two readings kill the hypothesis.** 2x5T — half the workers per decode, twice the independent CDF banks, same ten workers on the host — is **neutral-to-worse** than 1x10T on every stage, so per-decode worker count is not what the inflation tracks. And 10x1T, ten fully independent banks on the identical core mix, is **cheaper** than 1x10T on every stage, so more CDF banks is cheaper, not dearer, which is the opposite of capacity or sharing pressure. The host column is uniform across every phase including `mv_stack` (+38.7%) and `mode_ctx` (+27.8%), which decode no CDF rows at all. Structurally consistent: the bank is per *tile*, this stream is single-tile, and `PARSE_AHEAD_POOL_WIDTH = 2` caps live banks at two — it is never 8- or 10-way shared. **Controls, both byte-exact by construction as pure address-space changes** and verified identical at 1T and 10T over `--limit=30` (`f593c8008db8cbef...`). (a) **Spread**: `#[repr(C)]` on `TileCdfRows`, `BlockCdfRows`, `CoeffCdfRows` and `MvCdfRows` plus a 16 KiB pad at each struct's midpoint, bank 48,162 to 113,730 B, hot set pushed from a 47 KiB span into a 111 KiB one — **10T -0.126% (6/11), 1T -0.035%**. (b) **`#[repr(C)]` declaration order**, a wholly different field order with no padding — **10T +0.368% (6/11), 1T -0.049%**. A/A null **10T -0.240% (6/11)**. All three sit in one +-0.4% band at coin-flip counts, and (b)'s sign flipped between a 7-rep sweep (-0.550%) and the 11-rep one (+0.368%). **Making the layout 2.4x worse costs nothing measurable, so making it 13% better cannot pay.** **Ceiling.** 16,083 accesses per frame at 1.10 lines each is 17.7k line touches; at a pessimal 100% L1 miss served from L2 at about 16 cycles that is 0.28 Mcyc, **0.08 core-ms per frame — 0.26% of the 31.2 core-ms ten-worker steady frame, inside the instrument's own +-0.4% null band**, and consistent with SCALE-018's shadow-pass bound of +0.30% of the 1T wall for the whole scan-and-adapt family. **Verification.** Tree is docs-only against `f48dc6e5d`; all instrumentation and both controls reverted; bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0.<br><br>Closed. **This closes SCALE-018's one named successor and the last open shape of the parallel-inflation family.** Do not retry CDF bank field ordering, cache-line alignment, hot/cold bank splitting, per-frame q-context narrowing, or clone narrowing; the bank's spatial layout is measured at zero at both one and ten workers, and its whole cache-miss budget is below the resolution of the marginal steady-CPU instrument. Reopen only for a stream whose tile count makes the bank genuinely many-way live, and only with an instrument finer than +-0.4% of the steady frame. |
| SCALE-062 | Committed; retention call open | Re-price the K10-era rows that closed without measurement and parked concrete ideas in a small ledger, priced under the old >=10%-occupancy gate where one core-ms of steady work was worth about 0.4% of wall and is now 3.0%; implement whatever survives and settle that ledger permanently. | **Calibration first, because every parked ceiling was quoted in K10-era profile samples and no conversion to the current unit existed.** A fresh `/usr/bin/sample` 1T profile on the frozen shipped base `7aa1462f...` puts `splot-worker-0` at **3001 samples = 100% of one-worker decode CPU**; normalized to the 21.37 core-ms marginal steady frame that is **1 sample = 0.00712 core-ms per frame**, so the 0.1 core-ms triage bar is **14 samples** and the 1%-of-wall stacking bar is **44**. The mapping is cross-checked against SCALE-049's own 1T stage table on three stages it was not fitted to: deblock 1.19 against 1.243, CDEF+CCSO 3.20 against 3.016, LR/wiener 3.06 against 3.137 core-ms per frame. **Triage of all 24 ledgered ideas, each checked against current code before pricing.** Eight are already discharged: rolling CDEF output and direct LR stripe writes by SCALE-016, terminal backlog-copy shortening by SCALE-030, freeze-validation and K10.33's sample-validation slice by SCALE-051, fixed-arity binary decoding by SCALE-018/026, DC-only inverse transform and sparse dequant by SCALE-017, and K10.30's exact-capacity row vectors and output-plane recycling by code that already ships (`workspace.rs:1810` is `try_reserve_exact`; `take_recon_plane_buffer` / `recycle_recon_plane_buffer` are the pool, and the residual fill is SCALE-051's measured-and-rejected 0.084). Two were built and rejected when the ledger named them: direct single-reference warp output as K10.37, private clipped-MC direct writes as K10.42. **Eleven are under the 0.1 core-ms bar on the fresh profile**: exact-capacity metadata vectors 0.021 (3 samples at `RawVecInner::finish_grow`), coefficient-container movement 0.071, general-intra kernels 0.135 aggregate with no member above 0.07, intra metadata 0.185 aggregate whose largest member K10.41 already built and reverted, prevalidated LR windows 0.078, general-intra DC / fused residual 0.064, CDEF odd-direction first-distance tap aliases 0.067 — the alias is real and confirmed on directions 1/3/5/7 in `CDEF_DIRECTIONS`, but capturing it needs a runtime direction-parity split inside an `inline(always)` kernel, which is K10.46's rejected shape — direct CfL construction under 0.036 (the `prediction.resize(n, dc)` then per-sample re-read is still there, on a leaf below five samples), and the zero-storage deblock specializations at 0.004. **One is out of scope rather than under bar**: K10.23's wider fused two-axis compound coverage was ledgered as small and is not — the fully-fused dispatch is reached only from the optflow multi-cell path, the translational and single-cell paths never probe it, and widening needs phase-match generalization plus two const-generic relaxations plus rewiring `predict_fast` into two more call sites, in the kernel family K10.15/K10.37/K10.46 all measured negative. **Two crossed the bar and were built.** K10.33's partition/frontier traversal resolves to the § 7.17 LR source-block coalescing, a 26-sample subtree whose `vertical_merge_key` is a 136-byte `([usize; 14], usize, usize)` rebuilt on every comparison inside `sort_unstable_by_key`; a lazy field-by-field comparator over nested seven-tuples is byte-exact by construction (identical `Ordering` for every pair, so pdqsort yields the identical permutation) and was built, but its micro reads **-0.018 core-ms per frame with the sign flipping between slot orders (-0.084 / +0.048) over seven reps**, so it is **dropped**: the cost is the 152-byte element movement, not the key materialization. **K10.40, static scan slices, is the one survivor and it shipped.** The § 5.20.7.30 scan is a pure function of `(w, h, class)` with 48 values, but `tx_size_scan` rebuilt one per coded transform block into a `[u16; 1024]` and returned it by value inside `CoeffOrdinaryScan`, which every consumer then read as a borrowed `&[u16]` — so each transform block paid a 2 KiB zero-init, the scan walk, and a 2 KiB struct move for a table that never varies, a cost K10.40's 12-worker-ms ceiling never counted. `coefficient_scan_slice` returns a `&'static [u16]` built by `const fn`: `TX_CLASS_VERT` is the identity permutation so every shape borrows the prefix of one shared 1024-entry table, and the packed `TX_CLASS_HORIZ` and `TX_CLASS_2D` tables carry 3600 positions each, **16.1 KiB of added read-only data**. `coefficient_scan_order` keeps its signature and error contract and copies out of the same table, so there is one source of truth and no duplicated walk; the § 7.15.3 secondary transform is the second call site and gains most per call, having built the entire `w * h` scan into a 2 KiB stack array to read at most 32 entries. **Measurements.** Byte-exact at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`) and 1T = 10T over all 545 output frames, whose report is byte-identical to the base's. Marginal steady CPU, seven reps in both slot orders: **1T -0.179 core-ms per frame (-0.821%), negative in both orders (-0.098 / -0.260)**, against an A/A null of +0.035 that is +0.035 in both orders; **10T -0.103 (-0.330%)** against a +0.139 null, negative on the mean and in order A (-0.242), with order B (+0.036) inside that width's own null whose order B is +0.261. 30-frame paired medians against a same-binary A/A null at every width: 1T **-0.473% (5/5)** against +0.106% (0/5), 2T -0.187% (4/5) against +0.363% (1/5), 3T **-0.965% (5/5)** against +0.443% (2/5), 4T -0.416% (4/5) against -0.019% (3/5), 8T **-0.839% (10/11)** against +0.328% (3/11), 10T -0.687% (7/11) against +0.069% (4/11) — **every width negative, every width beyond its own null, and the two widths splot already wins at 5/5 and 4/5**. A new test asserts all 48 tables equal an independently written spec walk and is mutation-checked against a dropped 2-D diagonal wrap, a shifted identity and a transposed horizontal nesting; a null mutation (a `min` tie-break) was discarded as non-discriminating. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0, comment density and duplication both at budget.<br><br>Maintainer decision under the 2026-07-31 stacking policy. **The K10 small ledger is settled and should not be re-opened as a family**: of 24 parked ideas, 8 were already discharged by later rows, 2 were built and rejected under their own IDs, 11 are under the 0.1 core-ms bar, 1 is out of scope, 1 was built and measured null, and 1 shipped. **The bar itself is now calibrated rather than assumed**: the dropped member was predicted at 0.07-0.11 core-ms and measured null with a sign flip, so 0.1 core-ms per frame is the real resolution of the marginal-CPU instrument on this host, and anything a fresh profile prices below it cannot be confirmed. Do not re-price these rows again without a new profile that puts one of them above 14 samples. |
