| SCALE-064 | Committed; retention call open | Implement SCALE-062's six under-bar K10 members as one bundle priced as a single candidate, testing whether a stack of measured ceilings clears an instrument resolution that no member clears alone. | **Scope against SCALE-011-K10.20.** That row forbids "a blind cross-domain scalar bundle"; this one is not blind, and the delta is stated rather than assumed: every member carries a fresh-profile ceiling from SCALE-062's calibrated triage, each is a separate byte-exact commit, and the whole is measured as one candidate, which is SCALE-062's own accepted method. **Three of six were built and three are skipped on current code.** *Built.* **Intra metadata, 0.185 ceiling** — re-measures K10.41 inside the bundle. Every tile rebuilds seven intra-metadata MI grids and fills each with its own default, and § 5.20.5 partition leaves are disjoint, so a non-intra leaf that rewrites that default over its own extent cannot change a cell; the MRL, luma-palette and Y-mode default writes are deleted with their now-dead recorders, which is 24 of the 27 bytes per MI cell the six default writes moved, the 20-byte `Option<LumaPalette>` cell dominating. The IntraBC `DC_PRED` Y-mode write is kept because it publishes an active value an SDP chroma part reads, and the joint-mode, FSC and DIP defaults are kept to hold K10.41's exact shape, leaving 3 of 27 bytes adjacent and unpriced. **General-intra kernels, 0.135 ceiling** — the § 7.13.2.9 IBP angular blend indexed both buffers by a recomputed flat index, paying three bounds checks and a multiply per sample and blocking vectorization, and now walks paired row chunks with the zone-1 weight row resolved once per row; the § 7.13.2.8 IBP DC edge fetch built its `IntraPredictionEdgeLengthMismatch` eagerly on every successful sample and is now lazy. **CfL direct construction, 0.036 ceiling** — K10.47's `prediction.resize(n, dc)` then per-sample re-read is gone; the loop clips from the single DC directly and pushes the final sample, and the AC slice is bound once. *Skipped, with the witness each was asked for.* **Prevalidated LR source windows, 0.078 — OBSOLETE, not under bar.** SCALE-051's single-pass scan already is this shape: `wiener_ns_filter_luma_block_padded_layout_into` scans each padded row once into `clean_rows`, and the consumer `filter_padded_luma_row_in_range` does not re-scan, so K10.39's premise that consumers re-scan no longer holds. The only residue is the one scan itself, and removing it needs a trust token threaded from the decoder's `LrSourceWindow` through `splot-recon`'s public Wiener NS entry points, against the standing rule that trust-boundary validation stays at the public boundary. **Coefficient-container movement, 0.071 — disproportionate.** A field-by-field size census names the shape exactly: `NonZeroCoeffBlockStart` is 128 bytes and is threaded by value through five nested `coeff_loop` geometry input structs, returning as the 152-byte `NonZeroCoeffBaseDerivedLevelPass` and the 192-byte `NonZeroCoeffOrdinaryDerivedBasePass`, about 1.66 KB moved per coded transform block un-inlined; the adjacent `ParsedResidualPlane` is about 990 bytes and `LumaTransformPartitionUnits` is an inline `[Option<T>; 5]` of 608 or 728. The fix is to thread `&mut TransformCoeffBlockState` through all five input structs plus a macro adapter, a large refactor of the hottest parse path for a sub-resolution ceiling. **CDEF odd-direction tap aliases, 0.067 — disproportionate, and the K10.46 delta is stated.** The alias is re-confirmed exactly: for `dir` in 1/3/5/7 one secondary first-distance offset equals the primary one, `sec1` at dirs 1 and 5 and `sec0` at 3 and 7, selected by `(dir >> 1) & 1`; even directions never alias. A per-direction match arm with the alias folded is indeed a different shape from K10.46's rejected carried-vector state, and it saves a load, a `cdef_pair` concat and a min/max fold rather than only a load. But the aliasing site is `cdef_filter_full_rows_paired`, an `inline(always)` kernel already instantiated across `W`, `V`, `HAS_UNAVAILABLE`, `PAD_STRIDE` and `PAD_CENTER`; folding the alias multiplies those instantiations, which is the codegen growth K10.46 measured as the failure mode in this kernel family, for the second-smallest ceiling in the bundle. **Instrument correction, and it changes the answer.** SCALE-062's marginal-CPU script runs each slot order as its own sweep, so host drift between the four sweeps lands entirely in the delta. On this host it read the candidate at **+0.019 core-ms per frame, positive in both orders**, while its own A/A null read **-0.073, negative in both orders** — a null larger than the effect and same-signed in both orders, which is drift, not slot bias. Interleaving base, candidate and the base copy inside every rep with the arm order rotated per rep puts the null where a null belongs and reverses the candidate's sign. **Measurements.** Byte-exact at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`); over all 545 output frames the candidate's report is identical at 1T and 10T and identical to the frozen base at both widths. **Marginal steady CPU, interleaved, 9 reps: 1T -0.135 core-ms per frame (-0.626%), negative in 9 of 9 reps and in all three slot rotations, against an A/A null of -0.014 (5 of 9)**; 10T +0.209 (+0.677%, 4 of 9) against a +0.230 null (3 of 9), so ten workers is inside its own null in both directions. 30-frame paired medians against a same-binary A/A null at every width: 1T **-0.478% (5/5)** against -0.108% (3/5), 2T **-0.285% (14/22)** against -0.094% (14/22), 3T -0.137% over 33 pooled pairs and -0.188% over a separate 11 (27 of 44 overall) against -0.007% and -0.073% (23 of 44), 4T -0.342% (8/11) against -0.294% (9/11), 8T -0.003% (3/5) against -0.109% (3/5), 10T -0.160% (3/5) against +0.660% (2/5). **No width regresses beyond its null and the two won widths both improve.** **The 3T flip is not demonstrated.** Its pooled paired median does not reach the -0.2% preview line, and 3T needs -0.152% merely to reach parity, so the splot-versus-dav2d 3T preview was not run; the per-sweep spread is SCALE-063's own, +0.179 / -0.166 / -0.188 / -0.668% across four sweeps. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0.<br><br>Maintainer decision under the 2026-07-31 stacking policy. The bundle is a **one-worker CPU win that the whole-benchmark instrument confirms at both won widths** and that no width contradicts. **Two methodological results outlive it.** First, the marginal-CPU instrument must interleave its arms: SCALE-062's per-order sweeps gave this candidate the wrong sign at 1T, and any future sub-0.2-core-ms call made with the old script is suspect. Second, **the K10 small ledger is now settled by construction rather than by triage** — of SCALE-062's eleven under-bar members, three are built and retained here, one is obsolete rather than small, and two are priced with the concrete refactor they would need. Do not re-open the remaining members without a profile that moves one above 14 samples; the coefficient-container census and the CDEF alias table above are the evidence a successor would start from. |
| SCALE-065 | Investigated; no source change | Re-measure the ground-truth matrix on exact `origin/main` `ea71c8b85` after the SCALE-064 bundle and answer the one question SCALE-063 and SCALE-064 both left open: does splot now beat dav2d at three workers? | **Answer: no.** Timed on the shipped release artifact: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean, whole-workspace `cargo build --release`, binary `be713c756d1c128dbe57d404fcad9efaaae373e9c1a15a52dcdde5425b494ee4` — `ea71c8b85`'s tree is source-identical to the merged SCALE-064 branch head, so the checkout touched no file and cargo confirmed the artifact fresh. Method per the confirmed-matrix section; host up 3 days 12 hours, one-minute load 1.20 before the first timed sweep and 2.95 after the last. **Verdicts, pooled over every sweep at that width: 1T WIN 0.9056x at 11/11 pairs, 2T WIN 0.9784x at 11/11, 3T LOSS 1.0017x at 21/55, 4T LOSS 1.0035x at 4/22, 8T LOSS 1.1126x at 0/11, 10T LOSS 1.0955x at 0/11.** Per-sweep 3T ratios are 0.9992 / 1.0033 / 1.0025 / 1.0018 / 1.0006 and the throwaway warm sweep read 0.9938, so a fifth of the sweeps again land below parity and a single sweep at this width still proves nothing. **The protocol's own 55 pairs cannot decide 3T, and that is arithmetic rather than opinion.** Their paired median is +0.139% with a 95% bootstrap interval of [-0.030%, +0.287%] that spans parity, 21 of 55 faster is sign-test `p` = 0.10, and the per-pair ratio spread is 0.565 points, so 55 pairs buy a 95% interval whose half-width is +-0.149% — numerically the same as the +-0.15% tie band, which means the protocol resolves this width to exactly the width of the question. **So four further 55-pair sweeps ran at three workers, and 220 more pairs decide it.** They read 0.9996 / 1.0018 / 1.0015 / 1.0024 for 89 of 220 faster (40.5%), a +0.112% median, a [+0.021%, +0.181%] interval and `p` = 0.006; pooled with the matrix sweeps, **275 pairs give a wall-median ratio of 1.00127, a paired median of +0.126%, a [+0.032%, +0.178%] interval that excludes parity, 110 of 275 faster (40.0%) and `p` = 0.001**. Three workers is therefore a **TIE-AT-PARITY on effect size and a decided loss on sign**: the gap is inside the +-0.15% band and it is consistently a gap. Four workers needs no such treatment — +0.327% median, [+0.105%, +0.495%], 4 of 22 faster, `p` = 0.004, outside the band and decided at 22 pairs. **The SCALE-064 bundle's 3T preview did not reproduce against dav2d.** It measured -0.137% and -0.188% against its own base and needed -0.152% to reach parity; the matrix moved 3T by +0.02 points, which is nothing, and 4T by -0.11. The bundle's retained value is the 1T and 2T wins it did not disturb. **Residual gap at each lost width.** Absolute gaps are +0.60 ms at 3T, +1.00 at 4T, +17.90 at 8T and +14.80 at 10T. Splitting each whole benchmark into `T x wall - CPU`, splot's total CPU is **47.0 core-ms below dav2d's at three workers, 43.0 below at four and 28.0 below at ten, and only 6.0 above at eight**, while its excess idle is 48.8 / 47.0 / 137.2 / 176.0 core-ms, and occupancy is 2.80 / 3.72 / 6.55 / 7.64 against dav2d's 2.93 / 3.88 / 7.25 / 8.55. **Every remaining loss is still occupancy, not work**, and each named term keeps its closure row: SCALE-054 and SCALE-055 for the first-pyramid fill, SCALE-056 for the fixed `--limit=1` term behind 3T and 4T, and SCALE-049 through SCALE-052 plus SCALE-057 and SCALE-011-G for the steady CPU residual behind 8T and 10T. **Verification.** No source or diagnostics touched: the tree is docs-only against `ea71c8b85` and the timed binary is the shipped one.<br><br>Closed as a measurement. **Two of six widths are won and four remain lost with no open candidate**, and the three-worker question is now answered rather than deferred. **The instrument bound is the durable result**: at three workers one 11-pair sweep resolves +-0.33%, the mandated five resolve +-0.15%, and separating a sub-0.15% effect from parity takes about 275 pairs, which is 4 minutes of decoding and was run here. Do not accept or reject a 3T candidate on 11 or 55 pairs unless its own paired median exceeds 0.15%; below that, run the 220-pair confirmation or state the result as undecided. Re-measure this matrix before, not after, any future retention call. |

Scaling SCALE-055's replay by that move gives full handoff -2.19% / -3.54%, the bundled parse re-key -2.91% / -5.23% and the unreachable no-parse-gate bound -3.52% / -5.94%, against its 4%-at-both-widths go line. **Eight workers now crosses on the bundle where it read -4.39%, and ten still never crosses under any assumption**, so the binding side and the NO-GO are both unchanged. Steady runs **5.6-7.8% above its own ten-worker work floor** against dav2d's 12-15%, so there is no scheduling headroom left to convert and steady wall is set by `W`. The marginal steady frame is 30.157 core-ms against dav2d's 25.546 at ten workers (was 31.216 against 25.749) and **21.097 against 21.351 at one, which splot now wins**, so the steady gap is 100% parallel inflation. **Stage ledger** refreshed above with a same-source LTO/no-LTO column; read it as attribution as much as work, since cross-crate inlining moves helpers onto their callers. Only the total, -0.56 core-ms, is instrument-independent and it reproduces the -0.67 measured directly. Publication and copies fell 25% to +2.34 against dav2d (SCALE-049 had +3.58), the largest named non-kernel improvement since that row; scheduling rose 22% to +2.54, the one sub-term that moved the wrong way, landing exactly on the 1%-of-wall bar inside SCALE-032's coordination bound with every shape already closed. **Verdict: every closure holds and none re-opens.** The fill keeps SCALE-054's mechanism row and SCALE-055's price row, both now measured harder; the steady residual keeps SCALE-049 through SCALE-052, SCALE-057 and SCALE-011-G, and shrank; the fixed term keeps SCALE-056 and is a credit at both lost widths. **Verification.** All diagnostics reverted; the tree is docs-only against `96e1dbe50` and the rebuilt release binary hashes `73d436e05b179f64a919bfb254887922a264e18547fd11dcb24c1aa3ab73124f`, the shipped artifact.<br><br>Closed as a measurement. **The durable result is that a loss decomposition does not survive a whole-profile change: code-generation wins land on work-bound regions and miss latency-bound ones, so the same closures can hold while the ranking's weights move 20 points.** Re-run this decomposition, not just the matrix, after any future profile or toolchain change. The largest mechanism prices at 0.05 ms and every itemised candidate sums to about 0.45 ms, so NO-GO.** **Instrument.** A light env-gated `SPLOT_PARSE_SPAN` recorder on the admission scheduler's parse job (two `Instant` reads per coded frame, no phase scopes) reproduces SCALE-068 independently: the frames 2-7 chain is **16.59-16.99 ms** against its 16.690 and frame 2's parse **6.50-6.93 ms** against its 6.829, with successor starts 0.019-0.021 ms after their predecessor's end. A second env-gated pass reports per-frame deltas of the shipped `SPLOT_DECODE_TIMING` phase counters plus five temporary sub-phases (`intra_residual`, `coeff_scan`, `coeff_block`, `res_plane`, `tree_walk`) around the general-intra residual parse, the coefficient scan walk, the per-transform-block coefficient decode, the per-plane dispatch and the partition step. Frame 2 is the only parse running at 10T while it runs, so the parse phases are uncontended; medians of three traces. **Decomposition of frame 2's parse** (ms / intervals; the sub-phase build inflates the pass by its own `Instant` pairs, so read the shares): pass `row` **5.83 / 136 units**; `block` 3.93 / 1160; `intra_leaf` **2.93 / 936 (50% of the pass)**; `intra_residual` 2.25 / 936; `res_plane` 1.72 / 1824; `coeff_block` 1.34 / 2448; **`coeff_scan` 0.53 / 1112**; intra mode-info parse (`intra_leaf` less `intra_residual`) 0.68 / 936; per-leaf plane loop 0.53 / 936; per-plane dispatch 0.38 / 1824; per-transform-block preamble (`coeff_block` less `coeff_scan`) 0.81 / 2448; `resolve_row` 0.57 / 136; `mode_record` 0.68 / 1248; `tree_walk` 0.30 / 1632; partition walk remainder about 1.03; inter `coeff` 0.32 / 14. **The headline is that the coefficient symbol walk is 9% of the pass**: frame 2 is 936 intra leaves of 1170 blocks, and 91% of its parse is the code between symbol reads. SCALE-018's shadow-pass bound on the whole scan-and-adapt family, +0.30% of the 1T wall, is 2.8 ms over 30 frames and so about **0.19 ms on this frame, which is 2.9% of it** — consistent, and the reason SCALE-018/026/061 all read null. **Structural comparison with dav2d** (`~/Devel/dav2d/src/recon_tmpl.c:356` and `:1187`, read-only). dav2d bump-allocates each transform block's coefficients out of one per-tile arena (`coef *cf = ts->frame_thread[0].cf; ts->frame_thread[0].cf += imin(tw,32)*imin(th,32)`), passes raw `&t->a->lcoef[bx4]` / `&t->l.lcoef[by4]` context pointers, returns `int eob` with `all_skip` returning `-1` before constructing anything, splats the context with `dav2d_memset_likely_pow2`, and records into a preallocated `f->frame_thread.cbi[by * b4_stride + bx]` slot. Splot returns `Result<LumaCoeffBlock, GeneralIntraResidualError>` — **88-byte value, 96-byte error** — where `LumaCoeffBlock` owns a `Vec<i32>` taken from a process-global `Mutex<QuantBufferPool>` and **zeroed to the full transform area**, with a `Drop` that returns it through the same mutex; `TransformCoeffBlockState::new` takes a second zeroed padded `level` grid; the all-zero path still builds and returns the whole 88-byte value; and the result is wrapped in a **984-byte** `ParsedResidualPlane` (`#[allow(clippy::large_enum_variant)]`, `ParsedResidualPlaneKind` 728 B from an inline `[Option<ParsedTransformUnit>; 5]`), pushed into a vector and later drained. That is the 2.2x, and it is per-block object shape, not per-symbol work. **Candidates priced against the 0.1 ms line.** (a) **Implemented and measured**: replace the process-global `Mutex<ParsedResidualPlaneRecycler>` taken twice per general-intra leaf — 1,872 acquisitions on frame 2 — with the module's own thread-local `RecycledVec` pattern (net -85 / +20 lines). Byte-exact at 1T and 10T over `--limit=30` (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`). Fill chain **16.591 to 16.691 ms**, frame 2 **6.646 to 6.630**: null, as its own ceiling predicts (1,872 x about 20 ns = **0.037 ms**, 2.7x under the line). Fully reverted. (b) Boxing the 728-byte `PartitionedLuma` variant saves about 2.2 MB of moves per frame, about 0.11 ms, and buys about 936 allocations, about 0.06 ms: **net about 0.05 ms**. (c) Per-transform-block `quant` and `level` zero-fill, 1,112 nonzero blocks at about 1.4 KB: about **0.06 ms**. (d) Hoisting the frame-and-tile-constant geometry out of `read_loop_restoration_for_call`, 408 per-plane calls per frame: about **0.04 ms**. (e) `or_u8`'s `skip`/`take` fold to a slice fold: about **0.02 ms**. (f) A specialised arity-2 path for the 44% of reads SCALE-018 histogrammed at arity 2: bounded by that row's **0.19 ms** for the whole scan-and-adapt family on this frame, with SIMD (SCALE-018) and `#[inline]` (SCALE-026) both already measured net-negative. (g) Per-frame default initialisation of the seven MI grids and the neighbour flag and motion planes over 129,600 cells: about **0.1-0.16 ms**, but it is required initialisation, so it is a design change and not a candidate. **Every item is at or below the line and they sum to about 0.45 ms against a 1 ms-per-link go line.** **Why the family cannot pay.** The parse's cost is spread across per-leaf, per-plane and per-transform-block dispatch at 0.6, 0.2 and 0.3 microseconds each; no single term is over 0.9 ms and the largest, `intra_leaf`, is a whole subsystem rather than a mechanism. Reaching 1 ms per link needs roughly a 15% parse reduction, which on this shape means adopting dav2d's per-block object model — an arena-allocated coefficient plane, an out-pointer eob, and no per-block owned residual value — across the general-intra residual path. That is a mission-scale rebuild of the same order as SCALE-054's row-granular handoff, and SCALE-055 already priced that handoff's strictly larger prize at NO-GO. **The § 7.12 resolve pass is not a way out**: `resolve_parsed_leaves` writes `NeighbourMvGrid::record_motion` and the reference-MV and warp banks that the *next* superblock's parse reads, so it is a genuine parse dependency and cannot be moved off the chain. **Instrument limits, recorded honestly.** `/usr/bin/sample` at its 1 ms floor cannot decompose a 6.8 ms parse: 40 merged benchmark runs give 17,964 exclusive samples with **no parse function above the report's own 5-sample collapse threshold**, and a 545-frame 1T run puts `read_symbol_u16` at 29 samples of 2,516. The phase counters are the resolving instrument here; the sampler only corroborates the shape (`__psynch_cvwait` 20.1%, `_platform_memmove` 6.7%, all memset-family 1.4%). **Verification.** Tree is docs-only against `a8f50ad4d`; every diagnostic and the one candidate are reverted; the rebuilt release binary hashes `73d436e05b179f64a919bfb254887922a264e18547fd11dcb24c1aa3ab73124f`, identical to the frozen artifact every confirmed-matrix number was measured on; bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. |
| SCALE-070 | Rejected; no source change | Price the per-block object-model rebuild of the general-intra residual parse path — arena coefficients, out-pointer eob, no per-block owned residual value — the one successor SCALE-069 left open, against a go line stated before any measurement: **>=1.0 ms off frame 2's parse link reproducing across traces and >=1.5% at ten workers on the screen**. | **NO-GO on both halves.** **Instrument.** SCALE-069's `SPLOT_PARSE_SPAN` recorder rebuilt independently on exact `origin/main` `6c34b34da` — two `Instant` reads per coded frame around the admission scheduler's parse job, spans buffered in memory and dumped once at the end of the stream so no stderr write lands inside a measured interval or between a parse and the successor it releases. It reproduces both predecessors: frame 2's parse **6.49-6.78 ms** against SCALE-069's 6.50-6.93, and the frames 2-7 chain **15.89-16.93 ms** against its 16.59-16.99 and SCALE-068's 16.690. The instrumented binary is wall-neutral, +0.583% at ten workers and +0.130% at eight over five alternating pairs each, inside the +0.317% and -0.599% A/A nulls those five-pair screens carry. **Method: marginal-cost pricing, because a partial arena cannot be built honestly.** The `quant` buffer crosses workers by construction — the parse worker fills it and the ordered commit drops it — so a real arena has to thread a handle through every reconstruction entry point, and a prototype that copies out of the arena instead measures strictly more work than the shipped shape. Each named object cost is therefore run **eight extra times at its own site** behind an env switch, with `core::hint::black_box` barriers so the repeat cannot be folded into the original; every arm is byte-exact at 1 and 10 workers, its report digest identical to the frozen artifact's; and the delta divided by eight is the marginal cost of the single pass a rebuild removes. Forty interleaved reps per arm with the arm order rotated each rep. **Decomposition of frame 2's 6.78 ms link**, 8x delta then one pass, quoted raw so the arithmetic is reproducible from the deltas: coefficient zero-fill of the `level`, `quant_sign` and `quant` grids **+0.469 ms, 0.059**; the pooled `quant` take and recycle through the process-global `Mutex<QuantBufferPool>` **+0.186, 0.023**; one move of the 984-byte `ParsedResidualPlane` **+0.675 per pop-and-push pair, 0.042 per move**; one move of the 88-byte `LumaCoeffBlock` through its `Result` **+0.041, 0.005**. Charging the two plane moves a parsed plane makes, **the probed family is 0.171 ms of a 6.78 ms link — 2.5% of it and 5.8x under the 1 ms line**. Subtracting that sweep's **+0.050 ms A/A null** from every arm before dividing gives 0.052 / 0.017 / 0.039 / 0.000 and a 0.147 ms total; the raw figure is quoted throughout because it is the conservative one, and the two move figures are over-prices besides, since `black_box` forces a memory round trip the real, often-elided moves do not. At chain level the same four are 0.063 / 0.013 / 0.089 / 0.000, about **0.17 ms of a 16.2 ms chain**. **Half of dav2d's `all_skip` early-out already ships here, and only half**: 1,336 of frame 2's 2,448 transform blocks return before any pool take or zero-fill, so those two costs are per-nonzero-block — but the 88-byte `LumaCoeffBlock` is still built and still wrapped in a `ParsedResidualPlane` for all 2,448, exactly as the SCALE-069 row says. The plane-move probe sits at `RecycledParsedResidualPlanes::push`, which every parsed plane goes through, so that component — the dominant one — counts every plane; the block-`Result` probe covers only the two `plane_execution.rs` call sites and **not** the partitioned-luma one at `general_intra_residual.rs:690`, so its 0.005 ms is an under-count of a component already at the resolution floor. **Prototype.** The one removal expressible without the arena handle was built and is byte-exact at 1T and 10T: the `quant` pool becomes a thread-local bucketed `QuantBufferPool` and the `ParsedResidualPlane` list recycler a thread-local one, so the parse path takes and returns both without touching a process-global mutex — the mutex-free half of the arena, real on the hot path rather than simulated. **It regresses.** Frame 2's parse reads **+0.042 ms** with the pool alone, **+0.035** with the plane lists alone and **+0.066** with both, over 40 interleaved reps each against a **-0.006** A/A null; 30-frame paired medians are **+0.467% (1/5) and +0.206% (1/5) at ten workers** against a +0.317% A/A null, and -1.973% (4/5) then +0.110% (2/5) at eight against a -0.599% (5/5) null that is wider than either reading. The mechanism is SCALE-036's verbatim: takes and recycles are structurally on different workers, so a worker-local free list drains on the parse worker and its miss path costs more than the uncontended lock it removed. **That is a negative result about worker-local recycling, not about a cross-worker arena handle**, which threads one ownership handle to the consumer and never needs storage back on the producer's free list; the arena's own verdict rests on the marginal-cost ceiling, and the prototype only shows that the cheap way to reach it does not work. **What the probes do not bound, stated as uncertainty and not as a ceiling.** Two limits are real. First, repeating a `black_box` move measures move bandwidth, not the smaller return slot, the calling convention, or the construction and destruction the redesign also deletes, so **0.171 ms is the sum of the probed operations, not a bound on the whole rebuild**. Second, eight repeats of a fill at one site write storage the first pass has already made cache-hot, so the zero-fill figure is a **warm** cost and **under-states** the cold first fill an arena would remove — the one component whose bias runs against the verdict. What is available on the residue is a containing bound and a threshold, not a measurement: SCALE-069's decomposition puts the general-intra residual parse subtree at 2.25 ms of the link and its coefficient symbol walk at 0.53, so everything the rebuild could touch is at most 1.72 ms, and the unpriced slice would have to be worth **0.83 ms — 4.9x the whole probed family and 48% of that remainder** — for the rebuild to reach its line. **Verdict.** Every component that could be priced is 5.8x under the wall-time line, the only buildable member is negative, and no measurement in this row puts the unpriced ABI-and-lifetime slice anywhere near the 48% it would need, so the rebuild is rejected **on the evidence gathered**. On the benchmark the probed family is 0.10% of the ten-worker wall against a 1.5% line. **Re-open condition, explicit**: an ABI-faithful probe or implementation — an out-pointer `decode_general_intra_plane_coeffs` with no owned return value, and a cold-buffer zero-fill control — that puts the unpriced slice above 0.83 ms of the frame-2 link. Nothing weaker re-opens it. **This closes the first-pyramid fill permanently** — SCALE-054 closed every reachable shape, SCALE-055 priced the row-granular handoff, SCALE-068 re-priced that verdict at the post-LTO ceiling, SCALE-069 closed the parse's symbol side, and this row closes its object side — and the fill held the ledger's last named successor. **Instrument note, recorded honestly.** One decode stalled during an early sweep that overlapped a concurrent background poller and was killed after 21 minutes at 0.56 s of CPU; a clean re-check of 200 runs of the frozen artifact and 200 of the instrumented binary at ten workers reproduced nothing, so it is unattributed, and the harness now times out and retries. **Verification.** Tree is docs-only against `6c34b34da`; the prototype and every switch are reverted and kept as a scratchpad patch; the rebuilt release binary hashes `73d436e05b179f64a919bfb254887922a264e18547fd11dcb24c1aa3ab73124f`, identical to the frozen artifact every confirmed-matrix number was measured on; bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. |
| SCALE-071 | Rejected; no source change | Price the one slice SCALE-070 could not: `decode_general_intra_plane_coeffs`' return ABI and lifetime — constructing, moving and destructing the 88-byte `LumaCoeffBlock` behind its `Result` and the same shapes down its callee chain — plus the cold first zero-fill its warm 8x probe missed, against the re-open line that row stated: **re-open iff the two together exceed 0.83 ms of frame 2's parse link**. | **NO-GO. The gate does not fire, and the fill family closes permanently at every level.** **Instrument.** SCALE-070's `SPLOT_PARSE_SPAN` recorder rebuilt on exact `origin/main` `13940a345`: the instrumented flag-off link reads **6.50-6.65 ms** against SCALE-070's 6.49-6.78 and the frames 2-7 chain 15.80-16.37 against its 15.89-16.93. Every arm is an env switch inside one binary and every comparison is arm-versus-arm in that binary, so the +1.142% (0/5 pairs, ten workers) the probe code costs against the frozen artifact cancels by construction. Forty interleaved reps per arm with the arm order rotated each rep, plus three-trace confirmations. **(a) The ABI and lifetime slice measures at or below zero.** A real out-pointer path was built, not simulated: `decode_general_intra_plane_coeffs_into` plus out-pointer twins of `decode_staged_transform_tool_nonzero_coeffs` and `decode_general_intra_luma_partition_coeffs` write every decoded field into a caller slot that already sits in its final home — the `ParsedResidualPlaneKind::Single` field, the pushed `PositionedLumaCoeffBlock` — and return `Result<(), Box<GeneralIntraResidualError>>`, one register in place of a 104-byte `Result`, so no per-block value is constructed, moved or destroyed. A route census puts **23,087 of 28,767 decodes (80.3%) on the new shape**; the residue is the inter path, whose whole frame-2 parse subtree SCALE-069 measured at 0.32 ms. Byte-exact at 1T and 10T. Frame 2 reads **+0.031 ms** and the chain **-0.018** against A/A nulls of -0.009 and +0.018; three traces read 6.503 against 6.596. **The saving is 0.000 ms and its sign is the wrong way.** The mechanism is in the disassembly and was not anticipated by SCALE-070: AAPCS64 returns a 104-byte composite **indirectly through `x8`**, so the shipped callee already writes into a caller-provided slot and the out-pointer removes only the caller's copy *out* of that slot — the `ldur q0/q1` + `str q0/q1` pair after the `bl`, about three 16-byte moves per block. That is the same component SCALE-070 priced at 0.005 ms, and the redesign cannot recover more of it than exists. **(b) The cold first fill is 0.059 ms, and SCALE-070's warm figure was not warm-biased.** Two controls. *In situ*: take up to eight **distinct** buffers out of the shipped pools, fill each once at the temperature the pool actually holds, and return them all with the pool unlocked while the fills run — where SCALE-070 repeated the fill on the buffer it had just filled, the warmest case that exists. The extra fills are counted exactly, **66,488 against 27,620 real ones over 30 frames, 2.407x**, and 2.49x at `--limit=3`, so the early frames carry the same ratio. Frame 2 reads **+0.1073 ms** against a -0.0354 null, putting the whole in-situ zero fill of the `level`, `quant_sign` and `quant` grids at **0.045 ms raw and 0.059 null-corrected** — landing on SCALE-070's 0.059 warm figure from an instrument built to disagree with it. *Counterfactual*: circulate 21 MB of quant storage and 2.7 MB per thread-local grid through a FIFO ring so every fill writes DRAM-resident memory. Frame 2 reads **+0.775 and +0.714 ms** over two sweeps and 7.372 against 6.650 over three traces — but that is a bound on a machine this decoder does not run, and the discriminator is in the same sweep: **the identical ring code at resident depth (256 quant, 64 local) reads +0.021 ms, equal to the shipped pool**, so the shipped fill is demonstrably 0.71 ms cheaper than cold and cannot be paying the cold penalty. Census for the record: the shared quant pool saturates its 2,560 cap at 8.24 MB, with 13,793 takes and 6,764 misses over 30 frames. **Verdict.** **(a) + (b) = 0.000 + 0.059 = 0.059 ms of a 6.65 ms link, 14x under the 0.83 ms line.** Charging instead the whole inapplicable DRAM-cold counterfactual gives 0.031 + 0.775 = **0.806 ms**, still under the line before subtracting the collateral cache pollution that arm includes. All four arms byte-exact at 1 and 10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc140be565069838bcf7141aba3c80cefaa14400284840b2e1475a3be945`); raws deleted. **This discharges SCALE-070's re-open condition on its own terms and it was the ledger's last one.** Do not re-open the per-block object model, the coefficient arena, the out-pointer return or the zero-fill under any pricing. **Verification.** Tree is docs-only against `13940a345`; every probe is reverted and kept as a scratchpad patch; the rebuilt release binary hashes `73d436e05b179f64a919bfb254887922a264e18547fd11dcb24c1aa3ab73124f`, identical to the frozen artifact every confirmed-matrix number was measured on; bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. |
| SCALE-073 | Rejected; fully reverted | **Owner:** `/root` + one sequential investigation/implementation agent<br><br>Price the fill link's CDF-independent prologue — the construction SCALE-041 measured as `TileParser::new` on the key frame's cold one-worker head and SCALE-069 priced only through the once-per-run key-frame conversion — and, if it clears the line, hoist it off the § 8.2 chain into the trough's idle cores. | **Dependencies:** SCALE-068; SCALE-069; SCALE-041; SCALE-055<br><br>**The chain shortened 20.9% and the benchmark did not move; this is the first intervention to test the fill family's premise rather than decompose it, and it falsifies it.** **Instrument.** An env-gated `SPLOT_PREP_SPAN` recorder — one `Instant` pair per named prologue span plus one per link, about 19 reads per coded frame, buffered so no print lands inside a span — reproduces SCALE-068 independently: the fill chain is the six parses of display order hints 17, 8, 4, 2, 1 and 3, back-to-back with 7-16 microsecond successor gaps, spanning **16.100 ms** (median of eight ten-worker traces) against its 16.690 and SCALE-069's 16.59-16.99, with hint 17's link 6.501 ms and its pass `row` 4.959 against SCALE-069's 6.83 and 5.83. **Step-1 decomposition** (ms, per link, medians of eight traces; `(a)` is the § 8.2 CDF work itself — `resolve_initial_frame_cdfs`, the 47 KiB `tile_copy` and the terminal `finish_frame_cdfs`; `(b)` is construction sized from the frame header alone; `(c)` is the rest of the pre-`row` prologue): hint 17 **6.501 / 0.026 / 1.459 / -0.013 / row 4.959**; hint 8 2.726 / 0.014 / 0.247 / 0.010 / 2.453; hint 4 1.995 / 0.039 / 0.680 / 0.005 / 1.230; hint 2 1.930 / 0.033 / 0.893 / 0.016 / 0.934; hint 1 1.387 / 0.027 / 0.970 / -0.001 / 0.379; hint 3 1.486 / 0.025 / 1.008 / -0.057 / 0.504. Chain totals 16.03 with (a) **0.163**, (b) **5.257**, `row` 10.46 and epilogue 0.15. **The CDF inheritance the chain exists for is 0.027 ms per link, 1.0% of the chain**, while (b) is **0.876 ms per link and 33% of it**, against a stated 0.4 ms kill line — step 1 passed at 2.2x. (b) splits into the frame's zero-filled reconstruction workspace **3.234 ms over the chain (62%)**, `TileParser::new` **1.611 ms (31%)** — an order below the 1.37-1.40 ms SCALE-041 measured on the cold key frame, because a fill link hits warm pools — and the § 7.9 motion field plus the CDEF/GDF/CCSO state 0.41 ms. **Step 2, built and measured.** The workspace is sized by the `DecodedFrameInfo` the driver already derives before it submits the entropy job, so a second admission job with **no conditions** allocates it while the chain is still parsing and the entropy job takes its completion cell as one more admission condition — pool tasks still never block. +109/-18 lines over five files, byte-exact at 1/2/3/4/8/10 workers (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`), 545-frame report identical at 1T and 10T and to the frozen base, all nine `frame_pipelining` tests green including the depth-proportional storage gate, bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0. **The mechanism worked exactly as designed**: over eight traces per arm the on-chain workspace term goes **3.234 to 0.000 ms**, pass `row` is unchanged (10.524 to 10.497), (a) is unchanged (0.163 to 0.154), and the **chain span falls 16.100 to 12.743 ms, -3.36 ms, -20.9%**, every candidate trace below every base trace. **The benchmark did not move.** Eleven alternating pairs at each lost width on an idle host: 10T **-0.085%** against an A/A null of -0.137% (6/11), 8T **+0.254%** against -0.235% (5/11); five pairs at each won width are inside their own nulls (1T -0.382% vs -0.114%, 2T +0.185% vs +0.110%, 3T +0.161% vs -0.496%, 4T -0.064% vs +0.054%). A nine-rep `--limit` sweep at ten workers reads flat everywhere: `--limit=1` -0.08 ms, `--limit=2` — the fill — **-0.29 ms against the chain's -3.36**, `--limit=30` +0.18. CPU rises 15.7 core-ms at 10T (+1.2%) for the extra job and its cross-core first touch. **Where the 3.36 ms goes, named.** Per-frame parse-end medians over eight traces: the chain's last link ends **-3.29 ms** earlier and the next two frames -3.52 and -3.58, then the lead decays to **+0.54 ms by the third frame past the chain** and stays at zero for the remaining 24. The parse stream cannot bank a lead, because `InflightRing::reserve` blocks the driver until the oldest in-flight frame's filter phase is harvested: admission is paced by frame *completion*, so a parse-side saving converts only until the ring re-saturates, measured here at three frames. **What this closes.** SCALE-069 opened the family on the premise that on this chain every parse millisecond is a wall millisecond. That premise is now measured false past the first links, and it was the shared premise of every chain-side row — SCALE-069's per-symbol side, SCALE-070's object side, SCALE-071's ABI side and this row's prologue side — so the family is closed on the conversion rate rather than on any one candidate's magnitude. Runtime fully reverted; the rebuilt release binary hashes `73d436e05b179f64a919bfb254887922a264e18547fd11dcb24c1aa3ab73124f`, identical to the frozen artifact. |
| SCALE-074 | Investigated; no source change | Answer the maintainer's question after PR #1183 was closed and safe-portable-only became policy: on splot's hottest portable kernel, would the `wide` or `pulp` crates change anything against nightly `std::simd`? | **No on aarch64 for the shipped shape — `wide` is 1.30-1.58x slower and cannot express the slide at all, `pulp`'s portable API is 2.5-6.1x slower — and the one thing `pulp` does add is exactly the `unsafe` exemption #1183 was closed for, relocated into a third-party crate.** **Method.** The § 7.13.3.18 full-span horizontal row, the shipped `SlideLanes` cascade, reimplemented as six variants over the three libraries in a scratch crate **outside the repository** on the shipped release profile (`lto = "fat"`, `codegen-units = 1`, `overflow-checks = false`) and nightly-2026-07-22: (a) the shipped `simd_swizzle!` slide as control, (a') the pre-SCALE-059 overlapping-load shape, (b) `wide` 1.6.0 and (b') a hand-tuned `wide`, (c1) `pulp` 0.22.3's portable `Arch::dispatch`/`WithSimd`, (c2) `pulp`'s `core_arch::aarch64::Neon` token. Every variant asserted bit-identical to a scalar reference at four widths x 17 window offsets before timing; 21 alternating reps with the variant order rotated per rep, paired medians, two independent sweeps. **Splot's Cargo files were never touched** — no dependency was added to any workspace manifest and this tree is docs-only. Host Apple M2 Max, up 4 d 4 h, one-minute load 3.8-4.2; the rotation, the pairing and the two-sweep agreement are what carry a result measured on a loaded host. **Micro-perf, full span `K = 8`, ratio to the shipped slide, sweep 1 / sweep 2 at w=8/16/32/64.** `wide` **1.570/1.563, 1.584/1.557, 1.376/1.374, 1.301/1.299, and 0 of 21 pairs faster at every width in both sweeps**; the hand-tuned `wide` is no better (1.568/1.560, 1.630/1.620, 1.456/1.431, 1.362/1.366, 0 of 21). `pulp` portable **6.13/6.09, 5.00/4.90, 3.27/3.28, 2.469/2.470, 0 of 21**, and hoisting `Arch::new()` out of the call changes nothing (6.35/6.34, 5.15/5.07, 3.35/3.34, 2.478/2.476) — **the cost is the trait's expressiveness, not dispatch overhead**. `pulp` NEON **0.952/0.920, 0.994/0.963, 0.942/0.953, 0.969/0.969 at 16-20, 15-18, 20-21 and 20-21 of 21 pairs** — 3-8% faster, the only variant that beats the control. The largest sweep-to-sweep disagreement on any cell is **3.4%**, on the `pulp` NEON arm whose whole effect is 3-8%, so that arm's sign is solid and its magnitude is at the edge of what two sweeps resolve; every other arm agrees to 2.2%. **Codegen, aarch64, whole function then the 8-lane inner loop per 8 outputs.** Shipped slide **176** instructions, `1 ldp + 7 ext.16b + 16 smull/smlal + 2 rshrn + 1 str`, and the 16-lane block `ldp + ldr + 14 ext + 32 smlal + 4 rshrn + stp`. Overlapping-load control **221**, `8 ldur + 16 smlal + 2 rshrn + 1 str` — the same MAC count with eight load-port operations instead of one, which is SCALE-058/059's pricing rule read straight off the machine code. `wide` **124**, `8 loads + 2 mov.16b + 16 smlal + 2 ushr + 1 uzp1 + 1 str`, plus **eight separate bounds branches per iteration and a 32-byte stack `memcpy` for the `i32x8` splat in the prologue**: `wide` exposes **no cross-vector shuffle at all**, so the slide cannot be written, and its shift and narrow do not fuse into `rshrn`. `pulp` portable **268** with **zero `smlal`** — 10 32-bit `mla.4s`/`mul.4s`, 8 `ushll`/`ushll2` widenings, 4 `uzp1` and **59 `stp` of stack traffic** — because the trait has no widening load, no i16 multiply that keeps 32 bits, no arithmetic or constant right shift on i32 lanes (the row must bias and go through `wrapping_dyn_shr_u32s`) and no i32-to-i16 narrowing. `pulp` NEON **78**, `1 ldp + 6 ext.16b + 16 smull/smlal + 2 rshrn + 1 str`; LLVM folds the eighth `ext` away because `ext #8` is the second register itself. **Runtime-index sliding windows, the shape that blocked sub-pel full generality.** Neither library expresses it portably — `wide` has no shuffle, and `pulp`'s portable trait offers only single-vector `rotate_left/right_*s` at 32- and 64-bit lane widths — nothing at 16 bits, which is this kernel's lane width — plus f32/f64 interleave, and no two-vector `ext` at any width. `pulp`'s **arch** API does, by #1183's own mechanism: fold the runtime `tap_start` into the load address so every `ext` index is a literal again. Against the shipped runtime-length arm this reads **`K=6` (`tap_start` 1) 0.870/0.876, 0.780/0.788, 0.752/0.754, 0.711/0.711; `K=6` (`tap_start` 2) 0.853/0.848, 0.784/0.772, 0.761/0.752, 0.714/0.710; `K=4` 0.962/0.979, 0.827/0.846, 0.839/0.829, 0.743/0.749**, 21 of 21 pairs at every width above 8, and at w=8 19-21 of 21 for `K=6` and 13-19 for `K=4` — **an independent reproduction of #1183's -32.88% share-weighted pruned-span figure from a different implementation**. #1183 already measured that kernel integrated and it is **null at every width in both instruments**, so the capability is real and its conversion is zero. **Dependency and `unsafe` posture.** `std::simd` 0 crates. `wide` 2 (`wide`, `bytemuck`), 36.4k lines, ~849 `unsafe` mentions. `pulp` 8 (`pulp`, `bytemuck`, `cfg-if`, `libm`, `num-complex`, `num-traits`, `paste`, `pulp-wasm-simd-flag`, plus `raw-cpuid` on x86), 73.7k lines, ~2,092 `unsafe` mentions. **Where `pulp`'s `unsafe` lives**: `src/core_arch/{aarch64,x86,wasm}/mod.rs`, where a `delegate!` macro wraps every intrinsic as `#[inline(always)] pub fn f(self, ..) { unsafe { arch::f(..) } }` on a zero-sized token whose only safe constructor runs `is_aarch64_feature_detected!`; no `#[target_feature]` is used, so the wrappers inline freely on a baseline-NEON target and #1183's +49% non-inlinable-helper problem never arises. The workspace `unsafe_code = "forbid"` does not see dependencies, so `cargo xtask ci` would stay green — **which is the point: `pulp` satisfies the letter of the policy #1183 was closed under while doing the same thing, at the price of eight crates and 73.7k lines of third-party code to reach about ten intrinsic wrappers.** **x86, the one scenario where dispatch could matter.** Splot already sets `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3` for `x86_64` in `.cargo/config.toml` (AVX2+FMA+BMI2, with a recorded ~15% whole-stream measurement), so the "std::simd falls back to SSE2" premise is already answered for any build run from the repo. Cross-compiled whole-function instruction counts for the identical source size the thing being avoided: at a true SSE2 baseline (`-sse3,-ssse3,-sse4.1,-sse4.2`, the `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` default) the shipped slide is **516** instructions — `pmulhw`/`pmullw` pairs and 108 `movdqa`, no `palignr`; at the `x86_64-apple-darwin` default (SSE4.1) **370**; at `x86-64-v3` **227**, with `vpalignr` plus 8 `vextracti128` for AVX2's per-128-bit-lane shuffle; at `x86-64-v4` **185**. **`wide` adds no dispatch whatsoever** — its backend is a compile-time `pick!` on `cfg(target_feature)`, exactly like `std::simd`, so it needs the same `-C target-cpu` and changes nothing about portability. **`pulp` is the only one of the three with runtime dispatch**, and its x86 `Arch` has exactly three arms: `V4` (AVX-512, behind the **non-default** `x86-v4` feature), `V3` (AVX2) and **`Scalar`** — there is no SSE level, so below AVX2 a pulp-dispatched kernel is *scalar*, worse than `std::simd`'s SSE2 rather than better. The honest residue is therefore **one** scenario: a single distributed binary that must both run on pre-AVX2 x86 and use AVX2 or AVX-512 where present. Splot deliberately traded that away for the v3 floor; the AVX-512 half is already reachable with `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v4` and no dependency, and the sub-AVX2 half regresses under `pulp`. A second and unrelated gap is real: `.cargo/config.toml` rustflags apply only to cargo invocations inside the repo, so a downstream consumer of `splot-recon` or a distro build from another directory gets the 516-instruction shape — that has a packaging fix, not a SIMD-library one. **No x86 timing was taken, this host is aarch64, and instruction count is not time**: on aarch64 `wide`'s whole function is 124 instructions against `std::simd`'s 176 and it still measured 1.30-1.58x slower. **One cross-check recorded honestly.** On this harness the slide is worth **+3-9% over overlapping loads at `K = 8`**, not SCALE-059's isolated -30.98%; both arms here are fully unrolled and pay the same `#[inline(never)]` call and bounds-check overhead, so this measures only the load-shape change. The sign agrees, and the smaller magnitude is the one consistent with SCALE-059's own integrated -0.085 core-ms per frame at one worker. **Verification.** The tree is docs-only against `ee2dc874d`; the scratch crate and its `wide`/`pulp` dependencies live outside the repository and were never added to any manifest, so no Cargo change needed reverting; the rebuilt release binary hashes `8946b3cb6f3631481ee8c52ad59451502a6bd8629b89d6d37023834ea225f35f`, which is `ee2dc874d`'s own artifact and **not** the `73d436e05b...` the confirmed matrix was measured on — `#1186`, `#1187`, `#1188` and `#1189` changed decode source after `fe2aa5213`, so that artifact no longer reproduces and the matrix needs re-measuring before any future retention call. Nothing here depends on it: the kernels were measured in a standalone crate that never links splot. Bare `cargo xtask ci` exit 0.<br><br>Closed as a measurement, and it closes the portable-SIMD-library question permanently. **Do not re-open `wide` or `pulp` for the decode kernels**: `wide` is slower everywhere and cannot express the slide, `pulp`'s portable API is slower everywhere and cannot express the widening MAC, the arithmetic shift or the narrowing store, and `pulp`'s arch API is #1183's kernel with the `unsafe` moved into a dependency and its integrated value already measured at null. Re-opening needs either a maintainer decision that a dependency-hosted `unsafe` is acceptable where an in-tree one is not, or an x86 host on which the single-binary pre-AVX2-plus-AVX-512 scenario is actually splot's. |
| SCALE-091 | Investigated; no source change | Price the memory-copy family against dav2d's own copy share, decide whether "one output frame, threads write it directly" closes the eight-worker gap, and name the successor. | **The copy excess is real and 7.7x, the wall prize is 1.2%, and the existing zero-copy path is *slower* than the copying one.** All numbers on exact `7d0c4cadb` at eight workers on the long-run arm (`--limit=1000`, `--frame-delay=auto`), rotated alternating pairs, host Apple M2 Max. **Ground truth.** splot 4.0143 s / 27.870 core-s / **6.943 cores** against dav2d 3.6253 s / 27.700 core-s / **7.641 cores**: wall **1.0951x** (1 of 7 pairs faster), **CPU 1.0061x — parity**, occupancy **0.9086x**. The eight-worker loss is occupancy, not work. **The control the ledger never ran: dav2d's own copy share.** `/usr/bin/sample` at 1 ms over a full run of each decoder, self-time of `_platform_memmove`/`_platform_memset`/`__bzero`/`memcpy` as a share of non-parked samples: **splot 8.94% (memmove 6.83), dav2d 1.16% (memmove 0.73) — 7.7x**. splot's families: publication `OwnedFramePlaneRect::publish_into` 1.30, filter source `StripePlane::copy_from` 0.97, precompute `write_rect` 0.91, `DeblockedWindow::extract` 0.63, `publish_filter_stripe_to` 0.55, seal `copy_rows_into` 0.46, `take_surface` 0.36, `extract_bands` 0.30, `StripePlane::copy_rows` 0.27, `overlay_mi_grid` 0.22. dav2d's whole list is stripe-sized: `lr_sbrow` 0.43, `cdef_slice` 0.20, `refmvs_load_tmvs` 0.20, `backup_prefilter_data` 0.11, `copy_db_slice` 0.05 — **it never materialises a whole frame twice; its pre-filter backup is a line buffer.** Since splot's *total* CPU is at parity while it spends 7.8 points more on copies, splot's non-copy CPU is about 7.8 points *below* dav2d's. **Per-worker occupancy.** splot 92.7 / 91.8 / 90.3 / 89.4 / 88.2 / 87.5 / 88.2 / 86.2 % busy, **mean 89.3%, monotone in steal order**; dav2d 97.0 / 94.9 / 96.7 / 94.3 / 94.8 / 94.3 / 95.8 / 97.0, **mean 95.6%, flat**. **Band-eligibility census** (env-gated, 302 admitted frames, 3,020 superblock rows, 41,072 units): **56.3% of superblock rows and 86.2% of individual superblocks are band-eligible, but only 25.2% of rows get bands**, because `supports_owned_bands` is all-or-nothing per frame. **0 frames are zero-eligible and every one of the 226 fallbacks is `current_frame`** — never `intrabc`, `partial_tile`, `terminal` or `band_count`. **That gate is not the lever, because the zero-copy path loses.** Forcing every frame onto the legacy copying path (`SPLOT_FORCE_LEGACY`) measured **-0.66% wall, 5 of 7 pairs faster, +0.53% CPU and +1.2% occupancy (7.021 against 6.938 cores)** — SCALE-035's net-zero reproduced and turned negative. The mechanism is granularity, now named exactly: `band_batches` issues **one batch per superblock row** while `superblock_row_batches` issues `ceil(15/4) = 4` on this stream's 15-unit rows, so banding costs **4x fewer schedulable tasks** and the copies are buying occupancy. **The deletion control over-credits and must not be quoted.** Ablating the two spine copies measured publish **-2.76%**, seal **-2.74%**, both **-5.76% wall (7/7)** — but the profile prices both at only 1.76% of busy, and a deletion leaves the downstream filters running on zeroed samples. **The clean measurement is idempotent amplification**, which keeps output byte-exact and scales only the copy term: `SPLOT_AMPLIFY_COPIES=6` (five extra passes) measured **+6.05% wall / +5.58% CPU, 0 of 7 pairs faster**, so **one pass of publish plus seal is 1.21% of wall and 1.12% of CPU** and the deletion control over-credits **4.8x**. **CPU converts to wall at about 1.08x here**, so a CPU reduction is *not* wasted on an under-occupied decoder — but the whole spine-copy prize is 1.2%, not 5.8%. **Two occupancy hypotheses refuted.** Task supply: sweeping precompute batch units **1 / 2 / 8 against the shipped 4 regressed +0.81 / +0.52 / +0.20% wall** with occupancy flat at 6.93-7.00, so granularity is already optimal and the stalls are dependency-bound, confirming SCALE-011-K10.31. Worker supply: pool widths **10 / 12 / 16 reach 8.146 / 9.010 / 9.643 busy cores and 1.0603 / 1.0513 / 1.0723x** against dav2d at eight, at **+12 / +23 / +35% CPU** — runnable work exists and eight workers cannot stay on it, but oversubscription never reaches dav2d and pays efficiency-core rates (SCALE-034). **Zero-fill is diffuse**: 1.89% of busy, largest single owner `take_surface` at 0.36%, everything else at or under 0.11%. **Verification.** All instrumentation was env-gated and is fully reverted; the tree is docs-only against `7d0c4cadb`.<br><br>**The scaling decomposition, which reframes the whole row.** Fresh curve on `--limit=400`, medians of 3: **splot wins one worker outright — 8.7100 s against dav2d's 10.0848 s (0.8637x) on 8.695 against 10.054 core-s** — and then loses the advantage to parallel CPU inflation. Parallel efficiency 100 / 88.2 / 84.6 / 83.4 / 77.8 / **70.1%** at 1/2/3/4/6/8 workers against dav2d's 100 / 98.0 / 97.3 / 97.2 / 95.2 / **90.3%**; splot's CPU grows **+28.5%** from one worker to eight where dav2d's grows **+6.5%**. **At its one-worker CPU efficiency splot would run the eight-worker benchmark in 8.695/7.192 = 1.209 s against dav2d's 1.396 — a 13% win.** The `N x 1T` control separates the two causes: 8 concurrent one-thread decodes cost **+14.8%** CPU each (dav2d **+1.7%**) and one 8T decode costs a further **+10.3%** (dav2d **-1.5%**), so the larger half is memory-system sensitivity that is present even with no shared parallelism. **Two mechanisms are refuted.** The split path is not the cause: forcing the fused walk at eight workers costs *more* CPU (12.13 against 11.02 core-s) and far more wall (2.48 against 1.48 s). Footprint is not the cause either: an 8T frame-delay sweep holds CPU flat at 11.05-11.23 core-s while peak resident set moves 302 to 890 MB across delays 2 to 12 (dav2d 365 MB), reconfirming SCALE-057 from the other direction. **Per-symbol attribution of the inflation** (self-time share diff, 1T against 8T, scaled into one-worker CPU units) puts **`_platform_memmove` first at +5.19 points, 3.46% of busy at one worker against 6.83% at eight — about 20% of the entire inflation and the largest single grower.** `OwnedFramePlaneRect::write_rect` (+0.68) and `CurrentFramePlane::new` (+0.59) appear only at eight workers; the temporal family is a wash (`TemporalBand` +3.99 and `fold_units` +1.46 against `project_temporal_motion` -3.26 and `commit_temporal_motion_blocks` -1.37, the same work restructured). **Read `semaphore_wait_trap` and `__psynch_mutexwait` as idle, not CPU**: they are blocking syscalls, and their callers are 14.0% main-thread `LockLatch::wait_and_reset` plus 9.8% rayon `Sleep::sleep`, with real mutex contention only 1.18%. **So the copy family is the top CPU-inflation term after all** — not through the 1.21% wall its two spine passes cost in isolation, but because the eight-worker path moves nearly twice the copy traffic of the one-worker path.<br><br>**The decisive localization, and the one that names the successor: splot's intra-frame parallelism is already better than dav2d's, and the entire deficit is cross-frame pipelining.** At `--framedelay 1` and eight threads, where frame-level overlap is switched off and only within-frame parallelism remains, **splot runs 2.92 s on 4.01 busy cores against dav2d's 3.48 s on 2.96** — 16% faster wall and a third more cores. Going from frame-delay 1 to 8, **dav2d gains +4.75 busy cores while splot gains only +3.03**. After SCALE-093's stack the eight-worker head-to-head is **1.0874x with a CPU ratio of 0.9926 — splot's total CPU is now *below* dav2d's — and an occupancy ratio of 0.9132 (7.040 against 7.709 cores)**, so the whole remaining gap is that one term: at dav2d's occupancy the same CPU would finish in 3.455 s against 3.481 s, a win. **Successor: re-open SCALE-029 under modern pricing.** Frame `N` cannot start a row until frame `N-1` has pushed it through commit, the § 7.17 deblock frontier, a filter stripe and publication; dav2d filters inside the superblock-row task and publishes with fewer hops. SCALE-029 measured the recoverable wait at 0.29-0.30 ms/frame as an interval union and rejected it against a 1 ms/frame line, but the steady frame at eight workers on this arm is about 3.9 ms, which makes that union roughly 7.5% of wall — several times today's retention bar. The reference-gate ceiling ablation is not available to size it directly: § 7.13.3.18 fail-closes with `inter_reference_rows_unpublished`, so the probe must instrument publication-to-consumption lag rather than remove the gate. **Two further avenues were then measured and closed, and one instrument trap is recorded for reuse.** Sub-stripe reference publication is *structurally* capped rather than mispriced: CDEF, CCSO and LR are whole-stripe operations, so only the GDF slice can publish early, which is why SCALE-029 measured 0.29 ms/frame — do not re-open it as a pricing question. `motion_publish_ms` reads 20.5 ms/frame against a 3.87 ms period, but it measures frame admission to last-unit-landed, which is pipeline latency of order depth x period, not a removable wait. **The trap: `pass1_parse` is reported by two nested timers** (`frame_walk.rs` around `parse_inter_frame_blocks`, `tile.rs` inside `parse_tile_units`), so its naive total double-counts; halved, the serial § 8.2 parse chain is 428 / 470 / 532 ms at 2/4/8 workers against walls of 3336 / 1843 / 1073, and it inflates a further 477 to 537 ms as frame-delay goes 2 to 8 at fixed width. **That 50%-of-wall figure is not a bound and the parse is not the critical path**: 302 frames of 1.78 ms span 537 ms inside a 1075 ms wall, so the chain is idle about half the time — it is paced *by* frame completion, exactly as SCALE-073 found when a parse lead was re-absorbed within three frames. Do not re-open the entropy family on a steady-state-Amdahl argument.<br><br>**Superseded successor, priced and closed.** The only copy family worth a restructuring is the triple materialisation (`write_rect` + `publish_into` + `take_surface` = 2.57% of busy, plus `copy_rows_into` 0.46). Removing it requires reconstruction to write canonical storage directly **while keeping legacy task granularity**, which is exactly what neither prior attempt did: SCALE-035 tried the 2-D `rect_surfaces` partition against a concurrently-borrowed whole workspace and could not express it, and SCALE-011-B4/K10.9 kept the band but took the one-batch-per-row granularity that this row now prices at 4x. The shape the evidence supports is **per superblock row: split the canonical row band into disjoint column rects for the 86.2% of superblocks that read no current-frame sample, keep the ordered commit for the rest, and join once per row.** `CurrentFrameWorkspace::rect_surfaces` already expresses the disjoint per-row `&mut` partition in safe Rust and the multi-tile path already uses it, so the missing pieces are the per-row barrier and the mixed eligible/ineligible commit — not a new storage representation. **Do not carry the -5.76% ablation figure forward, do not re-open the all-or-nothing band gate on its own, and do not retry batch-granularity or pool-width tuning.** |
| SCALE-094 | Investigated; no source change | Decompose the band-storage fallback by dependency class, which SCALE-091's census could not do, and rank the classes a maintainer-supplied safe-ownership plan proposes to implement. | **General intra is 92.4% of the prize and three of the plan's four proposed classes have literally zero material on this stream.** SCALE-091 reported that every one of its 226 fallbacks was `current_frame`, but `ReconDependency::CurrentFrame` (`prediction/inter/block.rs:235`) subsumes `GeneralIntra`, non-fence `Intrabc` **and** current-frame-reading `Inter`, and `supports_owned_bands` returns at the first blocking reason, so that census could not separate them. **Method.** An env-gated `SPLOT_BAND_CENSUS` walk of the whole parsed tile that counts every blocking leaf instead of returning at the first, plus a `band_eligibility_reason` split of `supports_owned_bands` so the census reads what the frame *would* be admitted as with `BAND_STORAGE_ADMITTED` bypassed. Exact `a631ab4f4`, `--limit=1000 --threads=8 --frame-delay=auto`, 955 admitted frames, 128,925 units (135/frame = 15 x 9), 8,595 superblock rows. **Frame plan:** bands **309 (32.4%)**, `current_frame` **646 (67.6%)**; `intrabc`, `partial_tile`, `terminal`, `band_count` and `filters_disabled` **never fire**. **Superblock dependency:** `ReferenceOnly` 113,799 (**88.3%**), `CurrentFrame` 15,126 (11.7%), `GlobalIntrabcFence` **0**. **Blocking leaves, the row's own question:** `general_intra` 143,892 (**92.4%**), `unbounded_resolve` 11,780 (**7.6%**), and `intrabc` **0**, `current_frame_inter` **0**, `unbounded_inter` **0**. **Superblock rows:** fully clean 5,144 (**59.8%**), **mixed 3,451 (40.2%)**, fully blocked **0**; over the 646 fallback frames alone, 40.6% clean, 59.4% mixed, and 82.7% of units still clean. **Cross-check against SCALE-091's independent instrument on its 302-frame window:** 88.3% against 86.2% of superblocks band-eligible and 59.8% against 56.3% of superblock rows — the two agree, so the decomposition rests on a validated instrument. **Consequences for the supplied plan.** Its PR 5 (current-frame inter), PR 6 (IntraBC) and PR 7 (partial tiles) have **no material at all** here and cannot be measured, let alone retained; its PR 3 (general-intra horizontal wavefront) is the entire prize; and **zero fully-blocked superblock rows** means a horizontal wavefront always has both eligible and ineligible superblocks in the same row — the mixed-row commit is the load-bearing mechanism, not an edge case. A class the plan does not name is worth more than three that it does: **106 frames (11.1%) are blocked *only* by `unbounded_resolve`**, so tightening that containment proof alone moves band coverage 32.4% to 43.5% with no wavefront work. **The blocker the census does not remove.** `BAND_STORAGE_ADMITTED = false` since SCALE-093, because banding measured **-0.75% wall over 11 of 11 pairs when switched off**; expanding eligibility therefore moves *more* frames onto a path that is currently net-negative, and the plan's own acceptance gate ("positive direction at 8 and 10 workers") would reject it. The two named costs — `band_batches` issuing 4x fewer schedulable tasks, and a non-contiguous band collection dropping the § 7.17 frontier off `filter_contiguous_edge` onto per-sample `apply_edge_samples` — must be removed first; that is SCALE-092, already GO at step 1 and priced at about +1.4%. **Verification.** All instrumentation was env-gated and is fully reverted; the tree is docs-only against `a631ab4f4`. |
| SCALE-092 | Rejected; fully reverted | Reconstruct directly into canonical row-band storage while keeping legacy task granularity, per SCALE-091's named successor shape. | **Built, byte-exact at five widths, and rejected on its own numbers — and the granularity mechanism the row rests on is refuted by the build itself.** **What was built.** `partition_rows` factors the row-splitting loop out of `partition_plane_rects` and takes the storage's own global origin, so one helper serves both `CurrentFrameWorkspace::rect_surfaces` (origin `(0, 0)`) and a new `OwnedFrameRect::rect_surfaces` (origin the band's own rectangle); `OwnedFrameRowBand::rect_surfaces` delegates to it. The banded precompute then fans its superblocks over one disjoint column rect each, reusing the existing `CurrentFrameSurface::Rect` path. **No cross-job scope was needed**, which is where this differs from the row's own design note: the band is already exclusive to its precompute batch, so the disjoint `&mut` partition lives entirely inside one job and never has to be held across independently-scheduled jobs. **Soundness.** A banded frame admits only `ReferenceOnly` superblocks; § 7.11 `derive_motion` "reads reference samples and writes none"; and `prepass_write_is_contained` already proves each superblock's writes stay inside its own rectangle — so the units of one band neither read nor write each other's samples, and splitting is the same work in a different order. A trailing unit with no superblock writes nothing and only settles its own § 7.22 bookkeeping, so it runs against the whole band before the split rather than claiming a sixteenth column of a fifteen-column row. Raw 30-frame output is byte-exact at **1/2/4/8/10 workers** (186,624,000 B, SHA-256 `48f0dc14...`). **Measurements**, exact `a631ab4f4`, `--limit=1000`, rotated alternating pairs, paired medians, host Apple M2 Max. A/A null at eight workers is **-0.025%** (5/7) with a ±0.35% spread. Bands on **with** the fan-out: **8T +2.271% (0/11)**. Bands on with the original serial band loop: **8T +1.882% (0/11)** and **10T +2.113% (0/7)**. **First conclusion: task supply is not the band cost.** The fan-out takes a superblock row from one task to fifteen — past the legacy path's four, which is the whole of SCALE-091's "4x fewer schedulable tasks" — and buys **nothing**: occupancy moves 7.000 to 7.008 of eight cores (+0.1%, inside noise) while CPU rises 26.25 to 26.37 core-s. The nested join is pure overhead, the same result K10.52, K10.53 and SCALE-089 measured for nested Rayon inside an already-parallel task, and consistent with SCALE-091's own batch-units sweep and K10.31: these stalls are dependency-gated, not supply-gated. **Second conclusion: the deficit has more than doubled since it was priced.** SCALE-093 measured bands on at +0.79% on `7d0c4cadb`; it is +1.88% on `a631ab4f4`, because `#1463` cut the per-frame memory traffic that banding existed to save — the prize shrank while the cost held. Attribution at eight workers, base to serial bands: CPU 26.11 to 26.25 core-s (**+0.54%**, band allocation plus the frontier's per-sample deblock) against occupancy 7.076 to 7.000 cores (**-1.07%**), so the loss is about half work and half occupancy and none of it is task supply. **Verification.** All runtime edits reverted; the tree is docs-only against `a631ab4f4`. |
| SCALE-095 | Investigated; no source change | Price the deblock-contiguity term SCALE-092 left as the band deficit's unexplained half, and decide the supplied plan's "chunk-aware filtering" option on a number. | **The contiguity penalty is +9.13% of wall at full coverage, and with it the band deficit closes to within 0.13 points.** SCALE-092 attributed the +1.88% band deficit as roughly half work, half occupancy but did not price the work half. **Method**: an env-gated ablation that hands the *shipped contiguous* path the *banded* path's addressing — `PlaneBand::segmented(samples.chunks_exact_mut(stride).collect(), ..)` instead of `PlaneBand::plane(..)`, so `plane_ctx.rows.is_contiguous()` fails and every edge falls from `filter_contiguous_edge` to the per-sample `apply_edge_samples`/`gather_line` walk. Nothing else changes and the arithmetic is identical, so 30-frame raw output is byte-exact (`48f0dc14...`). Measuring it on the shipped path rather than on banded frames raises coverage from 25-32% to **100%**, which is what makes the term resolvable. **Result**, exact `bb103dfec`, `--limit=1000`, eight workers, nine rotated pairs: **+9.129% wall, 0 of 9**, with CPU 27.28 to 29.46 core-s (**+8.0%**) against occupancy 7.049 to 6.995 cores (-0.8%) — overwhelmingly work, as an addressing change must be. **The model closes.** At SCALE-094's measured 32.4% band-eligible frames the penalty is `9.13 x 0.324 = +2.96%`, against SCALE-091's idempotently-amplified copy prize of **-1.21%**, predicting a band deficit of **+1.75%** against SCALE-092's measured **+1.88%**. The band path's entire loss is therefore accounted for: it pays about 2.5x more in lost deblock addressing than it saves in copies. **This decides the supplied plan's filtering section against its own preference.** Its "Preferred: chunk-aware filtering" — a `PlaneRowSource` trait yielding `row_chunks` per row — *institutionalises* the segmented addressing this row prices at 9.13%, so it makes the deficit worse, not better; and its "Conservative first implementation: one assembly copy" reintroduces exactly the copy banding exists to remove. **The only shape the data supports is bands that are views into one contiguous per-plane frame buffer**, so the § 7.17 frontier keeps `filter_contiguous_edge` *and* the publish/seal copies go away. That needs the frame-level buffer to outlive and be borrowed by each precompute job — the cross-job scope SCALE-092's note named, which its column split turned out not to need. **Verification.** Ablation env-gated and fully reverted; the tree is docs-only. |
| SCALE-096 | Investigated; no source change | Re-price the spine-copy prize on current main, which bounds every zero-copy design the supplied safe-ownership plan proposes. | **The whole prize is +1.03% of wall, and the cheapest known mechanism to capture it costs +0.95%.** SCALE-091 priced the two spine copies at 1.21% on `7d0c4cadb`; `#1463` then cut per-frame memory traffic, so the figure that bounds the plan had to be re-taken. **Method**, SCALE-091's idempotent amplification rather than a deletion control: `OwnedFrameRect::publish_into` and `CurrentFrameWorkspace::copy_rows_into` each run `N` times behind `SPLOT_AMPLIFY_COPIES`, which scales only the copy term and leaves output byte-exact (`48f0dc14...`) — a deletion would leave downstream filters reading zeroed samples and over-credits 4.8x, as SCALE-091 recorded. **Result**, exact `767c778a1`, `--limit=1000`, eight workers, nine rotated pairs: `N = 6` costs **+5.163% (0/9)**, so one pass of publish plus seal is **+1.033% of wall**, slightly under SCALE-091's 1.21% in the direction `#1463` predicts. **This closes the supplied plan arithmetically.** Its entire objective — delete the per-superblock rectangle, the workspace publication and the sealed whole-frame copy — is bounded above by **1.03% of wall on this stream**, before any mechanism is paid for. SCALE-092's step-1 probes measured the cheapest expressible mechanism (the ordered-commit barrier plus the scope admission widening) at **+0.95%**, leaving a net ceiling of **+0.08%**: zero. The three measurements are mutually consistent and now form a closed model — banding pays SCALE-095's `9.13 x 0.324 = 2.96%` contiguity penalty to save this 1.03%, predicting the **+1.88%** SCALE-092 measured. **Verification.** Amplification env-gated and fully reverted; the tree is docs-only. |
| SCALE-097 | Investigated; no source change | Benchmark the plan's "reconstruct all raw regions, then filter" level, the one shape a single contiguous canonical buffer can express, which the plan itself asked to be priced independently. | **+50.75% of wall (0 of 9). The sealed copy is not waste — it buys a 49x return, and that inverts the whole premise of the supplied plan.** A contiguous canonical buffer cannot hand the § 7.17 frontier a prefix while reconstruction still holds band borrows, because safe Rust cannot merge adjacent `&mut [T]` back into one slice. That leaves exactly three expressions, and all three are now closed. **(a)** Views held across independently-scheduled jobs: the frontier never obtains a contiguous prefix at all — not a measurement but a language invariant, and `unsafe` would not change the schedule that makes it necessary. **(b)** Reconstruct every band, then filter — the plan's Level 2, "entirely safe and worth benchmarking independently". **Now benchmarked**: an env-gated ablation returns `None` from `safe_deblock_mi_end` for every nonterminal commit, so the frontier runs only at terminal, which is byte-exact (`48f0dc14...`) because SCALE-011-D11 already made a zero frontier perform no work. On exact `4e56d8f0d`, `--limit=1000`, eight workers, nine rotated pairs: **+50.747%, 0 of 9**, 3.7294 s to 5.6159 s. **(c)** A moving `split_at_mut` point on a serial spine: SCALE-044's replay, validated to +2.4%, prices it at `k` 1.13 to 0.62-0.65 and a projected 0.31 s against 0.1895 s at ten workers. **The reframing.** SCALE-096 prices the two spine copies at 1.03% of wall; this row prices what they buy at 50.75%. The copy is what decouples the frontier from reconstruction, and it returns **49x** its cost. Every earlier row that called the triple materialisation waste — including this mission's own framing from SCALE-033 onward — was measuring the price without measuring the purchase. **Consequence for the supplied plan**: its central claim, that removing the materialisations is the next step, is inverted. The materialisation is load-bearing. Do not remove it, and do not re-open canonical segmented storage, region ownership, or contiguous-buffer band views on any pricing. **Verification.** Ablation env-gated and fully reverted; the tree is docs-only. |
| SCALE-098 | Investigated; successor built and rejected | Measure the reference-visibility cadence of both decoders in the same units, to price what splot's explicit ownership handoff costs against dav2d's aliased in-task publication. | **Splot's publication chain is 48% longer: 11.589 ms against 7.826 ms from a frame's first published row to its last.** This is the first apples-to-apples measurement of the term every earlier row inferred. **Method.** Both decoders emit `(frame_id, luma_rows, ms)` on every advance of the watermark that makes rows readable as a reference: splot from `FrameProgress::publish` with the origin at `begin` and an atomic frame id; dav2d from the `f->cur.progress[1]` store in `thread_task.c` with the origin at `dav2d_decode_frame_init`, on an **instrumented scratch copy of its tree** outside the user's checkout, verified output-identical to the shipped build at `--muxer md5` (`dd97a110fc0ad57c3e624ceddd703377`, 300 frames). Per-frame ids are what make this work at all: both decoders keep several frames in flight, so a first attempt that grouped events by monotonic row order split 300 frames into 473 and 751 and had to be discarded. `--limit=300`, eight workers, 303 against 306 frames, 17 publications per frame on both sides. **The absolute columns are not comparable and are not quoted** — splot's origin sits after parse, dav2d's at frame start — but the cadence is origin-free. **Result.** Span first to last publication: splot **11.589 ms**, dav2d **7.826 ms**. Median step: 0.333 against 0.275 ms. Steps over 1 ms sum to **7.99 ms per frame against 4.41**. **Structure.** Median step by index, splot `2.01 0.23 1.08 0.22 1.08 0.19 1.01 0.19 0.70 0.18` against dav2d `0.06 1.16 0.05 1.06 0.04 0.95 0.04 0.97 0.04 0.87`: both publish in pairs, two stripes per superblock row, and splot loses in two places. Inside a pair it takes **0.19 ms against 0.04**, eight times per frame, about **+1.2 ms**; and its first inter-pair gap is **2.01 ms against 1.16**, about **+0.85 ms**. **What this does and does not establish.** It establishes that the ownership handoff has a real, quantified latency cost, which SCALE-091's "fewer hops" hypothesis asserted without a number. It does **not** establish that safe Rust forces it: the per-stripe scheduling hop itself was separately measured at a median of **0.028 ms against 0.437 ms of stripe execution**, so the handoff is cheap and the loss is in *when work becomes eligible*, not in passing it. The candidate mechanism is the § 7.17 read lead splot must prove and dav2d does not, but that attribution is not yet measured. **Verification.** Splot instrumentation env-gated and fully reverted; the dav2d copy lives outside the repository and its Cargo/meson files were never touched. | **Successor built and rejected in the same session.** The intra-pair term was attributed exactly: a frontier link creates its two stripes 14 us apart, but the second *starts* 177 us later and publishes 179 us later, so the whole term is queue wait for a free worker (per-frame totals: queue 2.86 ms, exec 8.10 ms, published-gap 11.86 ms, which reproduces this row's 11.589 ms span). Running the last released stripe inline on the frontier-chain worker instead of queueing it -- byte-exact at 1/2/4/8/10 workers (`48f0dc14...`) -- measured **+3.177% at 8T, 0 of 11 pairs**, against an A/A null of -0.111%. The premise was wrong in a way worth recording: the 1032 us gap between links is not slack, it is the chain's own critical path, so occupying that worker for 467 us of filter work lengthens the cadence it was meant to shorten. This is SCALE-042's result and SCALE-092's nested fanout again, on a third mechanism. **Reconciliation with SCALE-031**, which measured the consumer-side recoverable wait at only 0.64-0.86 ms/frame and closed it: both are right. Splot's publication chain is 48% longer *and* consumers are mostly not waiting on it, so chain length is not the occupancy gap. The 8T idle must be attributed somewhere other than publication latency.
| SCALE-099 | Investigated; corrected in place | Measure the ready-work backlog directly and name the owner of the eight-worker idle the ledger has hunted since SCALE-043. | **The decoder is starved, not slow: at 96.3% of job starts there are fewer ready jobs than workers, and at 17.9% there is exactly one.** Every prior row inferred the occupancy gap from wall, CPU or latency; none measured the quantity that decides it. **Method.** A gauge on the ready-spawn path increments when a proven-ready job is handed to the pool and decrements when it starts, so the value read at each start is the number of ready-but-unstarted jobs that existed at that instant. Histogram over `--limit=300` at eight workers. **Result**, share of job starts by backlog depth: 1 = **17.89%**, at most 2 = **43.60%**, at most 4 = **73.41%**, at most 7 = **93.59%**, 8 or more = **3.66%**. **This reconciles the whole ledger.** Splot's CPU is *below* dav2d's at every width (SCALE-067, and 26.34 against 26.81 core-s on a fresh eight-worker run) while its occupancy is 7.09 against 7.73 cores: it does less work and cannot spread it, because the dependency graph does not expose enough independent jobs at once. Every failed candidate now has one explanation. SCALE-098's inline stripe **regressed +3.18%** because it removed a job from a queue that is already empty. SCALE-092's fan-out and K10.52/K10.53's nested Rayon bought nothing because nested tasks subdivide work that is already running rather than exposing new roots. SCALE-091's batch-unit sweep (1/2/8) and K10.31 read null for the same reason, and SCALE-090's depth sweeps read null because the gate is dependencies, not slots.
